# Quarry waves 3-5 code completion (2026-11-10)

User order 2026-11-10, in the Quarry lead session: "continue with the waves". Quarry W4 (E4.1-E4.7) and W5 (E5.1-E5.3) of `docs/plans/quarry-browser-build-plan-2026-10-14.md` section 3.4, plus code leftovers of W3, continuing the W4/W5 work of 2026-11-07/08 (`.plan-work/quarry-w1w2/W4-ORCH2.md`, `W5-ORCH2.md`).
Branch `quarry-w45` (cut from main `031e7965d` + `afcee0802`). Code only: everything new is dark (every switch defaults off); nothing here is tested or live-proven. Each batch is compile-checked only (Quarry-v2 and Model Plane `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`, BFF gateway `cargo check --all-targets`, Go `build` + `vet`, TypeScript `tsc6` against the 33-error baseline).
Unit specs come from a read-only inventory (135 units: W3 48, W4 69, W5 18). Open owner questions with a recommended option were taken at that option for dark code (owner delegation of 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-27); each unit records the decision it used. Still needing the owner: FENCE-2 (delete the committed worktree snapshot under Quarry-v2/.claude) and the tracked duplicate tree apps/Frontend Plane/Ingestion Plane.
Merge rules: never hand-merge the agent-action-admission golden (regenerate at the second merge); session-core and capability-core migration numbers may collide with branch mp-w345 and are renumbered at whichever merge comes second.

The mode also ends when the session closes or the rail is hidden.; The attachment answer is validated (browser-scraped, grant none, ephemeral, same session, numeric region/viewport/image fields, PNG or JPEG) and rebuilt; otherwise attachBrowserRegion throws browser_region_attach_response_invalid (502), following the E3.3-c pattern.; selectionText is read from the browser body's DOM selection at press time, because the frame is an image and holds no selectable text. It is normalised by normalizeSelectionText.; CSS: added rules to interactive-browser-pane.css, which is not in the unit's file list. The toggle reuses the existing browser-dock-pane__ask header pill class.; Mode ends after any attempt that is refused or reaches the gateway, whether it succeeds or fails. The person must select again to retry. |

Admission refuses fill_otp_ref with Unsupported when the driver does not advertise otp_fill. The lead still has to compile, and the E3.1-d runtime adapter can now call these through the trait object.

The source goes into AppState.otp_source and every agent-run ObservationRunner. The approval grant (GrantValidation) gains two broker-owned fields, `mail_connection_id` (an opaque integration-corev2 connection id) and `grant_origin` (a canonical https origin, scheme+host+non-default port, no trailing slash). `validate_sensitive_approval` requires both for a fill_otp approval and otherwise returns 403 `otp_binding_missing`. There is no flag and no wire change, and it is independent of ZDR because the maps hold run ids and counters only. Two behaviour notes for review: a TTL-expired run that is later resumed starts with no marks, which matches the CredentialDeliveredReads contract (a mark ends when the run is released or a navigation loads the page), whereas before this change the mark survived until process exit; and an OTP fill still holding its lane past the 5-second wait can re-mark after release, leaving one small entry until exit, which only over-denies a later resume with the same run id.

It validates runId, content (trimmed, 1-2000 chars) and the optional clientMessageId. It then calls control_ai_run with {action:"resume", text}. Resume is the control action that carries text on a running run without pausing it: execution-core queues the steer text, and resume_paused changes nothing on a run that is already running. A paused run is resumed by the same call. Success maps to {outcome:"queued"} and 404/409 map to {outcome:"run_ended"}. The owner's steer refusals (empty, too long, not allowed with stop, steer disabled, queue full) become typed gateway error envelopes with the upstream status and gateway-written messages. The ZDR control refusal (typed zdr_feature_not_supported, or the legacy "ZDR browser-run controls are disabled" text) becomes a 403 in the catalogue shape with feature browser_run_steer. Nothing is logged. Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q3-E3.5-3a**: Q3-E3.5-3a built (code only, not compiled or tested). The edge now stamps params.webhook for crawl and batch when QUARRY_EDGE__JOB_WEBHOOK is on, and batch accepts and checks a webhook field (400 reserved_field while off). Metric job_webhook_handoff_total{kind,has_webhook} counts created jobs while the flag is on. Open: batch_payload_hash does not cover webhook (BATCH_IDEMPOTENCY). Keep QUARRY_EDGE__JOB_WEBHOOK off until Q3-E3.5-3b (control vet and store) and 3c (delivery) are in.
- **Q3-E3.5-3c**: Q3-E3.5-3c (2026-11-10): per-job webhook delivery is built dark behind QUARRY_CONTROL__JOB_WEBHOOK (default off). A run event whose job's params.webhook wants its type gets a pending webhook_deliveries row with WebhookID 'job:<job id>' (org-checked against the run's owner). The dispatcher resolves such rows against the job through an org-scoped lookup, derives the whjob_ secret at send time, signs with SignWebhookBody, and reuses the pinned client and retryOrDLQ; a ZDR job gets no rows; a switch that is off or a missing key dead-letters the row as job_webhook_unconfigured. Migration 029 drops the webhook_id foreign key from 002, which would refuse job rows on Postgres, and keeps the subscription cascade with a trigger. Q3-E3.5-7a's planned 029 is now taken and moves to 030.
- **Q3-E3.2-e1w**: Q3-E3.2-e1w built (code only, dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__ORIGIN_CONSENT, default off; OD13 counsel review still gates enabling). Route: POST /v1/agent/runs/:run_id/origin-extension {extension_grant_id} answers {scope, host_count} with no host names. The accepted OriginExtension lives on RunEntry.origin_extension, process-local and never checkpointed, so ZDR and persistence are unchanged and the gate still enforces turn scope for ZDR. Revalidation: before every person-lane step, WS action, delegate step, tab open and attach start, the edge re-reads both grants and re-runs the org-cap and deny-beats-approval check. A grant that is revoked, expired, changed or newly denied, or a flag turned off, restores the run's own egress on the session and on a live attach tab, then drops the extension. A failed restore refuses the action and keeps the extension for the next retry. The person lane is rebuilt at its next action through RunEntry.origin_extension_lane_stale. Tabs opened later and a separately opened attach agent tab inherit the widened boundary. The delegate's navigation and landing checks are not widened, and a borrowed attach tab is not widened. New counter quarry_origin_extension_revoked_total{reason}. Docs: docs/openapi.yaml. Nothing was compiled or tested, and no new tests were written. W5-081-a overlap (Model Plane calling this route) is untouched.
- **Q3-E3.3-h2**: Q3-E3.3-h2 (quarry-w45, not yet compiled): BrowserDriver gains a default point_hit that answers unsupported, plus BrowserPointHit {backend_node_ids: Vec<i64>, frame_id}, ungated in quarry-browser lib.rs. ChromiumoxideDriver overrides point_hit through hit_test_point, and PointHit is now an alias of BrowserPointHit. The hit-test route calls the driver's point_hit, so with QUARRY_EDGE__HIT_TEST on it answers 200 and off it stays 403. The agent_hit_test ZDR row keeps its treatment; only its basis text changed, so the JSON golden, its sha256 pin and docs/ZDR_MATRIX.md must be regenerated at the test phase with QUARRY_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test -p quarry-core zdr_matrix (the test writes all three together). Still open and outside this unit: D-E33h-1, since backend node ids already reach clients inside the observation's fingerprint_id (native_target_fingerprint in observation.rs), so 'backend ids never leave the process' does not hold for observations today. The ActiveSnapshot accessor (ask 2) is not in the tree and observation.rs is not in this unit's file set; the route still parses fingerprint_id (D-E33h-2). Also open: D-E33h-4 (frame marker) and W23 ask 3 (openapi and SDK route entries).
- **Q3-E3.3-l**: Q3-E3.3-l built, nothing compiled yet. BrowserObservation gains top-level `warnings` (closed codes ax_frame_timeout, ax_frame_unavailable, ax_budget_exhausted, ax_frame_limit, ax_truncated, plus ax_partial for an unknown driver code; fixed messages, no frame id, URL or page text, so ZDR-safe; capped at 50) and `settle_ms` (mirrors telemetry.settle_ms). BrowserNativeProjection now carries `warnings: Vec<ProjectionWarning>`, filled from the per-frame DeadlineWarning list (E3.3-k). That list is non-empty only when QUARRY_BROWSER_AX_PER_FRAME is on. No new switch, and the wire is unchanged when there are no warnings. openapi.yaml documents both fields and the ObservationWarning schema.
- **Q3-E3.2-e3-spa**: Q3-E3.2-e3-spa: Settings > Browser lists the viewer's own per-origin browser consent decisions (browser.list_origin_consents) and forgets one (browser.revoke_origin_consent), behind the existing VITE_BROWSER_POLICY_SETTINGS flag. A failed or refused list is an empty list, never fabricated rows. Forget is two-click and reloads the list after each attempt. Three consent codes were added to the ERROR_COPY table (browser_consent_unavailable, browser_consent_disabled, browser_consent_not_found). The registry and admission golden are unchanged by this unit, so it owes no regeneration; the Q3-E3.2-e3-reg regeneration is still the lead's.
- **Q3-E3.3-g2**: Q3-E3.3-g2: the gateway now has a browser_run.steer arm. It sends the person's text to model-gateway POST /v1/browser/runs/:id/control as {action:"resume", text}, via control_ai_run. A resume queues the steer text in execution-core (behind QUARRY_BROWSER_AGENT_STEER) and leaves an already-running run running. Outcomes: 2xx is queued, 404/409 is run_ended, steer_* and browser_steer_disabled pass through as typed envelope errors, and the ZDR refusal is a 403 zdr_feature_not_supported with feature browser_run_steer. No idempotency key is forwarded, because the upstream control route has none; clientMessageId is only echoed back. The arm is human-only (it rejects any stated actor other than human) and never logs the text. The model-access.ts exemption for browser_run.steer still says no_owner_executor and may now be stale, but it was not touched.
- **Q3-E3.3-g3**: Q3-E3.3-g3 (steer composer mount) is built in the quarry-w45 tree and not compiled. ChatLiveRunPanel mounts SteerInput under the live frame of a browser-agent run, behind VITE_CHAT_BROWSER_STEER (default off). Steers go through browser_run.steer; the gateway arm (Q3-E3.3-g2) is present in the tree but uncommitted. An owner unavailable answer hides the composer, and ZDR never mounts it. Queue and send-as-new stay hidden until ChatPage passes onSteerHeldMessages and onResend. No browser_run.steer_taken event exists, so markDelivered is not driven, and settle() marks unconfirmed steers as not taken at run end; a backend event is the fix.
- **Q3-E3.3-d2b**: Q3-E3.3-d2b (code only, not compiled or tested): the composer side of the browser region chip is built behind VITE_BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH (default off). A composer keeps up to three regions the browser pane attached, shows each as a chip (picture thumbnail, "Region of <host>"), removes them without a network call, and clears them on a browser-session change or a zero-retention turn. A send that carries a region is refused in use-chat-controller before any gateway or model call, so nothing reaches a model and the draft is kept. Forwarding waits on two things: a gateway send-time re-check of a carried region (the attach response has no id and the gateway stores nothing) and Model Plane classification of browser-region attachments (MP-ASKS W3-E3.3-d). Until then the switch must stay off.
- **Q3-E3.5-7a**: E3.5-7a is built in quarry-w45. Persistence: migration 030 (029 is W3-E3.5-3c's job webhook deliveries), store.Webhook.NotifyOnChange as json.RawMessage, the pg webhooks_blocklists.go and the in-memory UpdateByOrg. API: notify_on_change on create and PATCH (absent keeps the filter, null removes it), returned on the view and through the edge. Open decision for the owner: the duplicate rule (same URL and events) ignores the filter, and in v1 every subscription names change_detected, so one URL can hold at most one subscription per org whatever its filter. W23-W3-E3.5-7-status.md is stale: items 1 and 2 are now built, and its memory-store claim was wrong. Unit 7b (the batch path) remains open. No regeneration is needed: no generated golden or SDK in the tracked tree carries the subscription shape.
- **Q3-E3.5-7b**: E3.5-7b (Quarry, branch quarry-w45): change_detected events on the run-event batch path now fan out through fanoutChangeWebhooks with the subscription's notifyOnChange filter. The notice comes from payload status, change_type and paragraph_delta via eventChangeNotice and classifyChangeNotice. Other event types still use fanoutWebhooks. No wire change, no switch (the filter stays inert until a subscription sets notify_on_change), and the dedupe key is shared with the signed receiver. Open items: the batch copy has no paragraph_delta, so the min-delta threshold passes on that path; runtime page-level change_detected has no status and matches no typed filter; CHANGE_TRACKING.md and CONTRACTS.md still describe the batch path as unfiltered.

ListByOrgTag(org, "") would list the untagged rows, so the handler branches on the value (D-E356-5).; PATCH with no tag key, or with tag null, is a 400 with reason tag_required. The spec is silent; this stops a bare {} from reading as a clear.; PATCH answers 200 with the updated resource (schedule view or source row), where enable and disable answer 204. The caller sees the stored form of the tag.; scheduleWire (the orchestrator desired-state projection) also carries tag with omitempty, in addition to scheduleView. The orchestrator's ScheduleSpec does not read it, so reconcile behaviour does not change.; No feature switch, because the unit spec says 'switch: none'. The W3-E3.5-6 status doc asked for a default-off gate on the API unit, so the lead should confirm which applies. MountSchedules and MountSources are mounted unconditionally in cmd/control/main.go, so the PATCH routes and tag reads are live on control at deploy (internal only, since the edge does not route them).; Tag is accepted on any schedule kind, not only change_monitor, because the spec does not restrict it. The migration header describes monitors.; A monitor created from a source (monitor:true) does not inherit the source's tag. This keeps the earlier decision (W3-E3.5-6 status, item 6) and the spec's silence; D-E356-7 stays open. |
| Q3-E3.5-6b | E3.5-6 open item 'Rust edge drops source tags and ignores ?tag=' | built | Monitor tags now pass through the Rust edge for sources and schedules. POST /v1/sources and POST /v1/schedules forward a normalised optional tag, GET /v1/sources and GET /v1/schedules forward ?tag= to control, and new PATCH /v1/sources/{id} and PATCH /v1/schedules/{id} set or clear (empty string) the tag through control. Source and ScheduleSummary gained an optional tag that is skipped when absent, and quarry-runtime's SourceRegistration carries an optional tag. The change is additive, unswitched and leaves org binding, auth and ZDR handling unchanged. Nothing was compiled or run (code-only rule); rustfmt parses every edited Rust file. | - Deviations: The spec's SourceRegistration type did not exist: quarry-runtime's SourceRegistrar took four positional &str arguments. I introduced SourceRegistration<'a> (org_id, name, url, kind, tag: Option<&str>) and changed the trait to take it, updating pipeline.rs (passes tag: None because PageRunner has no tag), edge source_registrar.rs, the NoRegistrar test impl in scrape_actions.rs, and tests/url_gate_resources.rs.; Source.created_at and updated_at were DateTime<Utc> only, but control writes them as Unix milliseconds (store.Source int64), so a non-empty GET /v1/sources could not decode before this change (read from the code, not run). They now accept either form; serialization stays RFC 3339 as the openapi documents.; The list tag filter is ListFilter.tag plus ListQuery.tag, set only by list_sources and list_schedules; other list routes never forward it, which I read as the spec's scope of 'list routes'.; PATCH handlers parse the path id as SourceKind or ScheduleKind (src_ or sch_ plus ULID) before building the control URL. The pre-existing DELETE source, pause, unpause, trigger, backfill and DELETE schedule handlers still splice the raw path segment; I did not change them.; Validation mirrors control's store.NormalizeTag (trim, lowercase, 1 to 32 bytes of a-z 0-9 . _ -, letter or digit first) but is ASCII-only, so the edge refuses a non-ASCII tag that Unicode lowercasing could fold. Refusals are 400 BadRequest with details {field: tag, reason: invalid_tag}; a PATCH without a tag key gets reason tag_required before control is called.; No zdr_refusal call on the PATCH routes: the spec said ZDR unchanged and a tag edit only relabels an existing row, like pause and delete. The zdr_matrix schedules row names create, trigger, backfill and unpause as refused and does not name PATCH; the owner should confirm.; The edge forwards schedule JSON as raw values (list, create, pause, patch), so the schedule tag survives there without decoding. ScheduleSummary.tag serves the typed contract and the quarry-core test only.; openapi.yaml also gained the PATCH operations, a TagPatchRequest schema and tag on both create bodies, beyond the Source, ScheduleSummary and list-parameter additions the spec named. The file already had another writer's uncommitted agent-downloads changes; my hunks are additive only. |
| Q3-E3.7.10-edge | E3.7.10-CP open items + MP-ASKS 'Quarry (Ingestion), E3.7.10' (quarry-edge refuses service principals on job routes and /v1/crawl, /v1/batch) | built | The edge now admits service principals on the durable job routes by scope. In auth.rs a new fn job_route_scope(method, path) maps POST /v1/crawl and /v1/batch to crawl:write. It maps GET on /v1/{crawl,batch,jobs}/:id and their /errors, /results and /events to jobs:read; the /ongoing reads are matched as that :id segment. It maps DELETE /v1/{crawl,batch}/:id and POST .../pause/resume to jobs:control. Empty segments, deeper paths, other methods and other families return None, so they stay 403. require_service_route_scope calls it in its final arm. In job_lifecycle_routes.rs, authorize_control now refuses a service principal only when it lacks jobs:control; reads stay org-scoped through load_summary with claims.org_id. I updated module docs, and the comment on the zdr_gap_rows test that said service principals never reach the ZDR refusal. The ZDR refusal (refuse_durable_job_under_zdr) and the robots-override denial for service principals are untouched. Nothing was compiled or tested; rustfmt --check showed no diffs in the new code. | - Deviations: routes.rs needed no edit: the middleware already wraps all /v1/crawl, /v1/batch and /v1/jobs routes; only auth.rs plus the lifecycle handler needed changes.; GET /v1/crawl/jobs (the org job list, which axum routes to get_crawl) and GET /v1/jobs/ongoing are both treated as ':id' reads and require jobs:read; neither is reachable without it.; job_results_routes.rs got a doc-comment update only; its handlers are gated by the route scope and need no code change. |
| Q3-E3.4-7a | E3.4-7/E3.4-8 (W23-depth W23-b-E347-1 high: 'no Ingestion owner route for browser_download.send_to_knowledge'); ingest_client.rs DownloadHandoff has no caller | built | Added two edge routes in a new downloads.rs. GET /v1/agent/runs/:run_id/downloads lists the run's admitted downloads. POST /v1/agent/runs/:run_id/downloads/:artifact_id/knowledge hands one to Data Plane through DownloadHandoff::from_env(state.ingest).hand_off. The list is read from the caller's own completed DownloadRef/FrameDownloadRef receipts (last proof-bundle artifact id), re-read through state.artifacts.get_with_key under the claim's org, and checked with is_download_artifact_key plus downloads::admit_type on the bytes. Each entry carries artifact_id, mime_type, byte_size, sha256, admitted_type (pdf/txt/md/csv) and handoff_state (not_handed_off/handed_off), with no URL or filename. The POST checks in this order: QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOADS (403 downloads_disabled), the handoff flag (typed Disabled), ZDR token floor or ZDR run (typed ZdrRefused, 403, before any receipt or byte read), then owner/404, then the type check and parse, then hand_off, whose typed rejections pass through. Dedupe is by org, artifact id and sha256; a repeat returns the first document_id with created=false. The org and user come from the verified claims. Two ZDR matrix rows were added (list: Inert; POST: Refused FORBIDDEN) and the openapi.yaml paths and schemas were added by hand. | - Deviations: The spec lists docx and jsonl as admitted types. The real DownloadDocumentKind and downloads::DownloadType only have Pdf, PlainText, Markdown and Csv, and admit_type refuses ZIP/Office bytes, so DOCX is unreachable by design. admitted_type is therefore pdf/txt/md/csv and I did not extend the enum, since Data Plane document types are tied to it. The hand-off parses PDF through content_route::parse_document and decodes the other three as strict UTF-8.; The spec says to add `download_handoff: Option<Arc<DownloadHandoff>>` to AppState only if needed. It was not needed, so state.rs is unchanged and the handoff is built per request from state.ingest.; Both routes read QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOADS through the existing downloads_enabled() in step.rs, now pub(super) (a one-word visibility change). The handoff flag is read with ingest_client::download_handoff_enabled_from_env, the same reader DownloadHandoff::from_env uses.; A repeat call returns the first document_id with created=false. The spec said it returns the first answer; I kept created honest rather than replaying true.; idempotency_key is accepted and checked (at most 128 chars, no control characters, 400 otherwise). It does not change the dedupe key, which is org, artifact id and sha256 as specified.; The dedupe memory is process-local, bounded to 4096 entries and holds only document ids. handoff_state in the list reflects it, so it resets on edge restart. Data Plane's own fingerprint dedupe (sha256:<digest>) covers the gap.; source_url comes from the receipt's observation URL after receipt_for_read masking, reduced to scheme, host and path (userinfo, query and fragment dropped). The filename passed to the hand-off is a synthetic download.<ext>; no real filename is used.; A ZDR caller or run lists an empty array on GET (no receipt or artifact read). On POST a ZDR caller or run gets 403 ZdrRefused. |
| Q3-E3.4-7b | E3.4-7 (registry row browser_download.send_to_knowledge has no gateway arm; MP-ASKS W3-E3.3-g) | built | The gateway now has an arm for browser_download.send_to_knowledge and a list route for a session's downloads, both behind a new AppState flag browser_downloads (BROWSER_DOWNLOADS exactly 'true', default off). Nothing was compiled or tested, per the rules. The logic sits in a new module, domains/browser_downloads.rs. Its send_to_knowledge reads Quarry's owner projection of the session first, which answers 404 for a session the caller does not own. A projection that does not say zdr:false gets a 403 zdr_feature_not_supported naming feature browser_download, before anything goes upstream. Otherwise it POSTs the 7a route with the user's Quarry token and an idempotency_key. It maps Quarry's typed reasons to gateway envelopes without relaying upstream bodies: zdr_refused to 403, disabled to 501, unsupported_type to 415, no_text to 422, too_large to 413, org_mismatch and a missing run to 404. It returns {documentId, indexStatus:'pending', created}. dispatch_browser_download_send_to_knowledge in dispatchers.rs does the validation and refuses any actor other than human. GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/downloads is registered in browser.rs. It answers 404 while the flag is off and for non-owned sessions, an empty list under ZDR, and otherwise a list re-keyed to camelCase from fields checked against closed sets. The new field is added to every AppState literal. | - Deviations: Most of the code is in a new LF file, domains/browser_downloads.rs, not in browser.rs. browser.rs is CRLF and 12k lines. It only gets the route line, registered next to the artifacts route in the interactive group, and the test-state field. The dispatcher still lives in dispatchers.rs as the spec says.; The spec's 'sessionId or runId' is read as the same Quarry run: sessionId is preferred, runId is accepted as an alias, and the two must match if both are given. Unknown input keys are refused. The registry schema is strict with sessionId and artifactId only.; The 7a edge answer carries no index status, so the action output always reports indexStatus 'pending', including on a deduped repeat, and adds created: boolean. The registry output schema is not strict, so the extra key is fine.; List entries use the keys the existing drawer model already reads: artifactId, mimeType, sizeBytes, plus sha256, admittedType and handoffState. There is no filename, so the drawer falls back to the artifact id. Entries whose fields fall outside the closed sets are dropped.; A Quarry 404 on the list route (a run with no receipts) becomes an empty list, not a 404, because the owner check already passed.; The ZDR refusal on the action path is a 403 in the zdr_feature_not_supported catalogue shape with feature 'browser_download'. The SPA's refusalOf reads that as ZDR, and a 415 or 422 as unsupported_type.; config.rs got only the field, its parser browser_downloads_enabled and the build_state line. |
| Q3-E3.4-7c | E3.4-7 open items: 'no projection reports downloads; nothing mounts the drawer' | built | Mounted the downloads drawer in InteractiveBrowserPane behind VITE_BROWSER_DOWNLOADS_DRAWER (default off). With the flag on and the session not ZDR, the pane reads the owner's list through a new listBrowserDownloads (an empty list on 404 browser_downloads_unavailable or 403) when the session opens, when the controller's busy flag falls and after each agent-loop step, keeps the newest answer in a signal, and renders the drawer only when the list is non-empty. The drawer shows type, size and state only (no file name), disables files already handed off, and maps the owner's typed refusal codes to nb/en copy. Nothing was compiled or tested, per the CODE ONLY rule; the TypeScript was checked by reading only. | - Deviations: No tab-refresh timer exists in the pane. There is no fixed-interval poll.; Added an optional handedOff field to BrowserSessionDownload from the list's handoffState (not in the spec text). A handed-off file shows 'Sent to the knowledge base' and its send button is disabled.; Refusal copy is keyed on the owner's typed error codes (browser_download_unsupported_type, _no_text, _too_large, _handoff_disabled, _not_found), with the status fallback kept and 401 now mapped to signed_out. New nb/en copy covers no_text, too_large and disabled. Previously a 422 no_text reply showed the unsupported-type copy.; The drawer no longer shows the file name: the name span and its title attribute were removed, and the now-unused .browser-downloads__name CSS rule was removed. The spec says no file name text; the old drawer showed it.; listBrowserDownloads(sessionId, signal?) sends no org header, because the gateway list route reads identity from the token like the other browser-client session routes. Any failure other than unavailable or 403 rejects, and the pane treats a rejection as an empty list.; The send actor comes from the session store (user and activeOrg), the same shape as ChatLiveRunPanel's steer actor. The send input is {sessionId, artifactId}, which the registry schema requires; runId is not used.; readSessionDownloads is kept because its tests use it, and it now delegates to the new readDownloadListing. VITE_BROWSER_DOWNLOADS_DRAWER is typed at the end of src/vite-env.d.ts, which the spec's flag rule asks for. |
| Q3-E3.4-5b | E3.4-5 open item 'no view-only SSE route (proxy_sse_stream has no extra-header parameter)' | built | Built the view-only SSE twin of the owner's live socket: GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{session_id}/view/frames/stream in browser.rs, registered next to the WS view route. It answers 404 unless BROWSER_LIVE_VIEW_ONLY is on, passes the same owner projection check as the WS route, refuses a ZDR run with 403 before anything goes upstream, and relays quarry-edge's frame stream with x-quarry-live-scope: view_only set by the route. proxy_sse_stream gained an optional extra-header list (None for every existing caller); the relay body must stay in proxy_sse_stream_with_browser (pinned by tests/sse_relay_no_allowlist.rs), so the list is threaded there and 11 existing call sites got a trailing None. The sibling SSE routes default a missing zdr to false.; The new 403 code browser_live_view_zdr_unsupported needs nb/en copy. browser-errors.test.ts scans browser.rs for error("code", ...) literals and requires copy for each code, so an entry was added to src/shared/i18n/errors.ts. That file is outside the spec's list.; The /step read path is left unchanged, as the spec directs. The edge /step is a POST (agent_routes/step.rs) and does not call owner_live_scope. Not in this spec: the edge GET /frame (live.rs live_frame) does honour the scope, but the gateway GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/frame does not send it. |
| Q3-REG-imports-artifacts | E3.6-6a follow-ups 1-2 (W23-W3-E3.6-6a-status.md: manifest row office-extract + registry grant); E3.8.8 open item 'token-request manifest row + Auth Core regist | built | Added the two manifest rows (office-extract on ingestion with imports:read, artifacts-read on data-plane with data:read) to the Quarry token-request manifest and the byte-identical auth-core fixture. | Q3-E3.6-6a-wire | E3.6-6a/6b (W23-STATUS lane c: 'nothing calls install_office_extract_client') | built | The gap still existed: nothing called install_office_extract_client, so an Office body stayed at the typed 415 even with the flags on. quarry-edge now calls a new wire_office_extract_client in main.rs right after the build_model_client closure. It installs the client only when QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_VIA_IMPORTS is on, QUARRY_EDGE__IMPORTS_CORE_URL is non-blank and the cross-plane token provider exists; it then mints from the office-extract row, enables the client and applies the cap from QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_MAX_INPUT_BYTES. Any other state installs nothing and logs a warn with a closed reason, and the boot line is enabled=false or enabled=true only. content_route.rs gained OFFICE_MAX_INPUT_BYTES_ENV and office_max_input_bytes_from_env(), and ManifestImportsBearer::from_manifest was renamed to for_office_extract (see deviations). Syntax was checked with rustfmt --check only; nothing was compiled or tested, per the code-only rule. | Stale names outside this unit's files: the pinned manifest note for office-extract (crates/quarry-runtime/contracts/cross-plane-token-requests.json, line 133, 'Built by ManifestImportsBearer::from_manifest') and docs/CROSS_PLANE_INTEGRATION.md line 77 still use the old constructor name. Changing the manifest note needs its .sha256 pin and the byte-identical auth-core fixture regenerated, so the lead should update the note, pin, fixture and doc line in one pass.; The office-extract row has feature_flags [] although Q3-REG-imports-artifacts asked for QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_VIA_IMPORTS. The contract does not require the field for a granted row, and the row is pinned, so it was left unchanged. Deviations: Bearer is built with ManifestImportsBearer::for_office_extract(provider) instead of ServiceTokenRequest::from_manifest('office-extract') plus ManifestImportsBearer::new in main.rs. The same scan refuses any non-literal from_manifest( call, so the existing ManifestImportsBearer::from_manifest declaration (added by Q3-REG-imports-artifacts) was renamed to for_office_extract. Its behaviour is unchanged and the literal ServiceTokenRequest::from_manifest("office-extract") stays in content_route.rs.; QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_MAX_INPUT_BYTES is read at boot whether or not the switch is on, and an unparsable or zero value aborts boot with a message that names the variable and not the value. This follows the plan's rule that new readers abort boot on an unparsable value. Blank counts as unset (compose passes empty strings), and a value above 25 MiB is clamped to 25 MiB as the spec says.; The spec's ZDR line says the pipeline route already refuses. In pipeline.rs the Office branch (takes_imports_route) has no ZDR condition, and PDF/DOCX parsing is not ZDR-gated there either, so no gate was added. On the ZDR path the only guard is the client's persisted==false check. The lead should confirm the owner's ZDR reading for ephemeral first-party extraction.; The two flag parsers disagree on the value yes. content_route::office_via_imports_enabled, which this wiring uses, accepts 1, true, yes and on. pipeline.rs OfficeViaImports::parse accepts only on, true and 1, and warns on anything else. With yes the client is installed but the pipeline never calls it, so nothing is sent. This was not changed; one shared parser would remove the mismatch. |
| Q3-E3.6-6a-odf | E3.6-6a open item 'ODF/EPUB are 415 in imports-core' | built | Added .odt, .ods, .odp and .epub text extraction to imports-core _parse_local in apps/Ingestion Plane/imports-core/app/parsers.py; the gap was real, with no odt/ods/odp/epub branch before. The readers stream content.xml through a chunked stdlib XMLPullParser, refuse any DOCTYPE or ENTITY declaration before parsing, reuse _zip_budget, check the first 'mimetype' member against the extension's type, and cap rendered text at the zip expansion limit. ODS cuts its grid on a row boundary with the same truncated flag as _parse_xlsx; EPUB reads META-INF/container.xml, the OPF spine and XHTML items through _parse_html. Nothing is persisted, there is no network or model call, and no switch was added. chat_extract.py and openapi.yaml need no edit (no extension set is kept there, and the route is not documented in openapi.yaml). Checked with ast.parse, a grep-based name check and line endings (LF kept); nothing compiled or tested. | - Deviations: defusedxml is not a declared dependency (requirements.txt has beautifulsoup4 and lxml only), so the parse uses the stdlib XMLPullParser fed in 64 KB chunks, with a DOCTYPE/ENTITY refusal on the raw bytes for ODF and an ENTITY refusal on each XHTML item for EPUB.; No separate text:list-item branch: list items are walked as containers, and their text comes from their text:p/text:h children, which avoids counting it twice.; Deleted text (text:tracked-changes) and comments (office:annotation) are skipped, which the spec did not state.; ODT tables give one line per cell paragraph; rows are not joined with tabs (ODS grids are tab-joined). The spec did not require row joins for ODT.; ODP adds speaker notes under [Notater] and labels hidden slides (skjult), mirroring _parse_pptx; the spec said only text per draw:page.; The mimetype check is stricter than the spec's wording: the first member must be 'mimetype' and must name the type matching the extension (odt, ods or odp); a mismatch is refused as malformed (422).; EPUB XHTML items above _ZIP_MAX_TREE_PART_BYTES (2 MB, the existing tree limit) are refused with the existing 'too large to read safely' error (413), as large pptx slides are.; Chat-mode odt, odp and epub refuse text above 200 000 characters with OfficeArchiveTooLargeError, the same ceiling pptx uses; durable imports are bounded only by the 30 MB rendered-text expansion cap (_ZIP_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES).; ODS formulas are not listed in a [Formler] block; cells show their stored displayed text, which ODS always carries.; chat_extract.py was not edited: the spec's 'register in the supported set' only applies if one exists, and it does not. space_upload gets these types only when SPACE_UPLOAD_ALLOWED_TYPES includes them; its default is unchanged. |
| Q3-E3.7.3gw | E3.7.3 open item 'gateway does not forward wait/resume' | built | GET /api/ingestions/jobs/{id} now reads an optional wait (whole seconds, 1 to 30) and resume (1 to 512 characters of A-Za-z0-9_.-) from the query, refuses a malformed value with 400 invalid_wait or invalid_resume, and forwards only those two keys to quarry-edge. A request with wait uses a per-request timeout of wait + 5 s through a new sibling helper quarry_call_with_timeout in shared.rs; plain reads still call quarry_call unchanged, and the edge's 408 processing_continues envelope passes through forward() unchanged. Nothing was compiled or run (code-only order); rustfmt --check shows no diff in the new code, only four pre-existing hunks in jobs.rs (one in get_artifact_text, three in existing tests). | knowledge/quarry.rs crawl_status (the /v1/crawl/{id} read, around line 2773) is a second crawl status read that still drops wait and resume; it is outside this unit's file list and needs its own unit if the crawl page should long-poll.; No SPA caller sends wait or resume yet, and there is no keep-waiting loop in the UI; the wrapper only carries the args.; The edge's contract for the wait and the 408 envelope (openapi.yaml, CONTRACTS.md, SDK) is E3.7.11 and was not touched here. Deviations: The 408 is passed through unchanged instead of being mapped to a 200 {pending: true, resume} body. The spec's alternative applies because the ingestions client types (JobDetail in src/shared/api/ingestions-client.ts) have no pending shape. The typed envelope keeps error.details.resume_token, and the SPA's extractError keeps details and retry_after_seconds as ApiError hints.; The resume cap is 512, not the spec's 256 (jobs.rs MAX_RESUME_TOKEN_LEN, the is_resume_token doc, and the SPA regex and RangeError message). The edge's token is base64url of a small JSON marker. Measured: a 250k-page crawl with three-digit control sequence numbers gives exactly 256 characters, and 259 in phase cancelling, so 256 would refuse legitimate resumes of the largest jobs. To revert, change MAX_RESUME_TOKEN_LEN and the SPA regex and message back to 256.; quarry_call has no timeout parameter, so a sibling quarry_call_with_timeout was added in shared.rs, which the spec did not name. It wraps proxy_bearer_json_with_timeout, which already exists in upstream.rs. Plain reads still call quarry_call.; getIngestionJob takes options as a third parameter after signal, so the one existing caller (IngestionsRunsView.tsx line 299) is unchanged. It is async so out-of-range values reject instead of throwing synchronously.; A malformed wait or resume is a 400 (invalid_wait, invalid_resume, via the existing bad_request helper); the spec set no status. wait=0 is refused at the gateway, although the edge treats wait=0 as a plain read.; The 408's HTTP Retry-After header is not carried, because proxy_bearer_json and forward() carry only status and body. The body's details.retry_after_seconds is passed on, and the SPA reads it. |
| Q3-E3.7.7b-wire | E3.7.7b open item 'search_routes.rs does not pass the prefetched pages yet' (answer.rs prepare_with_pages unused) | built | The include_answer branch of POST /v1/search now reads the pages the scrape block already hydrated instead of fetching them again. The scrape block used to run after the answer synthesis, so it now runs first, once, on a cache miss with include_answer and a scrape block. The same run feeds the answer through Answerer::answer_with_pages (which calls prepare_with_pages) and then becomes the response's `scrape` report, so no page is fetched twice. A truncated page is marked incomplete, so it never enters the local corpus. Cache hits, ZDR requests and requests without a scrape block behave as before: ZDR still skips the answer, and no scrape block means an empty map. Under ZDR the pages stay in memory for the request only. Nothing was compiled or run; rustfmt --check found no syntax errors in search_routes.rs, and its only diff in answer.rs is a pre-existing formatting nit. | - Deviations: The old call site used answer_gated, which wraps prepare_gated. I call Answerer::answer_with_pages (which calls prepare_with_pages) so synthesis still runs. This is a runtime-only API from the same family; it was already in answer.rs.; AnswerResult gained two fields, `pages_fetched` and `pages_reused`, with `#[serde(default, skip_serializing)]`. The spec says to use the prepare result's counts, but answer_with_pages returned only AnswerResult. They are not on the wire, so /v1/answer and openapi.yaml are unchanged. Both existing struct literals in answer.rs are updated; no other AnswerResult literal exists in the tree.; Hydration now calls fetcher.fetch_extraction instead of fetch_source, so a reused page keeps the structured key figures a fetch of the answer's own would have produced. For SimpleHttpMarkdownFetcher this is the same fetch (fetch_source just drops the structured part), so scrape output and the `scrape` report are unchanged. The default trait impl delegates to fetch_source, so test fakes still work.; Beyond 'succeeded' pages, hydration refusals (policy, preflight Blocked, InvalidUrl) are passed as FetchFailure::PolicyDenied so the answer cannot fetch a URL the security preflight blocked. Failed fetches (HttpStatus, TooLarge, Decode, Extract, Timeout, Transport, Empty, TooShort) are passed as their failure so the same failing page is not fetched twice. DeadlineExceeded is left out, so the answer fetches that page itself as before.; The answer is not written to the search cache when any reused page was truncated, because the cache key does not carry the scrape caps and a short per-result cap would otherwise poison later requests without a scrape block.; No new answer-page metering was added. The answer's own pages_fetched and pages_reused go on the ANSWER_SYNTH event metadata and into a `search.answer.pages` tracing event (counts only). The search.scrape metering still counts only the pages the scrape block fetched, so a reused page is billed once.; Early hydration runs over diversify_results(results.clone()), the same set and order as the later report. diversify_results only reorders by host, and apply_score_policy only clears scores, so the report positions are identical. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q3-E3.5-6a**: D-E356-4: a single tag string per monitor (matches migration 027)
- **Q3-E3.7.10-edge**: B2-style default: grant exactly the scopes the registry already holds; ZDR refusal kept
- **Q3-REG-imports-artifacts**: Imports bearer scope: imports:read with a one-route exception in imports-core (least privilege) instead of granting imports:write

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q3-E3.5-6a**: E3.5-6 control HTTP routes (Q3-E3.5-6a, branch quarry-w45, code only, not compiled). Tag accepted on POST /v1/schedules and POST /v1/sources; PATCH /v1/schedules/{id} and PATCH /v1/sources/{id} added; ?tag= honoured on GET /v1/schedules and GET /v1/sources; invalid tags answer 400 with details field=tag, reason=invalid_tag; org only from the edge-stamped ?org_id; no switch and no migration (027 already on disk). Still open after this unit: D-E356-1 and D-E356-2 (the Rust edge drops tag on create and ?tag=, and Source has no tag field), the public openapi rows for tag and PATCH (deferred to the edge unit), D-E356-7 (a monitor made from a source does not inherit its tag), and D-E356-8 (a source's updated_at moves on tag PATCH, a schedule's does not). A repeat source registration keeps the stored tag (D-E356-9; use PATCH to change it).
- **Q3-E3.5-6b**: Q3-E3.5-6b built on branch quarry-w45 (code only, not compiled or run). The Rust edge carries monitor tags for sources and schedules: create forwards a normalised tag, GET with ?tag= filters sources and schedules, and PATCH sets or clears the tag through control with no ZDR refusal. Source timestamps now accept control's Unix-millis form, which fixes a decode failure of non-empty source lists. Open items outside this unit: the gateway/BFF must proxy PATCH and ?tag= before the SPA can use them; docs/CONTRACTS.md and docs/ERROR_REASONS.md do not list invalid_tag or tag_required yet; a monitor created from a tagged source starts untagged (D-E356-7); the pipeline registrar passes no tag; the owner should decide whether tag PATCH is refused under QUARRY_EDGE__ZDR_REFUSE_DURABLE_RESOURCES. Pre-existing: DELETE and pause-style schedule handlers splice the raw path id, unlike the new PATCH routes.
- **Q3-E3.7.10-edge**: E3.7.10 edge: require_service_route_scope now admits service principals on durable job routes via job_route_scope(method, path): POST /v1/crawl/batch -> crawl:write; GET /v1/{crawl,batch,jobs}/:id[/errors/results/events] and /ongoing -> jobs:read; DELETE /v1/{crawl,batch}/:id and POST .../pause/resume -> jobs:control. authorize_control (job_lifecycle_routes.rs) refuses a service principal only when it lacks jobs:control. Org scoping is the token's org through control. ZDR refusal on crawl/batch handoff is kept, and the robots override is still never granted to a service. execution-core's quarry token is ZDR-class, so its crawls are still refused at refuse_durable_job_under_zdr until the owner picks a non-ZDR token class. No switch. Tests for the table (a service with each scope, wrong method, deeper path, foreign org) belong to the test phase.
- **Q3-E3.4-7a**: Q3-E3.4-7a built, uncompiled. Quarry edge now has GET /v1/agent/runs/:run_id/downloads and POST /v1/agent/runs/:run_id/downloads/:artifact_id/knowledge, the first caller of ingest_client::DownloadHandoff. This closes W23-depth W23-b-E347-1 (no Ingestion owner route for browser_download.send_to_knowledge) at the Quarry edge. Both routes are dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOADS, and the POST also needs QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOAD_HANDOFF; ZDR is refused before any read. Admitted types are pdf, txt, md and csv only (docx and jsonl in the spec do not exist in the real allow-list). Dedupe is process-local by org, artifact id and sha256, with created=false on a repeat. The lead needs to regenerate the ZDR matrix golden, its sha256 pin and ZDR_MATRIX.md. Model Plane follow-up (W5-081-a overlap): a browser_download.send_to_knowledge capability can now call this route.
- **Q3-E3.4-7b**: Q3-E3.4-7b is built, dark, and not compiled or tested. The gateway has the browser_download.send_to_knowledge arm (dispatch_browser_download_send_to_knowledge in dispatchers.rs, arm in handlers.rs) and GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/downloads. The shared logic is in the new domains/browser_downloads.rs, behind AppState.browser_downloads (BROWSER_DOWNLOADS exactly 'true'). Ownership comes from Quarry's owner projection of the session, never from the client id alone. ZDR (or a projection that does not say zdr:false) gives an empty list on GET and a 403 zdr_feature_not_supported naming browser_download on the action, before any upstream call. Quarry's DownloadHandoffRejection reasons are re-keyed to gateway-owned codes and messages, and the upstream body is never relayed. The action answers {documentId, indexStatus:'pending', created}. The agent-action-admission exemption stays valid: the arm is human-only, and no golden changed. Open: 7c still has to mount the drawer and call GET .../downloads. The BFF flag, QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOADS and QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOAD_HANDOFF must all be on for the feature to work end to end.
- **Q3-E3.4-7c**: Q3-E3.4-7c built on quarry-w45 (frontend only). The downloads drawer is mounted in InteractiveBrowserPane and fed by listBrowserDownloads (GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/downloads, Q3-E3.4-7b), read only with VITE_BROWSER_DOWNLOADS_DRAWER on and the session not ZDR. Enabling it needs three switches: the SPA build flag, BROWSER_DOWNLOADS=true on the gateway, and QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOADS on Quarry (plus QUARRY_EDGE__DOWNLOAD_HANDOFF for the hand-off). No proto, golden, SDK or openapi change was needed. Nothing was compiled or run; suggested checks are a typecheck with tsconfig.app.json, the browser feature vitest files (downloads-model.test.ts and InteractiveBrowserPane.test.tsx), and lint.
- **Q3-E3.4-5b**: Q3-E3.4-5b built (code only, not compiled or tested): gateway GET /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/view/frames/stream, the SSE twin of the view-only WS. It is 404 unless BROWSER_LIVE_VIEW_ONLY is on, owner-only via the owner projection, 403 for ZDR (fail-closed), and sends x-quarry-live-scope: view_only upstream through a new extra-header parameter on proxy_sse_stream and proxy_sse_stream_with_browser. A client-sent scope header is stripped globally at ingress. This resolves the depth row in W23-W3-E3.4-5-status.md line 47 for the frame stream. Residual risk: the edge ignores the scope when QUARRY_EDGE__LIVE_VIEW_ONLY is off, and the frame stream's first live_view event is then unfiltered and may carry an interactive provider reference. Enable both flags together, and close this before any cross-user share is built.
- **Q3-REG-imports-artifacts**: Q3-REG-imports-artifacts built (code only, nothing compiled or tested). The Quarry token-request manifest gains two granted rows. office-extract (ingestion, imports:read, used_by content_route.rs) is the bearer for the imports-core ephemeral Office extraction. artifacts-read (data-plane, data:read, used_by data_plane_http.rs) is the no_egress scrape read of the stored page capture. The auth-core fixture is a byte-identical copy. Both .sha256 pins are refreshed, the new digest is 77d38acdebdb1708f316e723a11df03bd3421a4274ee4027a21699e1af499281 in zdr-contract-sync.test.sh, and EXPECTED_REQUESTS lists 12 rows. The Control registry gives quarry-edge imports:read on the ZDR ingestion audience; data-plane already had data:read. Decision recorded: imports:read plus a one-route exception in imports-core auth_middleware.py (POST exactly /api/v1/import/extract) instead of granting imports:write. The call sites are ManifestImportsBearer::from_manifest and DataPlaneArtifactReader::with_manifest_token_request. The edge-startup wiring that installs the imports client and passes the artifact reader to the no_egress driver is still open, and both rows stay inert until QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_VIA_IMPORTS or QUARRY_EDGE__NO_EGRESS is turned on. The new rows list feature_flags as [] (the two switches are env-read, not edge config fields), so the switches are named in the notes only. A new imports-core test for the one-route exception is not written (code-only rule).
- **Q3-E3.6-6a-wire**: Q3-E3.6-6a-wire (quarry-w45, code only): quarry-edge installs the imports-core Office extract client at boot through wire_office_extract_client in main.rs. It is behind QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_VIA_IMPORTS (default off), a non-blank QUARRY_EDGE__IMPORTS_CORE_URL and the cross-plane token provider. The bearer is ManifestImportsBearer::for_office_extract, which reads the office-extract row, and that row's call site is content_route.rs as its used_by requires. The body cap comes from QUARRY_EDGE__OFFICE_MAX_INPUT_BYTES (default 1000000, clamped to 25 MiB; unparsable or zero aborts boot). Every unbuilt state keeps the typed 415 imports_client_unavailable and sends nothing. No ZDR gate was added, because imports-core persists nothing and the client refuses any answer that says it did.
- **Q3-E3.6-6a-odf**: Q3-E3.6-6a (ODF/EPUB in imports-core) is built in apps/Ingestion Plane/imports-core/app/parsers.py: .odt, .ods, .odp and .epub are read with the stdlib only, using a chunked XML pull parser, a DTD/ENTITY refusal, the existing zip budget, a mimetype check and a 30 MB rendered-text expansion cap. Chat turns cut ODS grids with the truncated flag and refuse odt, odp and epub text above 200 000 characters. No switch was added. Nothing was compiled or tested in this batch: the lead should run the imports-core test suite and a sample-file check for each format.
- **Q3-E3.7.3gw**: Q3-E3.7.3gw: GET /api/ingestions/jobs/{id} forwards wait (1 to 30 whole seconds) and resume (1 to 512 characters of A-Za-z0-9_.-) to quarry-edge with a per-request timeout of wait + 5 s, and refuses malformed values with 400 invalid_wait or invalid_resume. The edge's 408 processing_continues envelope passes through unchanged because the SPA ingestion client types have no pending shape. Two decisions to confirm: the resume cap is 512, not the spec's 256, because real tokens for large crawls reach about 260 characters; and the SPA getIngestionJob gained an options argument after signal. Not compiled or tested in this unit; the integration step must compile the gateway crate and run its ingestions tests.
- **Q3-E3.7.7b-wire**: E3.7.7b wire done in code (not compiled). search_routes.rs now runs the scrape block once, before include_answer synthesis, when include_answer is set and a scrape block is present. The new helpers are run_scrape_block, ScrapeRun::prefetched_pages and hydrate_extractions. The answer reads the hydrated pages through answer_with_pages, and the later `scrape` report reuses the same run. AnswerResult carries non-serialized pages_fetched and pages_reused, which go on ANSWER_SYNTH metadata, so each fetch is metered once. Answers built from truncated pages are not written to the search cache. ZDR is unchanged (the answer is skipped), and a restricted web policy still skips the answer cache. The lead still has to compile and run the quarry-edge and quarry-runtime tests.

Each piece is cut at a newline when it can, else at whitespace. Whitespace-only pieces are not sent. The pieces go to pii_model_for_org in batches of at most 25, with at most 8 calls per page. Two new typed failure causes, model_tier_unavailable (no org, or any DocumentLaneError or count mismatch) and model_tier_too_large (past the 8-call cap), fail the page with the existing pii_redaction_failed error and field "markdown". Nothing is written before the final text exists. ZDR runs, runs without redaction, and runs with no client or the flag off behave as before. | - Deviations: Only the markdown goes through the model tier. body_text is the raw HTML source for HTML pages, and chunking HTML through a text PII model could corrupt tags. When the tier is asked, the html source artifact is not kept, as already happens for documents. The inline html and raw_html formats are not returned either: they are listed in omitted, with the warning format_unavailable_with_pii_model. Links and metadata stay deterministic-only. This keeps deterministic-only text from being kept or returned when the model tier was asked for.; A page whose text needs more than 8 Model Plane calls fails closed with model_tier_too_large. The cap is the constant PII_MODEL_MAX_CALLS_PER_PAGE and is shared by all fields of the page.; A ZDR run with the flag on does not call the model and does not fail. It keeps the deterministic-only text, per the spec wording 'the run is not ZDR'. The client also refuses ZDR itself.; A redacting, non-ZDR run with the tier on but no verified org fails closed (model_tier_unavailable) rather than skipping the tier.; No openapi, compose, flag-registry or proto change. QUARRY_EDGE__PII_MODEL already exists and is read by DocumentLaneSettings in mp_client.rs. mp_client.rs needed no change. No struct, trait or signature changed, so no call sites needed updating. |

Compile fix, Model Plane Rust workspace (apps/Model Plane/rust) and apps/Model Plane/proto: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Frontend Plane verevonv3 (SPA + BFF gateway) and other TS/JS: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Non-Rust files: Go (quarry-orchestrator), JSON, YAML, shell, SQL migration: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Quarry-v2 Rust workspace: green. 0 fixes.

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q3-E3.8.7w**: Per-org setting deferred: deployment mode via QUARRY_EDGE__REPUTATION_MODE plus a tighten-only org cap only if the catalogue key exists
- **Q3-E3.8.8w**: Data Plane contract taken as proposed (POST /v1/artifacts/lookup, URL in body); catalogue key deferred
- **Q3-E3.9.6p**: Prices not ratified: producer only, price table untouched

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q3-E3.7.2w**: Q3-E3.7.2w (edge half of depth row D-E372-1): POST /v1/batch accepts max_parallel or maxParallel behind QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_PARALLEL (default off), clamps it to QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_MAX_PARALLEL (default 4, max 16, boot-validated), stamps params.max_parallel for the orchestrator, and reports limits.max_parallel. The organization term is not applied because OrgLimits has no per-batch fan-out figure. Still open: the W3-E3.7.1 payload hash does not cover max_parallel, and the verevonv3 gateway batch route drops the field. Nothing compiled yet; the lead compiles after the batch.
- **Q3-E3.7.6-B-edge**: Q3-E3.7.6-B-edge, the edge half of redirect seen-marking. run_page reports final_url only when the fetch was redirected. RequestQueue::mark_seen (default Ok(0)) records ids as seen: in memory in a bounded set that enqueue refuses, and in Postgres as status 'seen' rows with an empty URL (migration 0021). POST /v1/internal/queues/{queue_name}/seen is the target of the orchestrator's FrontierMarkSeen; it stores ids only, is documented in openapi-internal.yaml, has an inert internal_queues ZDR row, and runs only once the orchestrator drives it under QUARRY_ORCHESTRATOR__CANONICAL_FRONTIER. The allowlist pin is 130 because the scan counts 129 at HEAD against the stale 121.
- **Q3-E3.7.6-B-go**: E3.7.6 B-go (orchestrator, dark): after a crawl page's ack, a page the edge fetched through a redirect marks its requested and final request ids seen in the crawl queue via FrontierMarkSeen. The body carries sha256 request ids only, never a URL. It runs only with QUARRY_ORCHESTRATOR__CANONICAL_FRONTIER on and under GetVersion crawl-canonical-redirect-seen-v1 (new), and a failure is logged and ignored. Durable crawls are refused under ZDR (routes.rs refuse_durable_job_under_zdr, crawl), so the call never runs for a ZDR run. Code only: nothing compiled or run, gofmt clean. Test phase: add a redirect-path replay fixture and a workflow test covering FinalURL set, flag on and off, and a failed seen call. The orchestrator flag is referenced only in Go code and its test; it is not in apps/Control Plane/flag-registry or any compose file in this tree (this predates the unit and was not changed).
- **Q3-E3.6-7-ocr**: Q3-E3.6-7 is built but not compiled. A PDF scrape that yields no text goes to the OCR lane (ModelPlaneClient::ocr_pages_for_org) when QUARRY_EDGE__OCR is on, QUARRY_EDGE__MODEL_PLANE_URL is set, the run has a verified org and it is not under ZDR. Pages 1..=min(pages, 20) are sent, including blank pages, because the sandboxed parser exposes only page counts. The OCR text becomes the document markdown before the normal redaction stage. A redacting run (redact_pii or an org default) therefore still sends the unredacted scan to the Model Plane and redacts only afterwards; this is what the spec says, and it differs from title_enricher, which redacting runs skip. This is a decision for the owner. Warnings: ocr_applied (page count), document_ocr_partial, document_ocr_unavailable (closed label). Not done: per-page metering, because PageRunner has no usage hook, and OCR of mixed text-and-scan PDFs, because per-page scan identities are not exposed. Safe-mode (actions) runs clear document_lanes.
- **Q3-E3.6-7-pii**: Q3-E3.6-7-pii built (code only, not compiled). In quarry-runtime pipeline.rs, the PII model tier (QUARRY_EDGE__PII_MODEL, default off, read from the shared document-lane client) now runs on the deterministically redacted markdown. It runs only when redaction is active and the run is not ZDR. It sends chunks of at most 5120 chars, 25 per call and at most 8 calls per page, to ModelPlaneClient::pii_model_for_org, and replaces the markdown with the returned text, re-scanned deterministically. Any lane error, missing org, count mismatch or over-cap text fails the page with the existing pii_redaction_failed error and cause model_tier_unavailable or model_tier_too_large. When the tier is asked, the page's html source artifact is not kept and the inline html/raw_html formats are omitted (warning format_unavailable_with_pii_model), because HTML cannot be chunked as text. Links and metadata remain deterministic-only. The tier is 'asked' only with a client present and the flag on, so the flag-off path is unchanged.
- **Q3-E3.8.2w**: W3-E3.8.2w (code only, not compiled): the request's fetch policy now reaches the fetch chain deadline on the three quarry-edge scrape routes through build_driver_with_policy; build_driver remains the default-policy wrapper. tier_stalled and fetch_deadline_exceeded are documented in ERROR_REASONS.md (504 TIMEOUT from the fetch deadline) and mirrored as TimeoutReasons in the orchestrator errs package. Still open: RunPolicy::from(ResolvedPolicy) always carries the default FetchPolicy, so a request timeout_ms does not set the chain budget (wiring it would change the policy fingerprint and needs a decision); the single-driver shortcut bypasses the budget; compose passthrough, RUNBOOKS, gateway/SPA mapping (E3.8.5) and moving the reason constants into quarry-core error.rs remain. Lead to compile quarry-runtime and quarry-edge, and run go test in services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/errs.
- **Q3-E3.8.1p**: E3.8.1 producers (Q3-E3.8.1p): with QUARRY_EDGE__FETCH_SUB_CODES on, the static, HTTP/3 and TLS-profile sends name a connect error as connect, a resolver failure as dns, and a handshake or certificate failure as tls (dns and connect retryable, tls not). Flag off is unchanged. Flag-on effects to decide before the flip: (1) Under honest (BUILD_DEFAULT, escalation.rs:34) connect, dns and tls failures become TransportError and fall through to the next tier, where UPSTREAM_BLOCKED used to fail them closed. tls is the ADR-5 question, because a handshake refusal can be a fingerprint block, and trigger_for_error cannot see the reason; keeping tls closed needs a reason-aware branch. (2) pipeline.rs:1612 and :1624 send UPSTREAM_BLOCKED to BadKind::Blocked and to autoscale overload; these failures now reach BadKind::Server and no overload. (3) Scrape retry (retry.rs:138) now retries connect and dns failures. (4) For the test step: fallback_driver.rs:1641 (honest_connect_failure_on_the_static_driver_does_not_fall_through_to_tls), escalation.rs:117-120 and RUNBOOKS.md:533 describe the flag-off state and need a flag-aware version. (5) Coverage: the pipeline preflight (pipeline.rs:1556 calling dns_guard.rs:157) returns INTERNAL 'dns lookup failed' before any send, so dns reaches the chain only through proxy egress, a pin miss, or the unpinned HTTP/3 and TLS sends. Typing the preflight failure is a follow-up outside this unit. Pre-existing and unchanged: the unclassified http3 path formats reqwest's Display, which carries the URL, into its message, and TransportFallbackDriver copies that into fallback_attempts; check it against ZDR.
- **Q3-E3.8.7w**: Q3-E3.8.7w: the edge now attaches the browser host-reputation hook to every agent browser egress policy it builds: run start, origin-extension widening, new tab, and the attachment tab. It is gated by the existing QUARRY_EDGE__REPUTATION_MODE (default off, which is also a kill switch for any org setting). The source is the shared `DefaultEngine` blocklist, exposed through a new defaulted `SecurityEngine::host_reputation()`, so hot reload reaches live runs. `agent_policy_gate::reputation_for` / `attach_reputation` / `effective_reputation_mode` do the work, and `human_browse::run_egress` attaches the hook after `open_web_egress`. The org cap (`browser.reputation`, tighten-only) is not wired because the web-policy catalogue has no such key; `org_reputation_cap()` returns None. Block mode is the first stage after Warn, since it can now refuse hosts. The pinned egress proxy path still does not consult the hook (D-E387-5). Nothing was compiled or run.
- **Q3-E3.8.8w**: Q3-E3.8.8w built, dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__NO_EGRESS (default off, nothing compiled yet). A scrape request with `no_egress: true` now runs through NoEgressDriver, built in main.rs from a DataPlaneArtifactReader on the ingest URL (else DATA_PLANE_URL) with the `artifacts-read` row and the cross-plane token provider, and stored as `AppState.no_egress`. The option is a quarry-core ScrapeOptions key (alias noEgress, default false). With it off, resolve answers `feature_disabled`. ZDR is refused with `no_egress_zdr_refused` before any fetch. A miss is NOT_FOUND `no_egress_miss` without the URL. The run uses the driver as its only one: no plan, HTTP/3, proxy, retry, per-host scheduler, DNS lookup (`Driver::reaches_network`), cache read or write, or index/title/ingest hop. The stream route and crawl/batch `scrape_options` refuse the key. The Data Plane `POST /v1/artifacts/lookup` contract is Data Plane owned and unconfirmed, so the reader's path constant is the assumption. The ADR topic 1 catalogue key is deferred as a follow-up (catalogue golden untouched). Regenerate the scrape schema golden and the SDKs at the test phase. Not covered here: `/v1/internal/run_page`, `/v1/extract` and other endpoints with their own option sets do not know `no_egress` and would ignore it.
- **Q3-E3.9.6p**: E3.9.6 producer (Q3-E3.9.6p): quarry-eval now writes lab/evals/usage_meters.json at the end of each fixture run, through an in-process metrics-facade recorder over the 12 canonical meter names, and it writes an empty map when nothing was recorded. In this tree that recorder observes nothing: the WS6.C meters are NATS UsageEvents emitted by quarry-edge rather than metrics counters, and the fixture run executes no metered path. The file is therefore always {"metrics":{}}, which release_matrix reads as a measured zero (D-E3967-8), so with a cap set spend_cap passes at 0 USD. Do not treat the Tier C spend gate as a control until a real producer exists (D-E3967-2) or an empty snapshot is treated as not measured. tier_b.rs was deliberately not changed.
- **Q3-GOLD-request-ask**: Q3-GOLD-request-ask (quarry-w45, code only): Quarry's RequestAsk wire golden is contracts/request-ask.v1.json (sha256 6b6d389763c382f06179a393fb05c0441b7f598b97fb075f60f223b071d8ecd1, pinned in the .sha256 beside it and in one GOLDENS row). RequestAsk now omits empty disable and deny_domains and absent robots_max, proxy_max and allow_domains when serialised; an empty allow list is still written. MP-ASKS status note (Model Plane not edited here): execution-core's placeholder can be replaced byte for byte, but its test ask_serialisation_matches_quarry_request_ask_schema (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/quarry_agent.rs, about line 3518) must change first. WireRequestAsk denies robots_max and proxy_max, and that test asserts the golden never carries them, so the robots_max and proxy_max cases fail it as written. The execution-core copy's VENDORED row and pin go in with that byte copy. The contract-goldens.yml path filter already names request-ask.v1.json*, so no workflow change is needed.
- **Q3-GOLD-schema-corpus**: Q3-GOLD-schema-corpus (branch quarry-w45, uncommitted): built. Quarry's schema corpus, crates/quarry-runtime/contracts/schema-corpus.json (33 cases from the Model Plane fixture minus const/anyOf/oneOf; sha256 e2ef5365f1f99363be9924f4bd9c9dbc0f64de6623d1447506b5d623a8b4990f), is pinned by a .sha256 and a GOLDENS row. Three schema_corpus tests in quarry-runtime ai_scrape.rs check its issues and bytes, and regenerate it under QUARRY_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1. Dark: no runtime change, no switch, no proto. Not compiled. Lead to run cargo test -p quarry-runtime schema_corpus. Open: no corpus case for invalid_schema (validator never emits it). Model Plane still uses its interim fixture; its VENDORED and CONSUMERS rows and an order-matching port are its own work.

- lab/evals release_matrix.rs: build_cost treats a usage snapshot with no metric as not_measured, so the Tier C spend_cap gate keeps failing closed (D-E3967-8). Q3-E3.9.6p makes quarry-eval always write {"metrics":{}}, and no metered path runs in the eval process, so an empty map is not a measured zero.

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q4-062a-edge-header**: Q4-062a edge half built, dark: QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_PARENT_RUN (off) lets a service principal send x-verevon-parent-run (request-id charset, 1-128 bytes); the edge scopes it in a task-local and writes it as the parent_run metadata key on the usage events of search/extract/map/scrape/crawl/batch. Advisory only: never read by auth, scopes, budget, grants, ZDR, policy or rate limits; user and api_key tokens never get one; dropped under ZDR (ZDR_ALLOWLIST unchanged, adding it needs an owner decision). The task-local does not cross tokio::spawn, so the agent run path (next unit) must set UsageAttrs.parent_run itself.
- **Q4-062a-edge-agent**: Q4-062a-edge-agent (edge half, agent runs): POST /v1/agent/runs reads x-verevon-parent-run through usage_attrs::parent_run_for under QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_PARENT_RUN (default off, service principals only, a resume uses its own request's header) and holds it on RunBudget via with_parent_run. The value is not in BudgetCheckpoint, receipts, the run record, the response or any decision. SegmentUsage carries it to emit_run_segment, which writes it as metadata off ZDR only. emit_run_segment scopes the task-local to the run's own value, so the header of the DELETE that closes a run cannot reach that run's segment. Interactive person sessions (human_browse.rs) do not carry the parent run. Nothing compiled or tested in this unit; the lead compiles after the batch.
- **Q4-E4.1-2a**: Q4-E4.1-2a built: JobResultsView now carries `kind` (closed ResultKind enum, page_index default, dataset reserved and never produced). The JSON envelope and the csv/jsonl X-Quarry-Result-Kind header both come from the same value. openapi.yaml and CONTRACTS.md state decision B2(a): results are a page index first, and a dataset needs WS5.I and WS16.J. Additive only, no Go change, no migration, ZDR 403 floor unchanged, and the route stays behind QUARRY_EDGE__JOB_RESULTS.
- **Q4-062a-mp-execution**: Q4-062a Model Plane half built, dark: execution-core web_search, web_fetch and the knowledge.map, crawl_site and batch tools send `x-request-id` ({run_id}.{step_id}) and the advisory `x-verevon-parent-run` (the run id) to the Quarry edge, on every attempt including the 401 retry. Values are validated (at most 128 bytes, `[A-Za-z0-9._:-]`). The parent run is withheld for ZDR and waived runs. No new MP switch; the effect is gated by the edge reader QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_PARENT_RUN (the edge adopts a valid x-request-id under QUARRY_EDGE__REQUEST_ID_ADOPT). The header is advisory only, and Quarry stays the only biller and authority. Not compiled. Overlaps four mp-w345 units that touch the same dispatcher hunk in runtime_loop/mod.rs.
- **Q4-E4.1-6a**: Q4-E4.1-6a built: quarry-edge/src/saved_job.rs is a pure module, dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__SAVED_JOBS and read per request. A definition `{version:1, job_kind, options}` is validated by the scrape reader plus `ScrapeLimits::default()`, or by the strict `/v1/crawl` parser with the source URL as the seed. A robots override, webhook, ZDR declaration or `url` in the options is refused as `not_saveable`. `crawl_schedule_config` maps a canonical definition to the scheduled-fire config and refuses options the fire path cannot honour as `not_schedulable`. No routes, openapi, protos or migrations are touched; the source routes that store `Source.config.definition` come in later units.
- **Q4-062a-mp-gateway**: Q4-062a-mp-gateway (Model Plane half of E4.6 062a) is built in code only on branch quarry-w45: model-gateway quarry.rs gains `pub mod attribution` and stamps x-request-id and x-verevon-parent-run at the three Quarry request builders (scrape, artifact read, search) inside their 401-retry loops; deep_research.rs run_deep_research scopes the whole pipeline (request id always, parent run only when not ZDR). No MP switch, per the unit. Live effect: deep-research Quarry reads send x-request-id as soon as this ships (edge QUARRY_EDGE__REQUEST_ID_ADOPT, default on), and the edge ignores the parent run until QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_PARENT_RUN (Q4-062a-edge-header, default off) is on. Chat tool-loop reads are stamped by Q4-062a-mp-toolloop, not by this unit. Not compiled; no tests written or run.
- **Q4-062a-mp-toolloop**: Q4-062a-mp-toolloop (E4.6 062a, Model Plane half): the Ask tool loop (run_tool_rounds_for_model_with_attachments, reached by run_tool_rounds, run_tool_rounds_for_model and sse.rs invoke_stream_sse) and run_forced_web_search_for_model (reached by run_forced_web_search and sse.rs) now run inside quarry::attribution::scope with request_id and, off ZDR, run_id. Each public entry is a wrapper over a private _inner with the same parameters; no public signature, switch, proto, route or migration changed. Three source-text guards were re-pointed to the _inner names. The parameter lists are duplicated, so a future signature change must be made in both the wrapper and its _inner; a positional call makes drift a compile error, not a silent one. Code only: nothing compiled or tested in this batch.
- **Q4-062b-pipeline**: Q4-062b-pipeline: built in apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/deep_research.rs plus the single call site in src/sse.rs (code only; nothing compiled; rustfmt --check parses the file). The outcome now carries stop (ResearchStop: completed, cancelled, deadline, budget_usd, cost_unpriced, no_evidence), halt_code (Option<String>), spent_usd_micro and source_summary (url_host and status only, max 50). The new halt and skip code 'cost-limit' is additive. For the wiring unit (Q4-062b-sse): no_evidence is a stop value its mapping list does not name; a synthesis failure with evidence reports completed with report None; caps in the receipt now reflect the run's effective caps. Open owner decision: the USD ceiling does not gate the final synthesis call (spec rule), so that call can exceed the ceiling. Lead checks when compiling: the gated search arm Ok(Ok((Vec::new(), 0, 0, Vec::new()))) and the &[] arguments to report_progress and finish_without_evidence rely on type inference.
- **Q4-062b-store**: Q4-062b (session-core research_runs store, migration 0115): each row is one addressable research phase of a chat turn. It holds ids, status, stop reason and halt code, budget and progress counters, and, for non-ZDR runs only, partial results and a report artifact id. A zdr row (a ZDR credential or a connection waiver) keeps ids, status, codes and counters only. The store drops partial and report ids for such a row, and the table CHECK refuses them. A thread has at most one running run; begin expires stale runs first and returns the running run's id on conflict. Erasure: the thread erase and the retention sweep remove a thread's rows through delete_thread_rows, the org purge removes an organisation's rows, and a legal hold keeps them. The migration number 0115 is provisional until merge, because mp-w345 units also claim numbers. Outside this unit: the ZDR posture matrix row and docs/ZDR.md are not touched.
- **Q4-062b-grpc**: Q4-062b-grpc: ResearchRunService (session-core ResearchRunGrpc over the 0115 research_runs store) is built dark behind RESEARCH_RUNS_ENABLED (literal true, default off; compose and flag registry). Begin refuses a thread that has a running run with aborted research_run_active and trailers x-mp-error-code and x-mp-active-run-id. Org and owner come only from the verified caller: the owner is the user, or the service principal for a service, so a run begun by a service is readable only through that service's token. A ZDR token is refused on writes (session:write); a ZDR or waived run is stored with zdr=true and refuses partial results, and Finish refuses report artifact ids, with zdr_durable_mutation_forbidden. The deadline default (900 s, bounds 60 to 3600) and the 1 MiB partial cap were chosen in this unit. Not compiled or tested here; Go bindings are pending the buf step.
- **Q4-062b-routes**: Q4 E4.6 062b gateway half (Q4-062b-routes) is built dark behind MODEL_GATEWAY_RESEARCH_RUNS (default off, in compose and the flag registry), alongside RESEARCH_RUNS_ENABLED on session-core. Routes: GET /v1/research/runs/:run_id, GET /v1/threads/:thread_id/research-run, and POST /v1/research/runs/:run_id/cancel (202 with stopRequested; 503 run_control_unavailable when the bus cannot tell). The cancel maps the rsr_ id to the chat session run id through Get, because the store mints rsr_ ids and the stream registers under the session run id; the decision line 'research run id equals the chat session run id' does not match the built store. partial and reportArtifactId are never returned for a zdr run or a non-persistent caller. Not yet wired: the chat branch (Q4-062b-sse) must call ResearchRunsClient begin, progress and finish, and use research_run_active and active_run_conflict, so the 409 has no route caller until then. Nothing was compiled or tested in this unit.
