A new `dispatch_save_recipe` handles the `browser_evidence.save_recipe` action, with its dispatcher arm next to `compile_procedure` in actions/handlers.rs; it answers 404 `browser_recipes_unavailable` while the flag is off. Nothing was compiled or tested. | - Deviations: The dispatcher arm is in apps/gateway/src/domains/actions/handlers.rs, which is where the generic action surface keeps its `browser_evidence.compile_procedure` arm. The executor function `dispatch_save_recipe` sits next to `dispatch_compile_procedure` in browser_evidence.rs, as the spec asks.; Upstream recipe-store failures are not passed through as they come. `envelope::error` only takes `&'static str` codes, and an upstream message could leak details. Failures map to the gateway's own closed codes: `browser_recipes_unavailable` (for capability-core `procedures_disabled`), `browser_recipe_not_found`, `browser_recipe_rejected`, `browser_recipe_limit_reached`, `browser_recipe_forbidden` and `browser_recipe_timeout`; any other 5xx becomes 502 `upstream_unavailable`. The upstream's closed `error.code` is carried only as `details.reason`. Quarry failures still go through the existing `forward_quarry_failure`.; The save route answers 201 Created, because the recipe store creates a resource. The action-surface envelope answers 200, like the other human actions.; The save sends `source_run_id` = the browser session id, an optional capability-core field, so a recipe can be traced back to its session.; The list route projects capability-core's `{procedures, next_after_id}` to `{recipes:[{recipeId,name,description?,sourceRunId?,latestVersion,createdAt,updatedAt}], nextAfterId?}` instead of passing the raw upstream body through.; When the flag is on, the existing compile route and the `compile_procedure` action also ask Quarry for a canonical compile, as the spec says. If `QUARRY_EDGE__PROCEDURE_CANONICAL` is off, Quarry's 400 `procedure_canonical_disabled` is forwarded through `forward_quarry_failure`. |
| QF-7-region-attach-action-contract | followups.md QB01 Q3-E3.3-d2a (region attach has no action contract; sibling tab-mention call has none either); CLAUDE.md 'add the action contract before wiring | built | I added two registry rows next to browser_evidence.compile_procedure: browser_region.attach ('Attach browser area') and browser_context.resolve_tab_mentions. Both have strict zod input schemas and the envelope output, and the descriptions say what the spec asks (human-only, ephemeral or read-only, grants nothing, refused under ZDR, browser-scraped). model-access.ts exempts both as no_owner_executor, so a model can never call them. In the gateway, handlers.rs routes the two action ids to the new dispatchers dispatch_region_attach (browser_evidence.rs) and dispatch_resolve_tab_mentions (browser_context.rs). Each dispatcher refuses unknown keys with a fixed 400 message and runs the same core the route uses (region_attach_view / resolve_turn_tab_mentions), so the owner gate, the ZDR refusal and the re-resolution are identical. The answer is the usual action envelope with no-store. While its switch is off, each dispatcher answers 404 browser_region_attach_unavailable / browser_tab_mentions_unavailable. BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH and BROWSER_TAB_MENTIONS are now read once into the new AppState fields browser_region_attach and browser_tab_mentions in build_state. The routers read those fields too, so the old std::env reads are gone. Both switches default off, and every AppState literal has been updated. | - Deviations: ownerPlane is 'ingestion', not 'frontend': PlaneOwner in src/shared/actions/types.ts has no 'frontend' value, and the neighbouring browser_evidence rows that Quarry backs use 'ingestion'.; The input shapes follow the real route bodies, not the spec. browser_region.attach is {sessionId, tabId (required), region{x,y,width,height}, image{mimeType: png/jpeg, dataBase64}, selectionText?}: the route's RegionAttachBody has no image width/height, tabId is required, and the field is selectionText, not selection. The mentions entries are {tabId: string/null/absent, token?}, matching TabMentionInput.; The dispatchers live in the domain modules (browser_evidence.rs / browser_context.rs), not in dispatchers.rs. This follows the existing dispatch_compile_procedure / dispatch_save_recipe pattern and keeps the private RegionAttachBody and region_attach_view module-private, so they did not need to become pub(crate). resolve_turn_tab_mentions was already pub(crate).; The model-access reason class is no_owner_executor, as the spec asks. The second argument is 'context_write' for the region attach (as for browser_run.steer) and 'read' for the read-only mention resolve.; The routers no longer read std::env. They take the new AppState fields, so build_state is the single reader. The old *_flag_on helpers stay only under #[cfg(test)] for the existing tests and now call config::human_browse_enabled, which parses the same way. config.rs reuses browser_context::TAB_MENTIONS_FLAG, and the unused REGION_ATTACH_FLAG const was removed. |
| Q4-E4.2-4b | plan 3.4 E4.2 Frontend; CLAUDE.md 'add or update the action contract before wiring a meaningful UI operation'; ProcedurePanel.tsx (WS9.D) | built | Built Q4-E4.2-4b in the Verevon v3 SPA. The action contract `browser_evidence.save_recipe` is in action-registry.ts (zod input {sessionId, name 1..120, description<=500}, exported output {recipeId, version, digest, slots}, human-only, irreversible) with an exempt model-access row (no_owner_executor / external_write), so a model cannot save recipes; its executor is the 4a gateway dispatcher arm. `useSaveRecipe` in use-browser-evidence.ts calls the action through executeAction (org from the evidence scope, user from the session), refuses ZDR scopes, and reads the answer through the output schema. ProcedurePanel shows the Save as recipe control (name input, slot names with kind, Saved line with version) only after a promotable compile with VITE_BROWSER_RECIPES === 'true', disabled for ZDR, and slot/expect badges on steps, also behind the flag; typed text is never shown. Nothing was compiled or tested (CODE ONLY); the admission golden and its Model Plane copies need regeneration by the lead. | - Deviations: Registry risk is 'medium' because the registry risk type has no external_write value; the external_write class is set in model-access.ts through the exempt row, as the spec asks.; ownerPlane is 'model' rather than 'ingestion': capability-core owns the recipe store (decision B2(b) in Q4-E4.2-6), and the save reaches model-gateway /v1/recipes. reversible is false because no action here removes a recipe.; outputSchema describes the action result payload (recipeId, version, digest, slots), exported like browserRunSteerOutput so the hook can parse it. The other browser evidence actions use envelopeStatusOutput.; Files outside the spec list changed. browser-client.ts gets type-only additions (optional slot, expect and target on the step view, and slots on the procedure view) that the badges and slot list need. browser-evidence.css gets badge and recipe styles. i18n/errors.ts gets copy for the recipe codes, because gateway-error-codes.test.ts requires copy for every code the gateway emits and browser_evidence.rs emits browser_recipes_unavailable, recipe_not_promotable, browser_recipe_* and invalid_browser_recipe_query.; The hook sends the save through executeAction with an actor built from the session user id and the evidence scope org id, the same org the compile call uses. executeAction supplies the idempotency key. The gateway's save dispatcher does not read it, so a timed-out retry could create a second recipe; the disabled button prevents double clicks.; Step badges render only on steps that carry a slot or an expect, and only with the flag on. |

Compile fix, Quarry-v2 Rust workspace: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Frontend Plane verevonv3 (SPA + apps/gateway BFF): green. 5 fixes.

Compile fix, everything else (Go modules, proto bindings, JSON/YAML, scripts): green. 4 fixes.

Compile fix, Model Plane Rust workspace + protos: green. 0 fixes.

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q4-E4.2-3e**: keep the wire AgentAction enum unchanged; replay-check takes raw JSON
- **Q4-E4.2-3f**: resolve locators on the Model Plane from the wire snapshot; Quarry remains the executor and re-validates every ref
- **Q4-E4.2-3g**: no auto-repair: mismatch hands back to the person
- **Q2-WS7.K-a**: ADR-1 outcome C (grant carries a snapshot), owner-delegated
- **Q2-WS16.S3a**: X-7 / DR-15.1 taken at recommendation G1 + O4 (two audiences, tenant retention; already registered by W2-CP-R13-1)
- **Q2-WS16.S3b**: X-7 / DR-15.1 at recommendation (G1 + O4)
- **Q2-WS10.S4b**: Proto field numbers are 21-23 on the Model side (fallback numbering); only JSON names matter to Quarry (MP-ASKS Lane D)
- **Q5-S3a**: D-W5-1 = QuarryBot/2.0 (+https://verevon.io/bot); robots tokens quarrybot and quarry; switch dark because the wire UA changes
- **Q5-S3b**: D-W5-1 recommended string; kept dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__UNIFIED_UA
- **Q2-WS14.S7-gw**: OQ-11 grantor: platform operators only (W23-FINISH-QUEUE decision 27); the Model never gets the key
- **Q2-WS15.S3a**: X-9 / D14 transport taken at option 2 (pull-model request rows), already built in control
- **Q4-E4.4b-edge**: Recommended: person tokens only (service principals refused by profile_guard), owner guard in the configured observe/enforce mode, no ingest, no local index, privacy class CredentialOrSecret, 404 for unknown ids
- **Q4-E4.2-4a**: save requires promotionAllowed; only the owner of the session can save
- **QF-7-region-attach-action-contract**: Model may not invoke either action (exempt no_owner_executor), matching the 'a mention never grants' rule
- **Q4-E4.2-4b**: person-initiated save only; no model-facing recipe tool in v1

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q2-WS6.B**: Q2-WS6.B built: the REST step, WebSocket step and delegate-lane failure arms (step.rs, attach.rs) now call failed_step_cost_for_error(ran, action, &error), so a navigate the driver refused before any page was used (domain grant, egress guard or target guard; SECURITY_BLOCKED, ran=None) charges the refusal rate to the ledger and receipt instead of the flat navigate cost. The dead_code allowance in budget.rs is gone. The gate projection stays at the flat estimate, no new switch was added, and BUDGET_ENFORCEMENT stays shadow. This closes the W2-U21a "not built" remainder.
- **Q2-WS8.B**: Q2-WS8.B built: the attach start_url, the tab-open URL and the resume recovery navigation now use validate_navigation_for_run with the run's RunNavPolicy, so the open-web DNS guard (D2-059) covers every navigation path, not only gate 12 of admit_action. The run-less validate_navigation is now #[cfg(test)] only. No switch (SSRF tightening). ZDR unaffected. Nothing compiled. rustfmt reported no parse errors in the four files (pre-existing formatting diffs only).
- **Q2-WS8.E**: Q2-WS8.E (W2-U07 edge half; closes D-U07-1 and SR-U07-1) is built in code and not compiled. new_tab records a tab kept by a timed-out open (tab_origins plus a Tab Opened timeline event) and returns the timeout with its tab_id. The attach open_tab branch closes that tab, returns the timeout, and adds no TabLedger entry. Only ids and classes are stored and no URL is added to any detail (ZDR). No switch: a bug fix, and the attach path rides QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH. The attach 504 still names the closed tab in details.tab_id, as the spec asks; that id is dead after the close.
- **Q5-S6a**: Q5-S6a (2026-11-11, branch quarry-w45): the ad-blocking claims in the Quarry docs are corrected. FIXES_APPLIED.md lines 84 and 92 and SCRAPING_ENGINE_GAPS.md gap 5 and section 2.5 are marked "claimed, no such code on main; archive" or "No adblock code exists". GAP.md's ad-blocking rows read "Not built" with citations: no ScrapeRequest field (crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:559), no ad or tracker filter, and blockAds appears only as a comment at crates/quarry-browser/src/browserless.rs:14. The register's Q5-S6a section records the two archive rows and the GAP update, all with replacement build plan E5.2 (F-t2-066), parked until ADR 5.
- **Q4-E4.2-3e**: Q4-E4.2-3e (execution-core Quarry client, quarry-w45): the client gains three surfaces, all inert with no switch. StepRequest and StepParams carry an optional `expect` (skipped when unset, default None) that step() forwards as Quarry's top-level step `expect` (Quarry's StepBody reads it, refused with 400 unless QUARRY_EDGE__STEP_EXPECT is on). WireObservation reads `action_outcome` leniently (status and reason_code only, the free-text detail is not read, never serialised back). WireSnapshotTarget reads tag, placeholder, test_id and the fingerprint id, read-only and never serialised, so the chat-facing tool output does not change. QuarryAgentClient::replay_check POSTs the raw-JSON procedure and actions with prefix to /v1/agent/procedures/replay-check and returns Quarry's ReplayDecision from the envelope data; client-side nothing is persisted or logged (the body carries bound slot text, so the request type derives no Debug), and Quarry's ZDR matrix lists the route as inert, a pure function of its body. Nothing calls these before 3g/3h.
- **Q4-E4.2-3f**: Q4-E4.2-3f built (pure, no switch). browser_agent/recipe.rs holds the saved-recipe types, Recipe::parse, bind_slots and BoundRecipe (text_for, recipe), resolve_locator (mirrors quarry-runtime observation.rs semantic_matches), and proposal_for_step. check_json is the stored action with the fresh snapshot_id and generation (and the bound text for typing steps), which Quarry's replay-check compares after dropping the handles and the slot text. Dependency Q4-E4.2-3e was already in the tree: WireSnapshotTarget tag, placeholder, test_id and fingerprint; replay_check; StepParams.expect; WireObservation.action_outcome. Flag for 3g/3h: plain CSS click and type steps (kind click or type with a selector) are kept by Quarry's v2 compiler and are proposed with no target, so decide whether recipe mode may run selector steps. The lead should compile execution-core and run the recipe tests, which are inline and have not been run.
- **Q4-E4.2-3g**: Q4-E4.2-3g (quarry-w45, code only, not compiled): execution-core now has the RecipePlanner replay planner (browser_agent/recipe_planner.rs), default step_expect and note_wire_observation on ActionPlanner (llm_planner.rs), and an expect argument on dispatch_step that only leased runs send (browser_agent.rs). Unit 3h must: set widened_actions for recipes with select, press, back or wait_for steps (otherwise the recipe hands back at that step); leave start_url unset for recipe runs (the loop otherwise navigates there before the recipe's first step); and never resume a run paused for approval with a fresh planner, because the planner state is in memory and a restart would replay from step 0. Lead to confirm: the planner persists and logs nothing, but the replay-check body carries bound slot text to Quarry, as the step call does, and the body has no zdr field. Upstream: recipe.rs proposal_for_step should also drop the top-level frame id, and wait_for_semantic halts when the element is not yet in the snapshot, since it needs a resolved target.
- **Q2-WS7.K-a**: Q2-WS7.K-a (proto half of WS7.K): upstream apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/browser.proto now defines WebPolicy (v, rev, caps map, robots_max, proxy_max, domains_allow, domains_deny) and web_policy = 20 on AcquireGrantResponse, ValidateGrantResponse and DeriveRunGrantResponse. The change is additive, with no request field, no switch and no content stored. The vendored-subset check in browser-grant-contract.test.mjs is now strict, with both PENDING_UPSTREAM sets emptied. Pending: the Go regeneration of browser.pb.go, which the lead runs. The readers are Q2-WS7.K-b.
- **Q2-WS14.S6-ui**: Q2-WS14.S6-ui (quarry-w45): 'Follow links to other sites' is a UI-surface crawl option (signal crawlAllowExternalLinks, default off) inside the VITE_KNOWLEDGE_SCRAPE_OPTIONS_ENABLED block. It reaches startCrawl. The agent-run path cannot carry it, because knowledge.crawl_site's Model-visible zod input has no such key and zod strips it, so agent-run crawls do not follow links to other sites even when ticked; a lead decision is open (disable the checkbox in agent-run mode, or add a human-only route). The job-row line 'Skipped N links outside this site' is driven by links_denied, which counts every scope denial (duplicate, depth, include/exclude pattern, robots, off-site), so it can overstate the off-site count; the off-site-only figure is denialCounts.external_host_disabled, which normalizeCrawlEvent already parses. linksDenied is written into the existing crawl-job localStorage snapshot the same way pagesBlockedByRobots is; it is a count, not content, but a literal 'nothing persisted client side' reading would need it excluded from the snapshot.
- **Q5-S1d**: Q5-S1d built on quarry-w45 (SPA only, code only, not compiled or tested). VITE_BROWSER_PDF (literal 'true', default off) shows a Save-as-PDF toolbar button beside the screenshot button. It is hidden for ZDR sessions, disabled by controlsDisabled(), and sends { type: 'pdf' } through the existing browser action route. browser-session.ts carries the PDF id and URL through the observation, timeline and replay paths and emits a kind 'pdf' descriptor (page.pdf, mediaKind 'pdf'), so the existing 'Last ned PDF' link renders once a timeline entry carries the PDF. Still open: the gateway projection timeline (browser.rs browser_timeline) and the Quarry observation (Q5-S1c) do not yet emit the PDF id, so the link will not appear end to end until both land.
- **Q2-WS15.S4b**: Q2-WS15.S4b built (code only; the lead compiles and runs the tests). PUT /v1/internal/schedules/{id}/runtime writes last_run_at and next_run_at (the body is unix milliseconds, stored as unix seconds like created_at and updated_at) and skipped_overlap_count through SchedulesStore.UpdateRuntime. The write never moves updated_at, so NeedsActivation and the migration 017 guard are unaffected. Contract for Q2-WS15.S4c: send unix milliseconds (time.UnixMilli), send all three keys (skipped_overlap_count is required), and send no other key, since an unknown key is a 400. Known gap 4 in docs/SCHEDULES.md and the row 5 note in docs/RUNBOOKS.md (around line 1426) stay open until S4c adds the caller; this route alone closes nothing. Pre-existing and not changed here: docs/CONTRACTS.md (around line 1439) still says the control internal routes are not described; the InternalControlSchedule.created_at description in openapi-internal.yaml says unix milliseconds while the wire carries seconds; and the orchestratorRoutes table in secret_split_test.go does not list the new PUT (it is covered by the prefix rule and the walk test only).
- **Q2-WS16.S3a**: Q2-WS16.S3a built. The Quarry runtime model lane mints its two org-bound bearers on the Dynamic path: model-extract (aud model-gateway) and model-extract-inference (aud inference-core), via ModelPlaneClient::lane_bearers, for the verified org only. chat_for_org and post_document_lane (OCR/PII) both use it. A 401 drops both cached tokens and retries once with force_refresh; if that refresh mint fails, the call returns Unauthorized. A first-mint failure or an empty org is the new ModelHopError::LaneUnavailable, which maps to AI report reason model_lane_unavailable; nothing is sent and the call is not counted. A client with no credential stays LaneNotGranted. The manifest rows dropped `pending` and now list mp_client.rs in used_by. Follow-ups: re-pin the manifest .sha256, re-vendor the auth-core fixture copy and update the zdr-contract-sync digest (together with QF-24's office-extract edit). The edge still builds its AI-formats client from the dev token only (ai_formats::availability / engine_for), and model_plane_gate still checks model-invoke for ai_formats, so the Dynamic lane is not reachable from the edge until a follow-up unit wires with_token_provider there. QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS stays default off.
- **Q2-WS16.S3b**: Q2-WS16.S3b built (not compiled, not run): in scrape_contract/ai_formats.rs, availability() and engine_for() now go through lane_credential(), which prefers state.service_token_provider (the minted model-extract + model-extract-inference pair per verified org) over the dev MODEL_PLANE_TOKEN. Engines are cached per (url, credential kind). The 16-permit process semaphore and the ZDR early return with no client are unchanged. Everything stays dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS (default off). In RUNBOOKS the 'no production path' paragraph is replaced with: grants exist, set MODEL_PLANE_URL plus QUARRY_SERVICE_API_KEY, run the E2 proof, then set AI_FORMATS=1. Open: /v1/extract still uses /v1/invoke through the model-invoke row (granted:false), so it stays 501 on a deployed stack until a decision opens that row or moves extract to the chat lane.
- **Q2-WS16.S6a**: Q2-WS16.S6a built (code only, not compiled). The /v1/extract model arm now runs on the shared AiScrapeEngine over /v1/ai/chat, via a new scrape_contract::ai_formats::engine_for_request (same cache, process semaphore and minted lane as /v1/scrape). It does one AiSpec::Json run per source (prompt-only uses {"type":"object"}; the schema is checked with parse_ai_object, so a bad one is a 400), with buffer_unordered(QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_CONCURRENCY) and results in `urls` order. A shared stop flag stops scheduling after the first 429 or lane refusal, and the engine deadline is the 50 s request budget. Each item carries `ai` (the json AiFormatReport) and `data` only when valid. Contract change: an answer that is still schema-invalid is now status error / SCHEMA_INVALID with no data, where it used to be ok with invalid data. New: a 403 for a service principal without extract:ai that sends a schema or prompt. Unchanged and still dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS: 501 texts, web-policy gate, ZDR (no engine built, per-item ZDR_UNAVAILABLE with ai skipped), privacy block, quarry.extract.url metering. Follow-up for the lead: the existing extract_routes.rs unit tests still mock POST /v1/invoke with the old reply shape. Several will fail at runtime until their model doubles move to /v1/ai/chat with the AiChatResponse shape. The same goes for the schema-invalid test (expects status ok), the prompt-only key-set test (now has `ai`) and the lane-refusal test that expects exactly one fetch (with concurrency it can be more).
- **Q2-WS10.S4a**: Q2-WS10.S4a built (types only, no switch): quarry-core/src/trace.rs (RunOrigin, AgentRef, RunTraceContext with sanitized(), EventTrace, valid_token, derive_origin, TRACE_TOKEN_MAX=64). Event now has an optional `trace` field, left out of the JSON when None. Seven new agent-class EventTypes (BrowserSessionOpened/Closed, BrowserControlChanged, BrowserTabChanged, StepReceiptRecorded, EgressDecided, ArtifactRecorded) are kept off the Ingestion bus by is_agent_class. They map to the SSE names browser.session.opened/closed, browser.control, browser.tab, browser.receipt, browser.egress and browser.artifact, and have Go mirror constants. Nothing emits them yet; Q2-WS10.S4d adds the emissions. Origin and agent are labels only (origin_is_not_authz).
- **Q2-WS10.S4b**: Q2-WS10.S4b built: quarry-runtime `trace_projection.rs` is a pure default-deny mapper from Quarry agent events to the Model Plane run-trace envelope, and it refuses ZDR runs. The envelope key for time is `ts`, not `occurred_at` (Model `Envelope` requires `ts`). `causation_id` is always present, possibly empty. `resource_ref`/`idempotency_key` follow the unit spec (`browser_run/<id>`, `quarry:<browser_run_id>:<seq>`) and differ from the Model golden (`run/<model_run_id>`, event id); reconcile this before mapper test C2. The payload allowlists match plan 3.2 WS10 'Payload allowlists'; strings over 256 chars are dropped, URLs are reduced to an origin, and a payload over 4096 bytes becomes a truncated marker. Not compiled and no tests written yet. C2 also needs the vendored golden under `crates/quarry-runtime/tests/fixtures/model-trace/`.
- **QF-11-spa-steer-held-wiring**: QF-11 built: ChatLiveRunPanel takes onSteerResend and passes it to SteerInput as onResend. ChatPage wires onSteerHeldMessages and onSteerResend to sendHeldSteerMessages, which sends each held message through submitWithWorkPeriod in order, stops at the first throw, skips foreign-origin and temporary threads, and puts unsent text back in the composer draft with an nb/en notice. Known limits, not fixed here: (a) a held message that the gateway refuses after the hand-off (asynchronous, inside sendContent) is not put back, because the draft hook's failure restore returns the composer text, not the held text (the same limit as the artifact-selection send). (b) A mid-run refusal (deliverMidRun) resolves without throwing, so the helper counts it as sent; the refusal shows in the chat's queued-input strip. (c) ChatLiveRunPanel calls steer.reset() when the watched run changes before it ends (around line 371), which drops that run's held messages without sending them. This is pre-existing and outside QF-11. (d) Composer-local state (chips, skills, agent mode, tone, effort, model pick) is not carried, because the page does not hold it.
- **Q5-S3a**: Q5-S3a: the code was already there from QB09 (Q4-E4.4a2-product-token). quarry_core::identity has the full D-W5-1 surface: the honest UA QuarryBot/2.0 (+https://verevon.io/bot), robots tokens quarrybot and quarry, the legacy edge, policy and HTTP/3 strings written exactly as before, and the dark switch QUARRY_EDGE__UNIFIED_UA, read once. It also has the edge, policy, http3 and tls UA selectors plus append_product_token and strip_product_token. Nothing changed in this unit. The inline test module is still to do.
- **Q5-S3b**: Q5-S3b built on quarry-w45 (code only, not compiled or tested). The edge default UA, HTTP/3 UA, answer fetcher UA, policy UA, discovery token and robots token set now come from quarry_core::identity; with QUARRY_EDGE__UNIFIED_UA off every string is byte-identical to before. Open for the batch: (a) the static driver follows the switch only after Q5-S2b deletes the compose line QUARRY_EDGE__USER_AGENT (apps/Ingestion Plane/docker-compose.yml:58), because an explicit env value overrides default_ua(); (b) with the switch on, the bounded client at fetch.rs:211, built with DISCOVERY_USER_AGENT (also used by map_routes MAP_UA), still sends the bare token QuarryBot, because the spec keeps that value unchanged, so the lead should decide whether discovery moves to HONEST_USER_AGENT; (c) the switch is read once in quarry_core::identity::unified_enabled() (Q5-S3a's design), not in quarry-edge's config parser, and this unit does not change that.
- **Q2-WS14.S7-gw**: Q2-WS14.S7-gw built (code only, not compiled): the verevonv3 gateway (domains/knowledge/quarry.rs) now forwards `ignore_robots_txt: true` from the UI crawl surface (POST /api/v1/knowledge/crawl) only when VEREVON_GATEWAY_CRAWL_ROBOTS_OVERRIDE is exactly "true" (default off, read per request like VEREVON_GATEWAY_AGENT_WEB_TOOLSET). Only a boolean true under ignoreRobotsTxt, ignore_robots_txt or ignoreRobots is forwarded. The Model surface (knowledge.crawl_site / web.crawl_site) never forwards a robots key, with or without the switch. The edge entitlement check (403 policy_denied, service principals refused) and quarry-control's audit-before-ack remain the authority. ZDR is unaffected. Follow-ups: the FE crawl options control (SPA toggle plus action contract) is still to build; the switch is not in AppState and is not in any compose/env file yet.
- **Q5-F1c**: Q5-F1c built: the org-core testdata and auth-core fixture copies of web-policy-catalogue.v1.json and their .sha256 pins were re-vendored byte for byte from quarry-core/contracts (sha256 b6c0a104c274c5820a95dc60cb5a11a1e95b046611460a40476698fe57665abb, LF), so browser.consent.reject (ability, baseline disabled, zdr_implies_disabled false) is now a valid org-document key. The org-core catalogue-shape test now expects 14 keys. The auth-core specs and the ZDR claims golden are unchanged. Nothing was compiled or run.
- **Q4-062b-sseid-c**: Q4-062b-sseid: the research run id now reaches the chat stream. A deep-research turn that begins a recorded run emits a research_run control frame, data {research_run_id, status}, with status running. It emits one more at each end: failed, cancelled on a client stop, or the pipeline's own completed or stopped. The frames are buffered for resume like other frames and are gated only by the existing MODEL_GATEWAY_RESEARCH_RUNS switch (default off), read per request. Nothing is sent for an unrecorded run or with the switch off. The frame is ungated by feature family because the id is the only way to reach the read and cancel routes. Status includes cancelled because the store records it. Nothing was compiled or tested here. The golden, pin, SPA vendored copy, generated SPA types, taxonomy regeneration and the SPA dispatchEvent case are listed for the lead.
- **Q2-WS15.S3a**: Q2-WS15.S3a (edge half), built 2026-11-11 in quarry-w45, not compiled or tested. Edge trigger and backfill pass control's 202 through and keep control's 409 and 501 codes with details.reason. They forward the caller's Idempotency-Key through the existing handoff helper (invalid values are ignored). New tenant reads: GET /v1/schedules/{id}/requests/{rid} and GET /v1/schedules/{id}/runs, in the list's auth chain, with ids parsed before the path is built. parse_schedule_id and parse_request_id were added here; QF-4 must reuse them. openapi.yaml and SCHEDULES.md are updated, with the edge halves of Known gaps 1 and 6 struck. The route pin needs 131 -> 133 and the SDKs need regenerating. The control flag stays off. RUNBOOKS.md item 1 (around line 1422) and item 14 (around line 1435) still describe the old edge gap and should be closed or reworded by the lead.
- **Q4-E4.4b-edge**: Q4-E4.4b-edge is built on branch quarry-w45 in quarry-edge. /v1/scrape accepts profile_id and profileId only while QUARRY_EDGE__SCRAPE_PROFILE_PIN is on (default off), and the stream route refuses them with unsupported_in_stream. A pinned scrape authorises the profile through profile_guard with ProfileOp::Attach: service principals and API keys get 403, and a profile the org does not hold gets 404 (a scrape never creates one). It is never ingested or run under ZDR, its privacy is forced to CredentialOrSecret (no managed browser, no model hop), it keeps no index, title, ingest, page-image or OCR hop, and it is never cached. Residual risks: the owner guard is observe by default, so until QUARRY_EDGE__PROFILE_OWNER_ENFORCE is set to enforce, a person can pin another person's user_private profile in the same org; and the pinned scrape's scrape_record artifact is org-readable, which the spec accepted.
- **Q4-E4.2-4a**: Q4-E4.2-4a built, dark behind BROWSER_RECIPES (AppState.browser_recipes, default off). With the flag on, the gateway compiles canonically (`canonical: true`) and `procedure_view` passes through the version 2 fields: `target.locator`, `slot` and `expect` per step, and `slots` with name and kind only. It mounts `POST /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/recipes` (owner gate, then ZDR refusal with `browser_receipts_unavailable_zdr`, then canonical compile, then quality check, then 409 `recipe_not_promotable` with reasons when the procedure is not promotable, then model-gateway `/v1/recipes` with the caller's capability token, returning `{recipeId, version, digest, slots}`) and `GET /api/v1/browser/recipes` (validated `limit`/`afterId`, projected list). The `browser_evidence.save_recipe` action has its executor (`dispatch_save_recipe`, 404 `browser_recipes_unavailable` while off) and its arm in actions/handlers.rs; the registry action itself is Q4-E4.2-4b. Upstream failures map to the closed `browser_recipe_*` codes, with the upstream code only in `details.reason`. Nothing was compiled or tested.
- **QF-7-region-attach-action-contract**: QF-7 built: browser_region.attach and browser_context.resolve_tab_mentions now have action contracts (registry rows with strict zod inputs; model-access exempt no_owner_executor, so they are human-only). Gateway: arms in handlers.rs go to dispatch_region_attach (browser_evidence.rs) and dispatch_resolve_tab_mentions (browser_context.rs). These refuse unknown keys and reuse the route cores, so the owner gate and ZDR refusal are unchanged, and they answer 404 while their switch is off. BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH and BROWSER_TAB_MENTIONS moved from router-time std::env reads to AppState fields set in build_state. ownerPlane is 'ingestion' because 'frontend' is not a PlaneOwner. The SPA still calls the routes. Still open: regenerate the admission golden. Nothing compiled yet.

The Save as recipe control in the Procedure pane shows only after a promotable compile with VITE_BROWSER_RECIPES=true, and is disabled for ZDR sessions. Open items for the lead: regenerate the admission golden (see regenerate). gateway-error-codes.test.ts will flag browser_region_attach_unavailable, which browser_evidence.rs emits in the working tree from another unit and which has no ERROR_COPY key yet. Q4-E4.2-4d-c names browser_recipe_unavailable (singular) for copy, but the gateway code is browser_recipes_unavailable (plural).

Status is checked against a closed set (running/completed/stopped/cancelled/failed); a run with any other status gets 502. A cancel returns 202 {data:{id, stopRequested}}. Typed upstream refusals keep their status, code, message and details (404 stays 404, 409 stays 409); untyped 404/409 get gateway codes, and anything else becomes 502 upstream_unavailable. In the SPA, action-registry.ts has the new chat.research_run.cancel contract (strict input {researchRunId} with the rsr_ regex, risk low, requiresApproval false, reversible false, ownerPlane model), and model-access.ts exempts it with no_owner_executor so a Model cannot cancel a run. | - Deviations: The flag is the field `chat_research_runs` on `SpaceFeatureFlags` (state.space_features.chat_research_runs), not a top-level `AppState` field. That struct holds the other chat flags (chat_locale_hint, chat_artifact_promote), and its documented convention is that Default is all-off, so the many AppState test literals and for_tests need no edit. It is read with the existing env_bool helper (1/true/yes/on).; model-gateway's GET /v1/research/runs/:run_id does not return a thread id today, so `threadId` is relayed only when upstream sends threadId or thread_id; otherwise it is null. `reportId` comes from upstream's `reportArtifactId`, which upstream already leaves out for ZDR, waived or non-persistent callers.; For cancel, the normalised body is {id, stopRequested} with 202, matching the upstream cancel answer, rather than the full run shape.; No dispatcher arm was added. browser_run.control, the pattern the spec names, has none, and no registry parity test requires one.; I added an org-scope 403 (org_scope_required) before the upstream call, mirroring history.rs scope_for, which the thread-goal relay also applies. |
| QF-8-bff-turn-browser-context | followups.md QB02 Q3-E3.3-d2b (regions not forwarded; gateway must re-check at send time); plan 3.3 E3.3 '@tab resolved in the gateway, failing closed; attachme | built | The gateway now checks browser page context on the chat send path. AppState has two new fields, both read once in build_state: `browser_turn_context` (BROWSER_TURN_CONTEXT, only the literal `true` turns it on, through a new `browser_turn_context_enabled` parser) and `browser_region_attach_key` (BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH_KEY). The region attach answer now carries `attachToken` and `attachTokenExpiresAt` when a key is set. The token is stateless: HMAC-SHA256 over `v1/user_id/org_id/session_id/tab_id/x,y,w,h/sha256hex(image)/expires_unix`, valid for 600 s, and nothing is stored. The new `inject_turn_browser_context` in browser_context.rs runs in `stream_chat` right after `apply_org_zdr_posture`. It always removes the client's `browser_tab_refs`, `browser_regions` and `browser_context_verified`, and stops there while the flag is off. With the flag on it does three things. A ZDR turn that carried either field gets 400 `browser_context_refused_zdr` before any upstream call. Tab refs (at most 8, all from one session) go back through `resolve_turn_tab_mentions` and are written back as gateway refs in snake_case. Regions (at most 4) must each pass `verify_turn_region` in browser_evidence.rs, which checks the key, the shape, the image/rect match, the sha256 digest and the token for this user, org, session, tab and rect, constant-time and unexpired; any failure gives 400 `browser_region_unverified`. Verified regions are written back as `trust_class: browser-scraped`, `grant: none`, `retention: ephemeral`, and the body gets `browser_context_verified: true`. | - Deviations: The token is `<expires_unix>.<base64url HMAC>`, not the bare base64url MAC the spec describes, because the verifier needs to know the expiry to rebuild the signed payload. The payload is exactly the format the spec gives.; Error codes: a tab-ref shape error (more than 8 refs, mixed sessions, missing ids, a field that is not an array) answers 400 `invalid_input`, the existing code in browser_context.rs. A failure inside the resolver passes through with its own status and code: owner-gate 404, ZDR-session 409 `browser_tab_context_refused_zdr`, unresolved tab 409 `browser_tab_mention_unresolved`. More than 4 regions answers 400 `browser_region_unverified`.; A region on a chat turn forwards only what the token covers: session, tab, rect and image. Selection text, labels and titles from the client are dropped, because they are not signed.; A region's input fields may be snake_case (chat body) or camelCase (the attach answer's names), through serde aliases. A tab ref's session_id/tab_id may be written either way too.; No wiremock tests were written, because the hard rules say CODE ONLY. The three tests the spec names (forged ref dropped when off, ZDR refusal, bad token refusal) are still to be added. |
| QF-9-spa-region-send | followups.md QB02 Q3-E3.3-d2b (composer refuses region sends); plan 3.3 E3.3 region attach | built | The region attach answer now keeps `attachToken: string / null`. browser-client.ts reads it from the gateway answer and accepts only 16-512 characters of [A-Za-z0-9_.-]. The composer's per-send region model (`DashboardComposerBrowserRegion`) now carries the token, the attached image (mime, base64, width, height) and the selection text, so a send has what the gateway needs to verify the region. A new optional `ChatInvokeRequest.browserRegions` (also added to `SendOptions` in chat-types.ts) becomes `browser_regions` in buildChatWireBody: snake_case `{session_id, tab_id, attach_token, region, image, selection_text?}`, at most 4, only for regions that have a token. It uses the same guard as browser_tab_refs (zdr, subscription, support read-only, conversation-only) and leaves the key out when the list is empty. `assertComposerRegionsSendable` now refuses a send with regions when VITE_BROWSER_REGION_SEND is not "true", in a ZDR or temporary chat, while an answer is streaming, with more than 4 regions, or when any region lacks a token. Otherwise it returns the regions, and handleComposerSubmit passes them to sendContent as `browserRegions`. The composer's existing remove-after-successful-submit logic clears them from the draft. The gateway refusal codes `browser_region_unverified` and `browser_context_refused_zdr` now have nb/en wording in streamFailurePresentation. The failed-send draft settle gives the typed text back. | - Deviations: The composer's region model is `DashboardComposerBrowserRegion` in DashboardComposer.tsx, not a type in region-attach.ts. region-attach.ts holds the gateway answer type (BrowserRegionAttachment), so the token goes there. I extended DashboardComposerBrowserRegion with attachToken, image and selectionText, and updated the one place that builds it in DashboardComposer.tsx. Before this change the send carried no image, so the gateway could not have verified a region.; Added two exports to region-attach.ts: a shared turn type `BrowserRegionTurnInput` and `BROWSER_REGIONS_PER_TURN_MAX = 4`. The switch reader `browserRegionSendEnabled()` also lives there and follows the `=== 'true'` convention of VITE_BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH.; The gateway codes are mapped in chat-normalizers.ts streamFailurePresentation, not in the controller. Gateway refusals arrive asynchronously through the stream's onError, after the composer submit has already resolved. The typed text comes back through the existing failed-send draft settle. The sent regions themselves are not restored after a gateway refusal, so the message tells the person to attach the area again. For an unverified token that is needed anyway, since a new token is required.; chat-types.ts SendOptions got the `browserRegions` field, because the send-options type lives there. |
| QF-1-batch-hash | followups.md QB02 Q3-E3.5-3a + QB04 Q3-E3.7.2w (W3-E3.7.1 batch idempotency; plan 3.3 E3.7 scope 'payload-hash 409') | built | The gap existed and is now closed. batch_payload_hash in handoff.rs takes the raw webhook and max_parallel values as two more Option arguments and adds each key to the canonical JSON only when it was sent, so a same-key retry under QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_IDEMPOTENCY that differs only in those fields returns 409 instead of re-attaching. The caller in routes.rs batch_handoff passes req.webhook and max_parallel.or(max_parallel_camel), taken before the URL check and the allowance clamp, and its W3-E3.7.1 comment now says so. The existing unit test passes None, None, gains the webhook-only and max_parallel-only inequality asserts, and gains a None/None assert pinned to the pre-change SHA-256. The two docs that state the hash coverage (openapi.yaml 409 and Idempotency-Key text, CONTRACTS.md E3.7.1 flag-on bullet) now match the code. Nothing was compiled or run, per the code-only rule. | - Deviations: Line numbers had drifted. The caller is now routes.rs:2007 (spec said 1865), the BatchRequest max_parallel fields are at routes.rs:1897-1899 (spec said 1755-1757), and the hash unit test is at handoff.rs:746-776 (spec said 733-739). I matched by content, not by line.; The None/None assert pins a literal digest, sha256:fa96cdc69a343e1f69f1901b7251daa4c815477ae5af96fda10bb6f9af878f1b, computed by hand with sha256sum over the pre-change canonical bytes {"ingest":true,"kind":"batch","urls":["https://a.example/","https://b.example/"]}. Key order follows serde_json's default BTreeMap, which Cargo.lock supports (no preserve_order or indexmap under serde_json). The lead's first test run is the first execution of that assert.; Docs beyond the spec's two files: docs/openapi.yaml (the 409 response description and the Idempotency-Key header description) and docs/CONTRACTS.md (the E3.7.1 flag-on bullet) state the hash coverage, so they now name webhook and max_parallel. These are not plan docs. No sha256 pin or test in the repo reads that wording; I checked sdks/, scripts/, services/ and the Rust readers of openapi.yaml.; Formatting: the lines I changed follow rustfmt --check output. rustfmt still reports hunks in both files that predate this change; I left those alone. |
| Q2-WS16.S5a | Plan 3.2 WS16.S5 (~2969) harvest_map.rs prefill + render_harvest_block | built | Added the pure module crates/quarry-runtime/src/harvest_map.rs with render_harvest_block (figures with unit and period in the answer.rs format, JSON-LD scalar fields, first table headers, cut to cap_chars on whole lines), prefill (returns Prefill with values, provenance and remainder_schema; PrefillSource and PrefillRule closed enums), and merge, and registered it with `pub mod harvest_map;` in lib.rs. prefill takes only top-level string/number/integer/boolean properties whose depth-1 key folds to the same name across JSON-LD raw nodes, microdata items and retained hydration blobs, type-checks and enum-checks the value, and skips a property whose candidates disagree. Nothing calls the module yet and no switch was added (S5b wires it). rustfmt --check passes on the new file; nothing was compiled or tested per the CODE ONLY rule. lib.rs also carries a concurrent LlmMetricsMode re-export from another writer, which I left untouched. | - Deviations: render_harvest_block cuts on whole lines rather than at a hard character offset: a line that would overflow cap_chars is skipped and later shorter lines may still fit. The result never exceeds cap_chars and never splits a figure or a character.; Labels, values, units and periods have control characters turned into spaces, so a page value cannot start a new line in the block. answer.rs render_key_figures does not do this; its figure line format is otherwise copied exactly. answer.rs was not edited.; JSON-LD scalars are every string field of JsonLdSummary except image (a URL), labelled with schema.org names such as `- JSON-LD datePublished: ...`.; 'First table headers' is read as the headers of the first table that has a header row.; Spec is silent on conflicts: when type-compatible, enum-admitted candidates from different keys or sources disagree, the property is not prefilled and stays with the model. Chosen for precision.; Spec is silent on value limits: prefilled strings are trimmed, must be non-empty and at most 2,000 characters. Integer targets accept an integral JSON number or a numeric string that parses exactly (no separators, no comma decimals). Number targets accept finite numeric strings only.; Key folding removes underscores and lowercases, as the spec lists case, underscore and camelCase; hyphens and dots are not folded. JSON-LD keys beginning with '@' never match.; merge has the signature merge(prefill: &Prefill, model_output: Value) -> Value. Prefilled values override the same keys in the model output. A non-object model output is replaced by the prefilled values. With nothing prefilled, the model output is returned unchanged.; Hydration provenance covers both JSON script payloads and window state assignments (BlobSource::Hydration and BlobSource::Assignment).; No in-file unit tests were written. The spec allows them but does not require them, and the CODE ONLY rule applies. |
| Q2-WS6.G | Plan 3.2 WS6.G (D6 S6) per-org render permits; .plan-work/quarry-w1w2/W23-W2-CP-L2-status.md 'Not done: plan-aware render permits are computed (org_render_permi | built | Built the plan-aware per-org render cap on branch quarry-w45 (code only; nothing compiled or tested). lease_pool.rs has a process-wide set_org_render_cap registry (soft cap 4096 orgs, floor 1) that each organization's semaphore resizes to on acquire: add_permits to grow, forget_permits to shrink, taking only idle permits so nothing held is lost; the override is clamped to the pool's own size and ignored on a pool without a per-org cap. limits.rs adds apply_org_render_permits (async, reads the resolver) and apply_org_render_permits_from (sync); both return at once until main records the boot switch and pool size, so the plan is never read while QUARRY_EDGE__POOL_ORG_MAX_PERMITS is 0. Wired into scrape (browser plans, after the cache hits), crawl and batch handoff, and start_run. rustfmt --check on the five files reports no hunks on the lines added; the files keep their HEAD hunk counts. | - Deviations: Two helpers instead of one. apply_org_render_permits(state_limits, org_id) matches the spec and is used only at the scrape site, which has no OrgLimits in hand yet. apply_org_render_permits_from(&OrgLimits, org_id) is used at crawl handoff, batch handoff and start_run, because those sites already read the limits; calling the async helper there would read billing-core a second time per request, and under an outage each read costs the full timeout.; Boot record location. The OnceLock (RENDER_PERMITS_BOOT in limits.rs) is set from main.rs where each browser pool is built (managed pool size 4 and local pool size 2), not at ~304, because the pool size is only known at pool creation. Exactly one browser pool is built per process on the scrape path (the local pool is registered only when no browser driver is registered), so the single set call is the one that takes effect. The OnceLock keeps first-set-wins per the spec.; quarry-edge compiles limits.rs and routes.rs twice: once in the lib (src/lib.rs, pub mod) and once in the bin (src/main.rs). main.rs sets only the bin copy. A test that wants the cap on must call quarry_edge::limits::set_render_permits_boot on the lib copy and drive the lib routes.; The override is clamped to the pool's own max_concurrent when applied, to keep the documented rule that an organization never gets more than the pool has. The boot cap with no override is not clamped, so behaviour without a plan override is unchanged.; Soft cap behaviour. The spec did not say what happens at 4096 organizations. Recording a new organization then evicts one arbitrary existing entry. That organization falls back to the boot cap on its next acquire until its next request re-applies its plan.; An unreadable plan answer (only fail mode closed produces one) is taken as the floor (OrgLimits::floor), not as the pool size. Fail-open (origin open) still yields the pool size through org_render_permits.; The browser-capable test is plan.driver == DriverKind::Browser, checked after the page-cache and result-cache early returns, so a cache hit never reads limits.; Not wired, because the spec names only scrape, crawl/batch handoff and start_run: scrape_stream (routes.rs) and internal_run_page. Browser pages run on those paths do not take the organization's render share.; A shrink (plan cap lowered) takes effect as held permits come back: the next acquire for that organization forgets the idle ones. It never cuts below the permits held. |
| QF-15-otp-approval-event-fields | followups.md QB01 Q3-E3.1-i2 (approval event must carry topOrigin/frameOrigin/mailProviderKey/mailAccountLabel); MP-ASKS W3-E3.1-i | built | I added four fields to OrchestrationEvent.BrowserActionApprovalRequired: top_origin=10, frame_origin=11, mail_provider_key=12 and mail_account_label=13. Each has a doc comment, and all four are empty on every gate that is not a one-time-code gate. In execution-core, RiskyActionDetail gains `otp: Option<OtpApprovalContext>`. OtpApprovalContext is re-exported from browser_agent, and its hand-written Debug never prints the label. Both publishes in require_approval fill the four fields through a new `otp_field` helper, which gives empty strings when `otp` is None, and every existing constructor sets `otp: None`. The model-gateway SSE payload mapper adds the four snake_case keys only when they are non-empty. The mp-orchestration OrchestrationEvent variant and proto_shim now carry the fields both ways, so the proto round trip loses nothing. In the SPA, run-console-client.ts and verevon-ui-events.ts map the keys to topOrigin, frameOrigin, mailProviderKey and mailAccountLabel: trimmed, dropped if empty or over 256 characters, and a label containing '@' is dropped. browser-session.ts copies them onto BrowserApprovalEntry, so BrowserApprovalGate and the card receive them. Nothing was compiled or run (CODE ONLY). | - Deviations: The spec did not mention the mp-orchestration crate, but it builds struct literals of the prost BrowserActionApprovalRequired and destructures the variant fully. I added the four fields to OrchestrationEvent::BrowserActionApprovalRequired in run_event.rs (serde default, skipped when empty), mapped them both ways in proto_shim.rs, and set empty strings in its round-trip test.; The spec did not mention browser-session.ts either. BrowserApprovalEntry did not carry the OTP fields, so they could not reach BrowserApprovalGate or the card. I added four optional fields to BrowserApprovalEntry and copy them in withBrowserApprovalRequested only when present. I also updated the stale comment in approval-model.ts that said the event does not send them yet.; The two RiskyActionDetail constructors that needed `otp: None` are in browser_agent.rs (the cookie-use gate and gate_risky_action). browser_risk.rs has none. The model-gateway sse.rs test literal got `..Default::default()`.; The SPA field cleaning is a small local helper in each of the two parsers (otpGateField / otpGateFields). I did not add a shared module, so verevon-ui-events.ts still has no imports.; No Rust code builds an OTP gate yet (no OTP RiskCategory exists), so every gate today publishes empty fields. The plumbing is ready for the OTP gate to set `otp: Some(..)`.; I did not regenerate any bindings (spec step 'Regenerate Rust/Go bindings'), per the task rules; the lead regenerates them. Rust picks the proto up through build.rs. |
| Q2-WS16.S6b | Plan 3.2 WS16.S6 budgets (max_output_tokens per format, ceiling 8192, _REPAIR, max_cost_usd) and WS16.S1 gateway fields (temperature, max_tokens, purpose) | built | AiChatRequest now has three optional fields, `max_tokens: Option<u32>`, `temperature: Option<f32>` and `purpose: Option<&'static str>`. Each is left out of the body when unset, the Debug output shows them, and a new constant AI_CHAT_PURPOSE_QUARRY_EXTRACT = "quarry.extract" holds the purpose. All five engine calls now send a per-format output cap (json 2048, summary 1024, question 512, highlights 1024, repair 2048), clamped by AiScrapeConfig::output_cap to the configured ceiling (`max_output_tokens`, 64..=8192, default 8192), together with a temperature and purpose quarry.extract. AiScrapeConfig also gained `model_repair` (default true; when off, an invalid json output is returned as SCHEMA_INVALID after the local repair, with no model repair call) and `max_cost_usd` (Option, default None). The engine adds each response's cost_usd to a per-request sum. Once that sum reaches the ceiling, the next call is skipped as BUDGET_EXHAUSTED with dimension llm_cost. To carry that, AiFormatReport has a new closed-enum field `dimension: Option<AiBudgetDimension>` (only value: llm_cost), and openapi.yaml documents it. The edge reads QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS, QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_REPAIR (default on; only 0/off/false turn it off) and QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_MAX_COST_USD (a negative, non-finite or unreadable value means no ceiling) into AiFormatsSettings, which passes them into the engine config. No content is logged. | - Deviations: Temperature: 0.0 for json, repair, question and highlights; 0.2 only for summary. Question and highlights reply in JSON with verbatim quotes, and the plan's MP extract design uses temperature 0.0 for those, so I treated only the summary as prose.; 'Add cost_usd to the tally' is a per-request running sum in the engine's State, not a per-format Tally field. AiTotal (on the wire) does not get a cost field.; The spec asked for a 'skipped BUDGET_EXHAUSTED dimension llm_cost', but AiFormatReport had no field for that. I added `dimension: Option<AiBudgetDimension>` (closed enum, snake_case, skipped when None) to quarry_core::ai_spec::AiFormatReport and a `dimension` field to the private Failure::Skipped. The call and token caps still leave dimension unset, so existing report output does not change.; The three new env vars are read on every boot, like the other AI_FORMATS_* knobs, not only when QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS is on. They only take effect on the engine, which runs only when the switch is on, and reading them unconditionally keeps the existing 'reads exactly ENV_NAMES' test working (ENV_NAMES grew from 9 to 12).; AiFormatsSettings no longer derives Eq, only PartialEq, because it now holds an Option<f64>. Nothing used Eq.; The two test-double gateway field lists (GATEWAY_FIELDS in quarry-runtime tests/ai_scrape.rs and quarry-edge tests/support/ai_model_double.rs) now also accept temperature, max_tokens and purpose. Without that, the doubles would reject the engine's new request bodies with 400 'unknown field' and the existing engine tests would fail. |
| Q2-WS16.S6c | Plan 3.2 WS16.S6 metering: quarry.extract.llm, QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_LLM_METRICS=off/shadow/on | built | Added the metric `usage_metrics::EXTRACT_LLM = "quarry.extract.llm"` and a separate tri-state `LlmMetricsMode {Off, Shadow, On}` in quarry-runtime usage.rs. It parses the same way as BrowserMetricsMode, is re-exported from lib.rs, and adds a new default trait method `UsageMeter::llm_metrics_mode()` that returns Off. In quarry-edge usage_attrs.rs: - `ENV_LLM_METRICS` and `llm_metrics_mode_from_env()` read the switch; a bad value falls back to off and logs once. - `ShadowMeter` gains an `llm_mode` field (set with the builder `with_llm_metrics`) and gates `quarry.extract.llm` on its own: off drops it, shadow increments `quarry_usage_shadow_events_total{metric}`, on forwards it. - A new helper, `emit_llm_formats`, emits one keyed event per AI format whose status is ok. Metadata is the closed set kind, repair, zdr, input_tokens and output_tokens. Nothing is emitted under ZDR or while the mode is off. main.rs wires the switch into the gate. `run_ai_formats` (scrape) now takes the page URL and the server-minted run id and meters after the engine runs. `/v1/extract` meters each `ok` source after the 429 and 501 short-circuits, using one minted id per request. BILLING.md has a new taxonomy row and section, and docker-compose.yml passes the switch through with `${QUARRY_EDGE__USAGE_LLM_METRICS:-off}`. | - Deviations: Event id is `quarry:<usage_id>:<url_hash16>:<kind>:quarry.extract.llm`, not `quarry:<request_id>:<url_hash16>:<kind>`. The existing `UsageEvent::keyed` helper always appends the metric and checks the id against billing-core's rule; crawl.page ids already have that shape.; The id uses a server-minted id, not the request_id. For scrape that is the run id the scrape.page event already uses; for /v1/extract it is one RunKind minted per request. request_id can be the caller's adopted x-request-id, which D2-033 forbids as a billing key, because a client could repeat it to suppress billing.; `run_ai_formats` gained two parameters (`url`, `usage_id`) and an allow(clippy::too_many_arguments). Its only call site, in routes.rs, was updated.; On /v1/extract the events are emitted once per `ok` item after the 429 and 501 short-circuits, next to the existing EXTRACT_URL emission, rather than inside `extract_target`. A request that ends in a 429 or the lane-refused 501 bills no LLM events, which matches the existing 'failures are free' rule.; In shadow mode the counter keeps the existing single `metric` label. I did not add a `kind` label, to avoid mixing label sets on the same counter name. |
| Q2-WS7.K-b | Plan 3.2 WS7.K (D7 S10); MP-ASKS.md 'W2-Q-WS7K' bullets authctx / policy.go / Grant.WebPolicy | built | The broker now reads the `web_policy` claim from the verified token. The claim is parsed into `authctx.Principal.WebPolicy` (a `json.RawMessage`, optional on every audience, with JSON null treated as no claim). It is deep-copied in `withPrincipal` and in `PrincipalFromContext`. In grant/policy.go, `ClaimPolicyResolver.Snapshot` builds a `WebPolicySnapshot` from that claim. It returns nil when there is no claim, and returns `ErrWebPolicyUnreadable` when v is not 1, rev is not "0" or 1-12 lowercase hex, the JSON is malformed, or an entry is empty or too long. `Narrow` refuses a requested host that falls under a deny rule (`ErrDomainDenied`, which wraps `ErrDomainNotGrantable`), intersects the request with the allow list on a dot boundary, and returns a canonical list. `Grant.WebPolicy` is stored by `CreateSession`, copied unchanged from the parent by `Derive` and by sensitive approvals, and deep-copied in `clone`. In the server, `AcquireGrant` runs `Snapshot` and `Narrow` before `CheckSession` when `BROWSER_BROKER_WEB_POLICY_SNAPSHOT` is on. An unreadable claim is refused as InvalidArgument `web_policy_unreadable`. `AcquireGrant`, `ValidateGrant` and `DeriveRunGrant` set field 20 from the stored snapshot. The switch is parsed strictly with `boolEnv`, defaults to off, and is passed through compose next to the origin-extension switch with default 0. While it is off, nothing is narrowed and field 20 is never set. | - Deviations: Spec says Narrow 'drops DomainsDeny hosts, and returns ErrDomainDenied when a requested host is denied'; implemented as refuse (ErrDomainDenied) when a requested host is under a deny rule, matching MP-ASKS 'refuses a requested host the policy denies'. An allow-list host strictly below a requested host replaces it (true subtree intersection), and an allowed host under a deny rule is never added. An empty result is ErrDomainDenied.; Deny/allow rule entries are matched after stripping a leading '*.' or '.', on a dot boundary. The snapshot keeps the entries exactly as signed, so the edge sees the claim 1:1.; Flag registry: BROWSER_BROKER_WEB_POLICY_SNAPSHOT was NOT added to apps/Control Plane/flag-registry/flag-registry.json. registry.go refuses an 'unregistered' name that does not follow the naming convention (*_ENABLED/_MODE/_CONFIRMED/_SAMPLE_RATE/_ALLOW_*), and catalogue_test only requires convention-named compose switches, so adding it would break the registry load. This matches the spec's 'only if the registry test requires it'.; Snapshot and narrowing apply on the ordinary AcquireGrant path: session, assistant-task and sensitive approval. The human_browse, origin_extension and api_key paths do not apply it. A sensitive approval stores its parent's snapshot, not the token's. Telemetry: GrantsDeniedTotal gets reason web_policy_unreadable or web_policy. |
| Q3-E3.1-b1-c | Plan 3.3 E3.1 Scope (allowedSensitiveActions + scope switch in grant.go) and MP-ASKS.md 'W3-E3.1-ab' (lines 152-164); OTP_HANDOFF.md blocker B3 (approval grant  | built | browser.proto now has `mail_connection_id = 23` and `grant_origin = 24` on AcquireGrantRequest, AcquireGrantResponse and ValidateGrantResponse, each with a doc comment (I checked both tags were free). In the broker, "fill_otp" is an allowed sensitive action. NormalizeSensitiveScopes takes it in the same shape as download_ref: exactly one frame, no dialog, no artifact. Grant and ApprovalParams now carry MailConnectionID and GrantOrigin. A new grant/fill_otp.go holds the checks. For fill_otp both fields are required: the origin must be a canonical https origin and the mail connection id must match ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}$. Both fields are refused on any other action. A ZDR parent or a ZDR request is refused, and the approval keeps the existing parent check and TTL cap. AcquireGrant passes the two fields through, and acquireResponse and ValidateGrant copy them back. It returns InvalidArgument 'fill_otp approvals are not enabled' unless BROWSER_BROKER_FILL_OTP_ENABLED is on (default off), and refuses the binding fields on any other grant kind. The values are never logged; logGrantIssued is unchanged and logs ids only. | - Deviations: Did not regenerate apps/Model Plane/go/gen/model_plane/v1/browser.pb.go with buf, although the spec says to: the harness rules say the lead regenerates bindings. server.go and human_browse.go already call GetMailConnectionId/GetGrantOrigin and set MailConnectionId/GrantOrigin, so the broker does not compile until the Go bindings are regenerated.; PgStore.CreateScopedSensitiveApproval (the durable store, not named in the spec) refuses fill_otp with the new ErrFillOtpNotDurable (FailedPrecondition). It has no columns for the binding, so a validated grant would come back without the origin and mail connection; the store already refuses read_only for the same reason. It also refuses binding fields on any other action. Because ZDR is refused as well, only the in-memory store can issue a fill_otp approval today. A follow-up migration that adds mail_connection_id and grant_origin columns is needed before this works on a Postgres-backed broker.; Added a check the spec does not ask for: the grant_origin host must equal or sit below a host of the parent grant (ErrDeriveWidensParent), so an approval cannot point outside the run's fence. A canonical origin also rejects uppercase, user info, path, query, fragment, an IP host, a single-label host and an explicit :443.; The switch uses the existing switchOn reader (like EnvOriginExtensionEnabled), as the spec says, so 'on' is also accepted besides 1/true.; New error mappings: ErrFillOtpBindingInvalid and ErrFillOtpBindingUnexpected map to InvalidArgument; ErrFillOtpZdrRefused and ErrFillOtpNotDurable map to FailedPrecondition. Telemetry outcome labels: fill_otp_binding_invalid, fill_otp_zdr_refused, fill_otp_not_durable.; Compose and registry: BROWSER_BROKER_FILL_OTP_ENABLED is in deploy/docker-compose.yml (default 0). In flag-registry.json it is added to the 'unregistered' list, next to the other BROWSER_BROKER_* switches, following their pattern.; I did not write the grant_test.go cases the dedupe note asks for, because the CODE ONLY rule forbids writing tests. They should cover the NormalizeSensitiveScopes shape, origin/id validation, refusing the binding on other actions, ZDR refusal and the parent-host check. |

Zero or many oneOf matches report one OneOfMismatch { path, matched } (code one_of_mismatch), and the codes match the Model Plane's shipped names (const_mismatch, any_of_no_match, one_of_mismatch). Each subschema is validated into a scratch list so its issues never leak, and issue order is fixed (type, enum, const, anyOf, oneOf, then kind checks), mirroring apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/schema_check.rs. The header documents that pattern, format and $ref are not validated, and three arms were added to schema_issue in quarry-runtime/src/ai_scrape.rs because it is an exhaustive match on ValidationIssue. Nothing was compiled or tested (CODE ONLY); rustfmt --check passes for json_schema.rs, and ai_scrape.rs was already not rustfmt-clean before this change, with none of its hunks touching the new arms. | - Deviations: Naming: the spec body names the variant OneOfMatches { path, count } with code one_of_matches; I used OneOfMismatch { path, matched } with code one_of_mismatch, following the LEAD NOTE and the Model Plane's shipped codes for parity. One variant covers zero and many matches.; $ref: the spec says $ref is inlined earlier by the AI spec normaliser. In this tree ai_spec.rs refuses $ref (ALLOWED_KEYWORDS, and the refusal test in ai_spec.rs) and does not inline it; inlining is the S4b unit. The header therefore says the normaliser refuses pattern, format and $ref today.; Depth bound: the spec says to respect the existing depth bound and to keep MAX_SCHEMA_ISSUES 20. json_schema.rs has no depth bound and no MAX_SCHEMA_ISSUES; the 20-issue cap is MAX_SCHEMA_ISSUES in ai_spec.rs, applied in ai_scrape.rs and unchanged. I added no validator depth bound, because none existed and the Model Plane port has none either. Recursion follows the schema's own nesting, and the normaliser's MAX_SCHEMA_DEPTH (10) caps depth for the AI formats.; Out-of-list file: quarry-runtime/src/ai_scrape.rs was edited (three arms in schema_issue, codes const_mismatch, any_of_no_match, one_of_mismatch). It is the only match on ValidationIssue outside json_schema.rs, so the crate would not compile without them. Another writer has concurrent unrelated edits in that file; I did not touch them.; Spec claim not fully true: quarry_runtime::StructuredExtractClient (pub, exported from quarry-runtime lib.rs) validates the caller's structured_output_schema directly with json_schema::validate, without the AI spec normaliser, and is not behind QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS. | Q2-WS15.L5c | Plan 3.2 WS15.L5 (~2790): knowledge-client getCrawlStatus/cancel/pause/resume/errors, isTerminalCrawlStatus timed_out/skipped, streamCrawlRunEvents follow=true  | built | The SPA side of the crawl controls is in the tree, dark by default. knowledge-client.ts gains getCrawlStatus, getCrawlErrors (with afterSeq as well as limit), pauseCrawl, resumeCrawl and cancelCrawl (DELETE), all with orgHeaders and typed results. streamCrawlRunEvents now asks for follow=true, keeps the cursor across reconnects, resumes on the edge's reconnect frame, retries a dropped stream once (the budget refills after progress), and falls back to the drain when the edge refuses the live tail with a 403 (its ZDR refusal). isTerminalCrawlStatus learns timed_out and skipped. CrawlControls.tsx (new) draws Pause, Resume and a confirmed Cancel by phase, shows "Job control is not enabled on this installation" on a 501, and is hidden unless getScheduleCapabilities().jobControl. KnowledgeComposer mounts it on running crawl rows, re-reads status on reload via getCrawlStatus, adds timed_out/skipped to TERMINAL, and keys job rows by job key and id. Nothing was compiled or tested, per the CODE ONLY rule; syntax was checked by reading and bracket counts. | BLOCKER for visibility, outside this unit's file list: the gateway never reports jobControl. GET /api/ingestions/schedules/capabilities (schedule_capabilities in apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/ingestions/schedules.rs) answers only trigger, backfill, overlap, kinds and needsActivation, and ScheduleCapabilities (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/config.rs, about line 625) has no such field. So CrawlControls stays hidden everywhere until the gateway change lands (open gap W2-FE-L5b in .plan-work/quarry-w1w2/W23-depth-lane-d.md). Fix: add job_control: bool (serde default) to ScheduleCapabilities, add "jobControl" to the answer, and add the field to the struct literals at schedules.rs about lines 416, 428 and 486 (plus any others). config.rs already has another writer's uncommitted changes, so it needs a coordinated edit.; The errors link is not wired. getCrawlErrors is exported and typed, but no UI reads it, and the unit spec does not ask for a UI.; No CSS was added. The controls reuse the global classes ingestions-actions-row, dashboard-knowledge-composer__job-detail and dashboard-knowledge-composer__error, so the layout is unverified.; The job's server phase is not stored on the composer row. After a reload during pausing or cancelling, the buttons show the stored status (Pause) until the next status change.; Not verified by a build or test run. Existing knowledge-client.test.ts (four streamCrawlRunEvents cases) and the KnowledgePage crawl-stream test were read by hand and should still pass, because the mocked frames end with done or a terminal event and the URL assertions are substring matches. Deviations: Added: a 403 on the follow=true request (the edge's ZDR refusal, which the gateway relays as 403 model_error) falls back to the drain once, so ZDR callers keep the redacted drain instead of a stream error.; Reconnect rules are my reading of the spec. One reconnect per dropped stream, with the budget refilled by an attempt that delivered events. A reconnect frame resumes without spending the budget, an in-stream error frame counts as a drop, and a stream that ends without done or a terminal event also counts as a drop.; Added: the cursor comes from the SSE id, or from the envelope seq for id-less legacy drain frames, so a resumed drain does not repeat events.; Added: snapshot and control_requested frames are not forwarded as progress, and a frame named done counts as the end marker.; Added to mapEventTypeToStatus, mirroring the edge's jobStatusForEvent: run_timed_out to timed_out, run_paused to paused, run_resumed to running, schedule_skipped to skipped. This is what makes the phase change show live.; Added the optional afterSeq to getCrawlErrors so it can page with next_seq.; KnowledgeComposer job rows are now keyed by job key and id, and IngestJobRow takes rowKey, readJob, orgId and onStatus instead of job. This keeps a pending cancel confirmation alive across per-event updates, and stops one job's state reaching another job that shares a key.; Added row display: a Pause icon and a Paused detail for paused jobs, and timed_out and skipped labels with the error icon. The failed regex now includes timed_out and skipped.; The capability is cached only when positive, so a failed read (which getScheduleCapabilities turns into all-off) is asked again on the next card mount.; localStorage tolerance of new status strings needed no change: isPersistedJob already accepts any string status. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q2-WS7.K-d**: snapshot follows the claim mode (no own flag), per W23-W2-Q-WS7K assumed decisions
- **Q2-WS8.F**: ADR-3/X-13 at plan defaults (ledger 2026-11-01 WS8.A entry)
- **Q2-WS8.I-a**: ADR-3/X-13 plan defaults
- **Q4-E4.2-3h**: fresh grant per run: the recipe run is started like any browser run with its own broker grant; no stored approval is ever reused
- **Q4-E4.2-3i**: user-initiated start; the Model does not choose recipes in v1
- **Q2-WS9.H-entry**: D-WS9H-1: entry point lives in the empty-chat state, same button component
- **QF-8-bff-turn-browser-context**: Stateless HMAC attach token (no server storage) chosen over an in-memory region cache, because the attach route 'stores nothing' and the gateway may run more than one replica
- **QF-1-batch-hash**: Fold webhook and max_parallel into the hash only when present (followup's suggested fix), so existing hashes are unchanged
- **Q2-WS6.G**: X-12 placeholder numbers; plan render_permits replace the operator floor (W2-CP-L2)
- **QF-15-otp-approval-event-fields**: Field names as MP-ASKS W3-E3.1-i proposed (topOrigin, frameOrigin, mailProviderKey, mailAccountLabel)
- **Q2-WS7.K-b**: ADR-1 outcome C; snapshot follows the verified user claim only, no request field (MP-ASKS W2-Q-WS7K)
- **Q3-E3.1-b1-c**: ADR-2a ratified 2026-10-23 (single-use fresh approval per OTP); fill_otp under a ZDR parent is refused (conservative, dark); field numbers 23/24
- **Q1-WS2.A-mp-evaluate**: ADR-6 ratified (Evaluate stays denied); R-12 recommendation: remove the variant
- **QF-3-office-flag-parser**: Strict parser (on/true/1) wins: this route sends document bytes to another service, so an unrecognised value stays off (fail closed)
- **Q1-WS4.G-suggest-zdr**: D1-072 has no recommended option in the docs; taken at the stricter choice the hard ZDR rule requires (degrade to empty under ZDR rather than forward without posture), matching the INNH-8 /v1/answer precedent
- **Q2-WS14.S7-spa**: OQ-11 grantor: platform operators only; visible only with effective robots_max ignore plus the web.robots_override entitlement

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q2-WS7.K-d**: Q2-WS7.K-d built (code only, not compiled). The WS7.K step layers now bind. Gate 8 in admit_action calls gate_policy_for_step with the run grant snapshot that validate_run_grant now returns and the caller's step_claim, which comes in through the new AdmitInput.caller field. Admitted carries both layers, so the landed-page check (check_landed_page, which now takes a layers parameter) applies the same layers as gate 8. new_tab and the attach-create start-URL and borrowed-tab checks also use them, with the attach grant's snapshot for attach. The delegate landed check uses the attach grant snapshot from that step's revalidation, and the dark attach-mode tab consent uses the run grant snapshot with no claim layer. There is no new flag: the layers bind under QUARRY_EDGE__WEB_POLICY_CLAIM_ENFORCEMENT bounded by QUARRY_EDGE__WEB_POLICY_ENFORCEMENT. Test helpers in agent_routes/tests.rs were updated with caller: None.
- **Q2-WS8.F**: Q2-WS8.F (quarry-w45): the owner browser-session projection (GET /v1/agent/runs/{run_id}/browser-session) now advertises tabControl (= QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH), attachAvailable (switch on, not ZDR, run holds a grant, driver has background_tabs, tab_pinning and per_tab_egress, no live attachment), and attach {state, attachId, tabId, tabEpoch, grantId} while an attachment is live. grantId is owner-internal. Each tab carries mark (deliverable or handoff) and attachId. The tab ledger keeps the mark kind in a marks map that survives the handoff and is dropped on forget or retain_live. Dark by default; no new switch. Before the switch goes on, the verevonv3 gateway spa_owner_projection must also strip attach.grantId. RUNBOOKS.md Not done (Agent attach section) should drop the projection fields.
- **Q2-WS8.I-a**: Q2-WS8.I-a (gateway half) is built in verevonv3 `apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs` and is dark behind BROWSER_AI_RUN_ATTACH_SESSION. - **Q4-062b-bff-c**: Q4-062b-bff-c built (not compiled). The verevonv3 gateway now relays GET /api/v1/chat/research-runs/{id} and POST /api/v1/chat/research-runs/{id}/cancel to model-gateway /v1/research/runs/:id[/cancel], using the user's model and session bearers.
