- **QF-8-bff-turn-browser-context**: QF-8 built (gateway only, dark). The chat stream route now always drops the client's browser_tab_refs/browser_regions/browser_context_verified. With BROWSER_TURN_CONTEXT=true it does three things. Tab refs (max 8, one session) are re-resolved through resolve_turn_tab_mentions, so they pass the same owner gate, ZDR refusal and fail-closed matching as the tab-mentions route. Regions (max 4) must carry the stateless HMAC attach token, `<exp>.<b64url mac>` over v1/user/org/session/tab/rect/sha256(image)/exp with a 600 s TTL, keyed by BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH_KEY, which the region attach answer now issues as attachToken/attachTokenExpiresAt. A ZDR turn that carries either field is refused with 400 browser_context_refused_zdr before any upstream call. Nothing is stored. Follow-ups: (1) wiremock tests (forged ref dropped when off, ZDR refusal, bad token refusal). (2) The SPA chat-client must send browser_regions with attach_token and keep the image bytes unchanged; the BrowserRegionAttachment type should gain attachToken. (3) model-gateway must read browser_regions / browser_context_verified. (4) Deploy env needs BROWSER_TURN_CONTEXT and BROWSER_REGION_ATTACH_KEY, which have not been added to compose/env examples. (5) Selection text is not forwarded on the turn because the token does not cover it.
- **QF-9-spa-region-send**: QF-9 built (dark behind VITE_BROWSER_REGION_SEND). The SPA now keeps the gateway's region attach token (browser-client.ts validates it: 16-512 characters of [A-Za-z0-9_.-]). Each composer region carries its token, image and selection. A chat turn sends `browser_regions` (at most 4, snake_case, only regions that have a token) beside `browser_tab_refs` under the same guard: never ZDR, subscription, support read-only or conversation-only. The controller refuses region sends while the switch is off, in a temporary or ZDR chat, while streaming, with more than 4 regions, or when a region has no token, and the draft is kept in every case. The gateway codes browser_region_unverified and browser_context_refused_zdr have nb/en wording in streamFailurePresentation, and the typed text comes back through the failed-send draft settle. Regions are cleared from the composer after a submit the controller accepted. A later gateway refusal therefore needs the area attached again, which is correct for an expired or unverifiable token. Nothing compiled or tested yet; no regeneration needed.
- **QF-1-batch-hash**: QF-1: under QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_IDEMPOTENCY the batch Idempotency-Key payload hash now also covers webhook and max_parallel (either spelling) when the caller sends them, so a same-key retry that changes only those answers 409 CONFLICT instead of re-attaching. Keys are left out of the hash when absent, so every existing hash is unchanged, and a pinned literal digest in the handoff.rs unit test guards that. No new switch, no migration, no proto change. ZDR is unchanged because batch is refused under ZDR before the hash is taken. Docs updated: openapi.yaml 409 and Idempotency-Key text, and the CONTRACTS.md E3.7.1 flag-on bullet.
- **Q2-WS16.S5a**: Q2-WS16.S5a built: quarry-runtime/src/harvest_map.rs (pure, no I/O, no logging) provides render_harvest_block, prefill, merge, Prefill and PrefillProvenance with PrefillSource and PrefillRule enums, and lib.rs gains `pub mod harvest_map;`. There is no switch, call site, route, proto, migration or generated artifact, and nothing is persisted or forwarded here, so ZDR is enforced at the model call in S5b. Risks carried to S5b: prefill checks type and enum only, so a prefilled value that breaks minLength, maxLength, minimum, maximum, pattern or format fails the caller's full-schema validation. Microdata flattening can put a nested item's property at depth 1, so a single nested value (a review author, for example) can prefill a top-level property. Hydration keys are seen only for retained blobs (object top level, 8 KB or less each, at most 8), and framework bookkeeping keys can match schema names, so the corpus precision test gates the prefill flip.

- **Q2-WS7.K-b**: Q2-WS7.K-b built (code only, not compiled). The browser broker reads the verified `web_policy` claim (authctx.Principal.WebPolicy) behind BROWSER_BROKER_WEB_POLICY_SNAPSHOT (strict boolEnv, default 0, in the Model Plane compose; not in flag-registry because the name does not follow the convention the registry requires). When on, AcquireGrant builds a grant.WebPolicySnapshot through ClaimPolicyResolver.Snapshot. An unreadable claim (v not 1, bad rev, malformed or unbounded) is refused as InvalidArgument web_policy_unreadable. A token without the claim mints with no snapshot. Requested hosts are narrowed by grant.Narrow: a deny hit is refused with ErrDomainDenied (wraps ErrDomainNotGrantable, so InvalidArgument), and the allow list is intersected on a dot boundary. The snapshot is stored on Grant.WebPolicy, copied unchanged into derived run grants and approvals (tighten-only), and returned as field 20 by AcquireGrant, ValidateGrant and DeriveRunGrant. Known gap: the Postgres store does not persist the snapshot yet (Q2-WS7.K-c), so with the switch on a non-ZDR grant comes back from ValidateGrant/Derive without field 20. ZDR grants live in memory and keep it. Tests still to write: TestSnapshotComesFromVerifiedPrincipalNotRequest, TestRunGrantSnapshotOnlyTightens, TestDeniedDomainIsNotGrantable, TestAllowListIntersectsRequestedDomains, TestPolicyAbsentYieldsNoSnapshot.

It holds `ModelTraceConfig` (url, optional user/password, subject_prefix "mp.v1.trace.run", queue_cap 2048, ack_window 32, with a Debug that hides the password) and the `#[async_trait] TraceTransport` trait (`publish(subject, msg_id, bytes)` plus a default `flush`). It also holds `NatsTraceTransport` (JetStream `publish_with_headers` with a `Nats-Msg-Id` header set to the bare event ULID, at most ack_window PubAck futures in flight, client name quarry-trace-producer) and `ModelTracePublisher`. The publisher has a bounded mpsc queue, ONE publisher task, and dropped/published/failed counters. Its `try_enqueue(&Event, &PublishCtx)` never waits: it refuses a ZDR run, an invalid model_run_id or an event that `to_model_envelope` maps to None, and when the queue is full it drops the event and counts it. Its `shutdown(deadline)` flushes for at most 2 s, then aborts. In events.rs, `EventSink` gets a `model_trace` field and `with_model_trace(Arc<ModelTracePublisher>)`; the durable `emit` path hands each event of a labelled run with model_run_id set and ZDR off to `try_enqueue`. `emit_ephemeral` and a ZDR `emit_for_zdr` never do. lib.rs re-exports the new types, and zdr_matrix.rs ROWS gains `browser_trace_publish` (Refused, FORBIDDEN) and `browser_trace_replay` (Inert, in-memory ring only). | - Deviations: The enqueue happens inside `EventSink::mint`, still holding the run's trace lock, instead of after `mint` returns in `emit`. Doing it after the lock is released would let two concurrent emits on one run reach the queue out of seq order. `mint` (a private function) gained a `publish_trace: bool` parameter, set true only from `emit_inner`, which is the non-ZDR emit / emit_for_zdr path. All three call sites are updated. The hand-off is a non-blocking try_send, so the broker is never awaited under the lock.; `ModelTraceConfig.user`/`password` are `Option<String>` (anonymous NATS stays possible), and the publisher also has a `failed` counter next to `dropped`/`published`. Publish errors are counted and logged without any payload. A failed PubAck for an earlier message is logged by the transport rather than reported against a later one.; Added a `TraceRefusal` enum (Zdr, NoModelRun, Unmapped, QueueFull, Closed) as the error type of `try_enqueue`. `ModelTracePublisher::start(config, Arc<dyn TraceTransport>)` and `connect(config)` are the constructors. The switch reader and boot wiring (QUARRY_EDGE__MODEL_TRACE_NATS_URL) are left to Q2-WS10.S5b, as the spec says.; `PublishCtx` was already re-exported from lib.rs (QB10), so it is not added a second time. |
| Q4-E4.2-4c-c | Plan 3.4 E4.2 Scope ('the runner is a deterministic planner mode ... proposes each stored step under a fresh grant'); inventory Q4-E4.2-3i (model-gateway accept | built | The Verevon gateway's POST /api/v1/browser/sessions/{session_id}/ai-runs now takes an optional `recipe {id, version?, tag?, slots}`. It is read through a new deny_unknown_fields `RecipeAsk` in browser.rs, which has a Debug impl that prints only the slot count. The gateway checks the recipe before any Quarry or Model Gateway call. It is refused with 400 browser_recipes_unavailable when AppState.browser_recipes is off. It is refused with 400 recipe_attach_unsupported when it comes with continueInTab, requireAttached or assistantInteractive, and with 400 recipe_plan_mode_unsupported when it comes with planMode. Its shape is checked against Model Gateway's bounds (id_invalid / version_and_tag / tag_invalid / slots_invalid). Recipe runs never get attach_session_id, even with the attach switch on. build_ai_run_request writes `recipe` only when one is present, so a plain start is byte-identical. Model Gateway's 400 `{error:"<code>"}` recipe refusals map to the gateway's own typed codes, and recipe_requires_user maps to 403 browser_recipe_forbidden. The browser_run.start action dispatcher and contract (browserRunStartInput gains a strict, refined `recipe` object) and startBrowserAiRun/StartBrowserAiRunParams pass the recipe through. The idempotency fingerprint includes the recipe only when one is present. | - Deviations: The spec has the gateway answer 400 `browser_recipe_unavailable` when the switch is off. I reused the existing Q4-E4.2-4a gateway code `browser_recipes_unavailable` instead, with status 400 here, as the lead note says to reuse 4a's codes and the SPA already has copy for it. Model Gateway's `browser_recipe_unavailable` is mapped to that same code.; The spec lists plan_mode under 400 recipe_attach_unsupported. A recipe with planMode is refused with 400 `recipe_plan_mode_unsupported` instead, matching Model Gateway's own code, which the lead note says to map. continueInTab, requireAttached and assistantInteractive get recipe_attach_unsupported.; Recipe runs are made unattached even when BROWSER_AI_RUN_ATTACH is on: the attach switch is masked for a recipe start, so no attach_session_id is sent and the attach-only ZDR and grant checks do not apply. | Q2-WS16.S4b | Plan 3.2 WS16.S4 ai_schema normalise (local $ref inlining, schema_recursive, schema_ref_not_supported, strip $schema/$id/$comment, ComplexityClass) + S6 flag QU | built | The json schema normaliser in ai_spec.rs now accepts anyOf, oneOf and const (checked by the S4a validator) and inlines local $ref to #/$defs/ or #/definitions/ before the keyword checks. Cycles give schema_recursive, other refs give schema_ref_not_supported, and the root's $defs/definitions are dropped. Depth, property and size limits and the root-object check run on the inlined schema, and JsonSpec carries a complexity class. AiScrapeConfig gains model_json_complex, which run_json uses for Complex schemas; the QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_MODEL_JSON_COMPLEX reader (default verevon-balance) is in ai_formats.rs, with the engine config, cache key and ENV_NAMES pin updated. ERROR_REASONS (CRLF kept), the openapi keyword note, CONTRACTS.md and SELF_HOST.md are updated. Nothing was compiled or run; rustfmt parses every changed Rust file, and the JsonSpec and AiScrapeConfig literals in extract_routes.rs and tests/ai_scrape.rs were updated for the new field. | Not edited, outside this unit's file list: quarry-core/src/json_schema.rs header (around line 34) still says the AI spec normaliser refuses $ref, pattern and format; it now inlines local $ref and refuses only pattern and format.; Not edited, outside this unit's file list: apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/ingestions/lib/errors.ts SCHEMA_CODES. Its schema_keyword_not_supported copy names $ref, anyOf and oneOf as unsupported, and it has no entries for schema_recursive or schema_ref_not_supported. Deviations: A local $ref that names no definition (for example #/$defs/Missing) is invalid_value at <path>.$ref. The spec names only schema_ref_not_supported, for refs that are not local definition refs.; schema_recursive, schema_ref_not_supported and the inlining schema_too_large are reported at <path>.$ref, the keyword's path, as schema_keyword_not_supported already is.; The 64 KiB check now applies only to the inlined schema, as the spec says, so the raw schema is no longer pre-checked. The walk stays linear in the raw size, and the HTTP body limit is the only cap on raw input.; Two bounds that are not in the spec: each inlined definition is charged its raw serialized size against MAX_SCHEMA_BYTES, cumulatively, so reference fan-out cannot blow up; and inlined definitions may nest at most MAX_SCHEMA_DEPTH deep (schema_too_complex). The charge can exceed the normalised size when a definition carries many $comment, $schema or $id keys.; anyOf and oneOf branches add one depth level, as the S4a validator's own header describes. An empty list is invalid_value and a non-list is invalid_type.; Beside a $ref only title and description are accepted; they overlay the inlined definition. Any other sibling keyword is schema_keyword_not_supported at its own path.; $defs and definitions are dropped only at the root. Nested ones are schema_keyword_not_supported, and a non-object root $defs is invalid_type.; A ref may point below a definition (for example #/$defs/A/properties/b). Percent-escapes and ~0/~1 in pointer tokens are decoded.; Complexity uses the root-is-1 depth numbering: Complex when any node is deeper than 3 levels, when there are more than 30 properties in all, or when any anyOf or oneOf is kept.; The root type check runs on the inlined root, so a root $ref to an object definition is accepted. |
| Q2-WS16.S5b | Plan 3.2 WS16.S5 pipeline InlineParts structured/json_ld + flag QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_HARVEST=off/context/prefill | built | Wired the page-markup harvest into the AI scrape engine behind QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_HARVEST (off/context/prefill, default context; dark because QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS is off by default). The pipeline returns ai_structured and ai_json_ld on request, never serialized. The engine appends a 4,000-char harvest block inside the untrusted page frame in context and prefill modes, keeps highlight and question verification on the page text only, and in prefill mode takes exact-name top-level json fields from the markup, sends only the remainder schema, skips the model call when nothing remains, and checks the merged result against the full schema, reporting ai.json.prefilled. The edge reads the switch once (unknown value, off with a fixed-label warn) and the scrape route sets want_harvest only when AI formats are requested, a model hop is allowed and redact_pii is not on. Nothing was compiled or tested (CODE ONLY); rustfmt parse checks pass on every touched Rust file. | - Deviations: want_harvest is also false when the request carries redact_pii on, and when a model hop is not allowed (ZDR or a privacy block). The spec's literal rule was AI specs present and mode not off. Reason: the route's redaction covers ai_source but not the values harvested from the markup, so a redacting request would send unredacted markup values to the model.; A blank QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_HARVEST counts as unset and takes the default context. Only a non-blank value that is not off, context or prefill turns the harvest off, with the warn. This matches the file's other blank-means-default env handling.; The wire types AiPrefill, AiPrefillSource and AiPrefillRule live in quarry-core ai_spec.rs, because quarry-core cannot depend on quarry-runtime's harvest_map types. The engine maps harvest_map's PrefillSource and PrefillRule onto them. The report field is prefilled: Vec<AiPrefill>, omitted when empty and set only on an ok json result.; Before the remainder check, prefilled keys are removed from the model's answer, since the markup's value replaces them anyway. This stops an additionalProperties:false schema failing on a key the model echoed. The model call is skipped only when the remainder has no property and no required name left.; The context block is added after the page is cut to max_input_chars, so a request may send up to 4,000 characters beyond the page cap, plus a short header.; /v1/extract (extract_routes.rs) passes harvest None. Its AiScrapeInput literal was updated so it compiles, but no harvest is wired there, because the spec scopes this unit to the scrape route.; AiScrapeConfig::default().harvest is Context, matching the env default. Without a harvest on the input the mode has no effect. |

The handler checks the run_id ([A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}) and forwards only three query parameters: `cursor` (at most 256 printable ASCII characters), `limit` (1..500) and `types` (a comma list of the seven BROWSER_SESSION_OPENED..BROWSER_ARTIFACT_RECORDED names, de-duplicated); a bad value gets a typed 400 (invalid_run_id, invalid_cursor, invalid_limit, invalid_trace_types). The handler turns the page into `{data:{runId,events,hasMore}, meta:{cursor}, links:{next?}}`, keeps only the 17 envelope keys on each event (payload and agent are an object or null, origin is a string or null), and sets `Cache-Control: no-store`. | Q2-WS8.J-a | Plan 3.2 WS8.J (D8 S8.9) 'same rule on Revoked, IdleReleased, SessionEnded'; tests named sweeper_releases_attach_with_lapsed_grant / sweeper_releases_idle_attac | built | The run sweeper now handles agent attachments on runs that are not expiring in the same pass. The new `sweep_attach` in attach.rs takes the run's entry lock without waiting. It skips the run if the entry is held, an action is in flight, or the run is closed. An attachment idle past `limits.attach_idle_ttl_s` (QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH_IDLE_TTL_S, default 180) ends IdleReleased. Otherwise its attach grant is checked again through `grant_validator.validate_for_org` and `attach_grant_refusal`. A refused grant ends the attachment Revoked; a broker outage keeps it. Ending goes through `finish_attach`, now pub(super): an unmarked agent tab is closed, a borrowed tab is returned, and agent.detached is sent via emit_for_zdr with no URL. `sweep_once_ttl` calls it for runs with a live attachment, honours the sweeper dry run (count and log only), and records the result in two new SweepReport fields, `attaches_released` and `attaches_would_release`, plus a new metric `quarry_agent_attach_released_total{reason=idle/revoked}`. In close.rs, `finish_run` now ends a live attachment as SessionEnded before it writes the durable run record. It runs only the event half of `finish_attach` (new `record_attach_end`: timeline event and agent.detached), because releasing the session closes every tab. The ended attachment's lane is still waited on before the session is released. | - Deviations: sweep_attach takes an extra `dry_run: bool` parameter beyond the spec's (state, handle, now_ms, ttl_s), so a dry run can return the phase it would end with without ending anything.; SweepReport gets two fields instead of one: `attaches_released` and `attaches_would_release` (the dry-run count, like expired/would_expire). Existing SweepReport struct literals in agent_routes/tests.rs and tests/run_sweeper_integration.rs now end with `..SweepReport::default()` so they still compile.; finish_run does not call `finish_attach` itself. That function closes tabs and restores egress through the driver, and a delegate action may still hold the attach lane. finish_run instead calls the new `record_attach_end` (the event path the spec names), and the session release closes the tabs. This runs before the durable lifecycle record so that the attach event's checkpoint save cannot reopen a checkpoint an explicit close has already closed. Side effect: if an explicit close then fails its timeline append, the run stays registered with its attachment already ended (ending delegate access early, which is the safe direction).; Handoff marks are now honoured for IdleReleased as well as Released. Before an idle release, the sweeper re-checks the attach grant, as release_attach does, and clears the mark if the grant is invalid or the broker cannot answer. Revoked, Aborted and SessionEnded still close the tab.; The sweep is not gated on QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH. No attachment can exist while the switch is off, so the sweep does nothing then. If the switch is turned off mid-run, the attachments already alive are still released, which is the safe direction.; If the run's parent grant_id is missing, the sweep treats the attachment's grant as refused (Revoked). |

The static Accept-Language is the TLS profile's fixed value (quarry-tls TlsProfile::accept_language; the override is never set), so no request locale reaches the static path. |

| Q2-WS15.S3b | Plan 3.2 WS15 ownership/ZDR check (2) 'schedule_requests ... covered by org erasure' and WS15.S3; SCHEDULES.md Known gaps 2 and 13 | built | Org erasure now purges schedule_requests. The Postgres PurgeOrg runs `DELETE FROM schedule_requests WHERE org_id = $1` inside the purge transaction, just before the schedules delete. The in-memory store purges its request rows through a new memScheduleRequests.purgeByOrg, which matches only the literal OrgID. PurgeResult has a new ScheduleRequestsDeleted field (json schedule_requests_deleted), which is added into Total() and written to the gdpr subscriber's "erasure data purged" log line. In the orchestrator, Manager.Backfill and backfillWith now take an overlap string. A new backfillOverlapPolicy helper maps an empty value to ALLOW_ALL (the old behaviour) and maps skip, allow and cancel with the existing overlapPolicy mapping. requests.go reads params.overlap_policy from the claimed row (trimmed and lower-cased) and passes it on, and the requestTemporal interface was changed to match. | - Deviations: Control only accepts and stores the overlap values skip, allow and cancel (validBackfillOverlap in resources/schedule_requests.go), not the skip/buffer_one/allow/cancel_other set the spec names. The mapping follows the real stored values through the existing overlapPolicy. It also accepts buffer_one (BUFFER_ONE) and cancel_other (CANCEL_OTHER) as aliases, so an edge value in that spelling would map correctly if control ever lets it through. Any other non-empty value falls to SKIP, as overlapPolicy already does.; Existing test code was updated so it still compiles (CODE ONLY exception (a)): fakeTemporal.Backfill in requests_test.go gets an extra ignored overlap parameter, and the m.backfillWith call in reaper_isolation_test.go now passes "".; reaper_isolation_test.go already failed gofmt -l before this change (aligned one-line fake methods). That formatting was left alone. |
| Q2-WS15.S4c | Plan 3.2 WS15.S4 row quarry-orchestrator schedules.go reconcileWith (RecentActions/NextActionTimes, Describe every 5 min, skipped_overlap rows) | built | Built the orchestrator runtime sync dark behind QUARRY_SCHEDULE_RUNTIME_SYNC (default off). New runtime.go in package schedules: the flag parser, syncRuntime, the five-minute Describe and PUT of last_run_at, next_run_at and skipped_overlap_count to control (signed with the control-internal secret), and one POST per new overlap skip (run_key sched:<id>:skip:<n>, reason skipped_overlap, at most 10 per pass across schedules). schedules.go gains Config.RuntimeSync, the lastRun and nextRun fields on existingSchedule filled in listExisting, the Manager runtime map, and the syncRuntime call at the end of reconcileWith. main.go reads the flag once into Config. Nothing was compiled or tested (code-only rule); gofmt -l is clean on all three files, and line endings are kept (LF). Known limits: a schedule deleted and recreated under the same id restarts its skip count, so new skip keys can collide with old ones and control returns the existing job, so those rows are absorbed silently while the count stays right; a disabled job schedule with a pending backlog gets 409 each pass until it is re-enabled; skips that happen while the orchestrator is down are counted but not written as rows; docs/SCHEDULES.md and docs/RUNBOOKS.md list QUARRY_SCHEDULE_REQUESTS but not the new flag, and no compose entry was added, matching the sibling switch. | - Deviations: syncRuntime takes two more parameters than the spec's signature: desired (map[string]ScheduleSpec) and desiredOrgs (map[string]struct{}). The org rule needs the desired set, and the sync is limited to desired schedules that are present in Temporal.; Skipped-run rows are POSTed only for schedules whose workflow is ScheduledJobWF (spec.go ScheduledJobWorkflow). Control's registerScheduledRun refuses a change_monitor kind with 409 because store.AdmissionKind accepts only scrape, crawl and batch. A monitor's overlap skips still update skipped_overlap_count in the runtime PUT but produce no row.; The skip-row body carries run_id, which the spec's body omitted. Control's registerRunBody requires a non-empty run_id of at most 128 characters, so each row gets a fresh run id from quarrycontracts.NewID(KindRun).; The 10-row cap is global per reconcile pass, across all schedules. Rows beyond the cap are not dropped: each schedule keeps a cursor (rowsPushed) and the backlog drains on later passes. A failed row stops that schedule's rows until the next pass.; The first skip count read per schedule after process start is the baseline. Skips before this process saw the schedule are counted in the runtime field but are not written as rows, so history is not backfilled.; The runtime PUT is signed by passing the control-internal secret as the shared argument to controlauth.Sign, the same effect as requestRunner.signingSecret in requests.go. controlauth.IsControlInternalPath does not list /runtime, so the control secret must be passed explicitly. controlauth is unchanged because it is outside the unit's file list.; The flag accepts 1, true, yes and on (the parser of QUARRY_SCHEDULE_REQUESTS), not only 1 as the spec's text says.; Failures are logged with the schedule id and a status code or label only (describe_failed, runtime_push_failed, skip_row_failed, plus the HTTP status, 0 for no response). Response bodies and transport error text are never logged. |
| Q5-S5a | E5.1 F-t2-070 tab context menu, W5-ORCH2 s2 W5-S5 (missing action contract) ; BrowserChrome.tsx:596-637, 1549-1590 | built | Gap confirmed before writing: the contract file did not exist, and the tab menu still had the menu-div Escape handler, which never fired because focus stayed on the tab button. New src/shared/actions/browser-tab-menu.ts holds the zod discriminatedUnion of browser.tab.select, browser.tab.new, browser.tab.close and browser.tab.open_outside (http/https url), a type guard for the outside link, and the pure browserTabMenuItems(). BrowserChrome.tsx builds the menu from browserTabMenuItems through a For keyed by position, parses each chosen action through the schema before calling the existing onBrowserSelectTab, prepareNewTabNavigation or onBrowserCloseTab, and drops the menu-div key handler. While the menu is open, document listeners handle Escape, pointerdown outside, Tab and ArrowUp/ArrowDown; the first enabled item takes focus on open and the opening tab button gets focus back on close. The VITE_BROWSER_TAB_MENU gate is unchanged. Nothing was compiled or tested, per the code-only rule. | - Deviations: Tab now closes the menu (WAI-ARIA menu pattern), and only while focus is inside the menu. This was not in the spec.; Escape is handled only while the menu is open. It calls stopPropagation, and it restores focus to the opener only when focus was inside the menu, so an Escape pressed in the address field does not move focus.; browserTabMenuItems offers the outside link only when outsideUrl is non-null AND parses as http(s), so an unsafe href is never rendered. The chrome already passes only http(s) urls.; tabId schema is 1 to 128 characters, not the gateway's [A-Za-z0-9_-]{3,128} wire rule. The session tab filter in browser-client.ts keeps any string tabId, and a stricter contract would make Vis fane and Lukk fane silently do nothing for an id the strip already shows.; The open_outside item closes the menu on a later turn (setTimeout 0) so the anchor is still in the DOM when the browser follows the link.; Menu items are rendered with For keyed={false} (the Solid 2 rc replacement for Index) so the focused item's DOM survives session updates.; Focus returns to the opening tab on every close, including a pointer down outside. The pointer's own mousedown then moves focus to what was pressed, so the visible result is unchanged. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q4-E4.2-4c-c**: recipe runs are unattached in v1 (as Q4-E4.2-3h/3i)
- **Q2-WS16.S5b**: none (plan default context; prefill flip waits for corpus precision)
- **Q2-WS17.E2a**: WS17.E2 as planned: the erasure saga holds org:keys:destroy:any (X-8 recommendation, env master keys, dark)
- **Q1-WS4.I-catalogue-keys**: X-16 at its recommendation: existing codes until the Model Plane Z-series commits (done), then one PR adds the keys and re-vendors
- **QF-5-reputation-catalogue-key**: One Protection key (Forced = block) instead of a 3-level org mode: the catalogue has no ordered off/warn/block scale, and an org may only tighten, so 'force block' is the only org lever that adds safety; warn stays a deployment mode
- **Q2-WS7.G**: D2-U34-5: under ZDR an egress receipt stores the host only
- **Q2-WS13.7-dispatch**: initiated_by is not stamped on this path: it forwards no actions (the tighten-only label only refuses actions) and stamping 'agent' would reclassify UI-originated scrapes for budget metering (budget.rs classify)
- **Q2-WS8.J-a**: ADR-3 attach 900 s with 180 s idle release (plan Q7 default)
- **Q2-WS15.S4a**: X-9 / D15 taken at recommendation: fire-time posture re-check, observe first, enforce later via QUARRY_EDGE__SCHEDULE_POSTURE
- **Q5-S5a**: Standalone presentation contract; no registry row (avoids the Model Plane golden re-vendor)

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q2-WS10.S4c**: Q2-WS10.S4c built (code only, not compiled). quarry-edge `POST /v1/agent/runs` accepts an optional `trace {run_id?, agent?, origin?}`: labels only, sanitized, with invalid tokens dropped (never a 400). The origin comes from `derive_origin(is_service, hint)`, and `run_id`/`agent` are kept only for a service token. `start_run` opens the event trace with `EventSink::open_run_with(RunTraceMeta{...})`. From then on, every event the run mints carries `Event::trace` {origin, agent, run_id = Model run id, request_id (current request scope, valid token only), causation_event_id}. `emit`, `emit_ephemeral` and `emit_for_zdr` return the minted `EventKind`. `emit_caused` and `emit_for_zdr_caused` link causation. `EventSink::trace_meta` exposes org, actor, browser run id and ZDR for the S5a publisher. The checkpoint stores origin and model_run_id (JSONB, no migration), and a resume restores them. The live browser-session projection carries `runOrigin`; the checkpoint projection omits it. There is no switch: nothing leaves the process until Q2-WS10.S5a.
- **Q2-WS10.S5a**: Q2-WS10.S5a built (code only, not compiled). New module quarry-runtime/src/model_trace_publisher.rs: ModelTraceConfig, TraceTransport, NatsTraceTransport (JetStream publish_with_headers, Nats-Msg-Id = bare event ULID, ack window 32), ModelTracePublisher (bounded queue 2048, single publisher task, try_enqueue refuses ZDR / bad model_run_id / unmapped, a full queue drops and counts, shutdown flushes for at most 2 s). Subject mp.v1.trace.run.<model_run_id>. EventSink::with_model_trace sends events to the publisher from mint while holding the run lock, on the durable emit path only. That keeps the order on the wire equal to seq order, and emit_ephemeral and ZDR emit_for_zdr never reach it. The two new ZDR matrix rows are browser_trace_publish (refused) and browser_trace_replay (inert, in-memory ring). The lead must regenerate zdr-feature-matrix.json, its sha pin and docs/ZDR_MATRIX.md. It stays dark until S5b wires QUARRY_EDGE__MODEL_TRACE_NATS_URL at boot.
- **Q4-E4.2-4c-c**: Q4-E4.2-4c-c built. The verevonv3 gateway now accepts `recipe {id, version?, tag?, slots}` on POST /api/v1/browser/sessions/{id}/ai-runs, behind AppState.browser_recipes (BROWSER_RECIPES, default off), and forwards it to model-gateway POST /v1/browser/runs only when present. Recipe runs are always unattached: attach_session_id is never sent for them, and requireAttached, continueInTab and assistantInteractive are refused with 400 recipe_attach_unsupported; planMode is refused with recipe_plan_mode_unsupported. The gateway's closed codes are browser_recipes_unavailable, recipe_attach_unsupported, recipe_plan_mode_unsupported, recipe_id_invalid, recipe_version_and_tag, recipe_tag_invalid and recipe_slots_invalid, plus model-gateway's recipe_requires_user mapped to 403 browser_recipe_forbidden. Slot values are never logged (Debug shows the slot count only) and never echoed in a refusal. The browser_run.start contract (browserRunStartInput.recipe), the action dispatcher and startBrowserAiRun all carry it. The agent-action-admission golden must be regenerated: the browser_run.start schemaHash changes. Needs MODEL_GATEWAY_BROWSER_RECIPE=true and EXECUTION_CORE_RECIPE_RUNNER downstream to run end to end.
- **Q2-WS16.S4b**: Q2-WS16.S4b built (code only; nothing compiled or tested). The json schema normaliser accepts anyOf, oneOf and const, which the S4a validator checks, and inlines local $ref to #/$defs and #/definitions before the keyword checks; cycles are schema_recursive, other refs schema_ref_not_supported, and a dangling local ref invalid_value. JsonSpec carries ComplexityClass, and Complex schemas use QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_MODEL_JSON_COMPLEX (default verevon-balance). ERROR_REASONS, CONTRACTS, SELF_HOST and the openapi keyword note are updated. Follow-ups outside this unit: the json_schema.rs header comment and the verevonv3 ingestions SCHEMA_CODES copy are now stale.
- **Q2-WS16.S5b**: Q2-WS16.S5b built (code only; not compiled, not run). Adds the harvest wiring behind QUARRY_EDGE__AI_FORMATS_HARVEST (default context, off unless AI formats are on). Pipeline: InlineRequest.want_harvest, and InlineParts.ai_structured and ai_json_ld, which are never serialized. Engine: HarvestMode, AiScrapeConfig.harvest, AiScrapeInput.harvest. In context mode a 4,000-char harvest block sits inside the untrusted page frame; highlights and the question still verify against the page text only. In prefill mode json takes exact-name top-level fields from the markup, sends only the remainder schema, makes no call when nothing remains, and checks the merged result against the full schema, reporting ai.json.prefilled provenance. The scrape route sets want_harvest only when AI formats are requested, a model hop is allowed and redact_pii is off. Lead: compile quarry-core, quarry-runtime and quarry-edge, then run ai_scrape, harvest_map, the ai_formats env-name test and docs-contract. No generated SDK or golden mirrors AiFormatReport; openapi.yaml was edited by hand and parses.
- **Q2-WS8.I-b**: Q2-WS8.I-b: the SPA side of per-tab Take over and Give back, and of Stop agent, is in place. browser_tab.control and browser_attach.stop are registered as human-only, exempt as no_owner_executor in the model-access table, and the browser client calls the gateway's tab control and attach-stop routes. Attach state and tab marks reach the SPA only through closed allow-lists, and no switch was added. The agent-action-admission golden must be regenerated at merge.
- **Q2-WS17.E2a**: Q2-WS17.E2a built (code only, dark): org-core allowlists org:keys:destroy:any and pins it to quarry-edge (like wrap/unwrap). The compose ORG_CORE_SERVICE_CREDENTIALS quarry-edge row now carries it, so the Quarry org erasure shred hop (QUARRY_EDGE__ERASE_SHRED_KEYS, off) can reach DELETE /internal/orgs/:org/keys once ORG_CORE_KEYS_ENABLED=1. The admin scope (rotate, metadata) is still granted to no principal. Test follow-up, existing tests the lead must fix: (1) org-core/internal/http/service_auth_test.go ~line 190 lists "org:keys:destroy:any" among scopes expected to FAIL validation for quarry-edge; it now passes, so that test fails. Move destroy into the accepted-for-quarry-edge loop and add a negative test for another principal. (2) Two test registry rows still list only wrap/unwrap for quarry-edge and should add destroy: keys_handlers_test.go line 36 and service_auth_test.go line 210. (3) The pinning loop in keys_handlers_test.go (~line 305) should also cover destroy for billing-core. (4) Any contract test that reads the compose registry text as written (e.g. retention_posture_contract_test.go) should be rechecked.
- **Q2-WS17.E2b**: Q2-WS17.E2b (docs only, 2026-11-11): added apps/Control Plane/ORG_KEY_CUSTODY_RUNBOOK.md, the org-core master-key custody runbook. It covers minting once, a two-person XOR escrow, loss, add-only master versions, the 503 keys_unavailable diagnosis, the restore drill, the enabling gates, rollback and switch rules, and limits for counsel. Linked it from the Quarry RUNBOOKS profile-encryption section, and fixed that section's restore step (non-existent expires_at and viewport.is_mobile fields). Updated the CONTROL_PLANE_OWNERSHIP Not done line: runbook written, minting and the restore drill remain person steps. Still open: the cmd/server wiring, the envelope_profile_store is_current_key blocker, no holder for the admin scope, the production secret path, and the custodian decision (D16 Q3).
- **Q1-WS4.I-catalogue-keys**: Q1-WS4.I-catalogue-keys built (X-16 closed at its recommendation): the ZDR refusal catalogue (mp-contracts zdr_refusal::FEATURES) now holds the Quarry keys durable_crawl, browser_profile, session_resume and page_screenshot. The verevonv3 ZdrFeatureKey/FEATURE_LABELS gained them with Norwegian labels. ZDR.md has four Quarry rows, and the quarry-edge zdr_feature_matrix comment was updated with the PENDING lists unchanged. There is no switch because this is vocabulary only. Follow-up for the lead: regenerate the golden JSON and its four vendored copies with their .sha256 pins, ZDR_REFUSALS_CONTRACT_SHA256 and the zdr-contract-sync.test.sh GOLDENS digest. Do it after mp-w345 W3-EP-6-P3-b (named_agent) merges, because that unit also appends to FEATURES. Separately, before this change the SPA already lacked legal_hold, learn_command and skill_authoring.
- **QF-5-reputation-catalogue-key**: QF-5 built, code only, not compiled. browser.reputation.block is a protection with baseline allowed, unlockable false, reserved false, since W3-E3.8.7, placed after browser.consent.reject; its golden is hand-written with sha256 pin 772f02d7c6306539591e44c61c385feeff555b5ef71b8d273480ba88bfaa8a9e and must be confirmed by the golden test. The edge reads the org cap from the run's stored web-policy decision, so it binds only where the claim is enforced, and it is dark by default because QUARRY_EDGE__REPUTATION_MODE is off and no org sets the key. Rollout notes: every non-default resolved policy now carries browser.reputation.block allowed in its snapshot, so an edge build without this key refuses such snapshots as an unknown capability; deploy readers before writers. The protection's neutral value is disabled, so a claim or grant snapshot that states allowed for this key is kept (unlike abilities) and marks the run non-default; QF-6 must not emit it. The default allowed value reports decision source invariant. Control Plane vendored goldens (apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/fixtures and apps/Control Plane/org-core/internal/org/testdata) are QF-6 and were not touched.
- **Q2-WS7.G**: Q2-WS7.G built (D2-U34-5): for ZDR runs, browser egress receipts store scheme://host[:port] only. BrowserEgressPolicy carries `host_only_receipts` (default false, so existing policies are unchanged). The quarry-edge gate sets it from the run's ZDR posture (egress_policy via the decision's zdr; live/extended/tab/attach paths). The chromiumoxide guard reads it from the session/tab policy meet, which fails closed to the host when no session policy is installed. The download receipt, the proxy receipt sync and the runtime pinned egress proxy honour it too. open_web_egress carries the flag across the open-web policy swap. No switch: this is ZDR hardening. The receipt contract is unchanged (url stays a string). A browser_egress_receipts row was added to the ZDR matrix. The golden/sha/markdown regeneration is owed. Nothing compiled and no tests written.
- **QF-22-quarry-docs-env-and-status**: QF-22 (docs only, 2026-11-11). The per-job webhook, batch parallel pages and fetch deadline switches, and monitor tags with control migration 027 are now documented against the code. QF-1's batch hash, which covers webhook and max_parallel, is already in the tree, so the 409 is current behaviour. Not changed, possibly stale and outside this unit: RUNBOOKS Agent attach 'Not done' still lists the sweeper release, while the RUNBOOKS metrics table credits quarry_agent_attach_released_total to Q2-WS8.J; ERROR_REASONS.md has no rows for invalid_tag or tag_required (openapi.yaml has them); CONTRACTS 'Crawl scope and robots: planned status' is a 2026-10-23 snapshot that still says scope fields are not applied on the crawl path, which CRAWL_SCOPE_ROBOTS section 12 contradicts.
- **Q2-WS10.S6-gw**: Q2-WS10.S6-gw built (code only, not compiled). The Verevon gateway serves GET /api/v1/runs/{run_id}/trace (chat.rs route next to /events/replay; streams.rs `run_trace`). It proxies model-gateway GET /v1/runs/:run_id/trace using the session-derived identity and the same model, session and capability bearers as the replay route. Only cursor (at most 256 printable characters), limit (1..500) and types (the seven BROWSER_* trace names) are forwarded. The page becomes {data:{runId,events,hasMore},meta:{cursor},links:{next?}}, with each event cut to its 17 envelope keys, and is marked no-store. Upstream 404 becomes run_not_found and 501 becomes run_trace_unavailable (new i18n row). Owner enforcement stays in Model Plane. The SPA reader is getRunTrace in src/shared/api/run-console-client.ts, with a strict RunTraceEvent type. There is no switch: pages stay empty until SESSION_CORE_TRACE_CONSUMER and the Quarry publisher are on, and a ZDR run gives an empty page. No UI consumer or action contract yet.
- **Q2-WS7.K-c**: Q2-WS7.K-c: the durable grant store persists the web-policy snapshot in browser_grants.web_policy (JSONB, NULL when none), added by migration 0008 (0002 was reserved by R-3; 0007 is the fill_otp binding). A session stores the caller's snapshot; a derived run or attach grant and a sensitive approval store their parent's snapshot unchanged. A stored snapshot that cannot be decoded fails the read as a store error, never as no snapshot. The store refuses ZDR grants, so no ZDR snapshot reaches the table. Persistence is dark behind BROWSER_BROKER_WEB_POLICY_SNAPSHOT, since no snapshot is minted with it off. origin_extension and human_browse grants carry no snapshot in either store, and the extension path checks the org site policy, not the web_policy claim. Deploy the broker before Quarry reads grant layers: the entrypoint applies 0008 on start.
- **Q2-WS9.A-ask**: Q2-WS9.A-ask built (frontend only). KnowledgeComposer now passes onAskVerevon to ScrapePreviewPanel, so the omnibox Ask Verevon row appears in the Home/Innhent browser. The handler writes a pending chat launch with only the typed text (startNewThread: true, zdr from zdrRequested() or the live session's zdr) and goes to /chat. A ZDR launch stays in memory only (the existing writePendingChatLaunch behaviour). No new switch, no new action id, no proto or migration changes, no regeneration needed. Not yet compiled or tested. A test that renders the composer inside a Router and checks the launch payload is still to be added.
- **Q3-E3.1-b2-c**: Q3-E3.1-b2-c: browser-broker migration 0007 adds nullable mail_connection_id and grant_origin to browser_grants, with CHECKs (1 to 128 characters; https:// prefix). The Postgres store persists both on fill_otp sensitive approvals, validates them with the memory store's checkFillOtpBinding, reads them back through the shared column list, and no longer refuses fill_otp; a binding on any other action is still refused. BROWSER_BROKER_FILL_OTP_ENABLED (compose default 0, already at apps/Model Plane/deploy/docker-compose.yml:2556) is registered in apps/Control Plane/flag-registry/flag-registry.json as model-plane and removed from unregistered; remove the switch after the OTP live proof. Built as code only; not compiled or tested in this unit. gofmt is clean on postgres.go. The batch compile, the browser-broker grant tests and the registry ratchet test are the first checks.
- **Q2-WS13.7-dispatch**: WS13.7 dispatch (Q2-WS13.7-dispatch): knowledge.scrape_url now forwards capture to quarry-edge as formats (markdown plus screenshot or pdf; the screenshot object form carries fullPage and quality only). The URL gets the direct scrape route's SSRF pre-filter, and a request with a capture takes the 135 s browser ceiling. The path is dark behind the existing edge switch QUARRY_EDGE__SCRAPE_CAPTURE. initiated_by is not stamped, because the path forwards no actions and stamping would reclassify UI scrapes for budget metering. The action envelope is unchanged, so capture bytes and artifact ids are not returned on this path.
- **Q2-WS16.S6d**: Q2-WS16.S6d built in the quarry-w45 checkout (code only, not compiled): StructuredExtractClient, StructuredExtractRequest/Response, ExtractionUsage (Rust and the Go mirror) and AiFormatRunner::json/json_for_org are deleted; summary, query and extract_for_org stay. The ledger should record DEC-15 as done. Historical and plan docs still name the removed items (docs/gap-quarry.md, docs/PROGRESS.md, docs/OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md, docs/FOLLOWUP_TASKS_2026-08-28.md, docs/PARITY_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md) and were left as they are. AiFormatRunner::extract_for_org has no production caller since S6a (only ai_formats.rs tests call it); it was kept because the spec does not name it, so the lead should decide whether it goes under DEC-15 too. No regeneration is needed because no schema golden or SDK lists the removed types. The stale copies under apps/Frontend Plane/Ingestion Plane/ and .claude/ still carry the old code and were not touched. lib.rs also holds another writer's uncommitted lines (the model_trace_publisher module and the RunTraceMeta re-export), which were left in place.
- **Q2-WS8.J-a**: Q2-WS8.J-a built and not compiled. The run sweeper's `sweep_once_ttl` calls `sweep_attach` in attach.rs for every run with a live attachment that is not expiring in that pass. It runs under a non-blocking try_lock of the entry and skips runs with an action in flight. Attachments idle past QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH_IDLE_TTL_S (default 180 s, read from RunLimits.attach_idle_ttl_s, its first production reader) end IdleReleased. Attachments whose attach grant no longer validates end Revoked; a broker outage keeps them. Both go through `finish_attach`, now pub(super), which closes or returns the tab and sends agent.detached via emit_for_zdr. Handoff marks are honoured on an idle release only after the grant is re-checked. Dry run counts `attaches_would_release` and logs `would_release`. SweepReport gains `attaches_released` and `attaches_would_release`, and there is a new metric `quarry_agent_attach_released_total{reason}` (added to the RUNBOOKS metrics table). `finish_run` ends a live attachment as SessionEnded before the durable record and runs `record_attach_end` (timeline event plus agent.detached), and its lane is waited on before the session is released. The tests named in the plan (sweeper_releases_attach_with_lapsed_grant, sweeper_releases_idle_attach, orphan_agent_tab_after_executor_crash) are not written yet.
- **QF-2-gw-batch-crawl-wait**: QF-2 (QB04 Q3-E3.7.2w and QB03 Q3-E3.7.3gw) is built in the verevonv3 gateway, code only, with no new switch. The batch create path forwards a valid maxParallel as max_parallel; the edge still decides the clamp under QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_PARALLEL with ceiling QUARRY_EDGE__BATCH_MAX_PARALLEL (default 4, hard 16), and values 17 to 64 pass the gateway check and are clamped there. The crawl status read forwards wait and resume to /v1/crawl/{id}; the edge gates wait with QUARRY_EDGE__JOB_CONTROL, caps it silently at 25 s (WAIT_MAX_S), and refuses it under ZDR with a 403 that the gateway passes through, while the gateway itself persists nothing. Ledger correction: the unit text says validation(...) answers 400, but the helper returns 422 (validation_error). Follow-up outside this unit: the SPA action schema ingestionsCreateRunInput in src/shared/actions/action-registry.ts does not declare maxParallel, so a zod-parsed client path would strip it; a contract change there would need the usual regeneration.
- **Q2-WS14.S3-cancel**: Q2-WS14.S3-cancel: run_cancelled of a crawl (the in-workflow BFS and the durable frontier) now carries the WS14.S3 scope summary (pages_blocked, pages_blocked_robots, links_denied, hosts_added, denials) and the sitemap block, the same as run_completed. The batch run_cancelled already carried pages_blocked and pages_blocked_robots under batch-check-v1. The change is an activity-input payload change only: no GetVersion, no flag, no migration. ZDR is unchanged because the edge refuses durable crawl and batch under ZDR (refuse_durable_job_under_zdr), so no cancelled run carries content for a ZDR org. Doc follow-ups, not edited here: docs/RUNBOOKS.md line 1902 says only run_completed gains the scope fields (run_cancelled now does too), and docs/CRAWL_SCOPE_ROBOTS.md line 79 says 3 samples per code while crawl_denials.go keeps 10 per code (denialSamplesPerCode), which RUNBOOKS line 1902 already states.
- **Q2-WS15.S4a**: Q2-WS15.S4a built (code only, not compiled). Change-monitor fires now run the edge's fire-time preflight from the version marker monitor-preflight-v1 when the schedule args carry schedule_id. Control now writes schedule_id into the ChangeMonitorWF args, so each existing change_monitor schedule is updated once on the next reconcile. A denied fire writes schedule_skipped {schedule_id, reason} with no URL and ends without probing. The edge decides through QUARRY_EDGE__SCHEDULE_POSTURE; observe, the default, always allows. ScheduledJobWF now sends target_kind, so the edge's skipped_kind_disabled check is live for scrape, crawl and batch fires. PreflightResult carries zdr. Executions recorded before the marker replay unchanged. trace_projection was not changed: change-monitor events do not reach the Model Plane run trace. Follow-up: the edge doc comment in schedule_preflight.rs still says the orchestrator sends neither target_kind nor enabled.
- **Q3-DOC-GAP-c**: Q3-DOC-GAP-c (2026-11-11): the GAP.md geo and locale rows now match the code. Per-page locale, timezone and location are built dark behind QUARRY_EDGE__PER_PAGE_LOCALE on the chromiumoxide driver only; the geolocation grant has no live test. location.country and country egress are not built and stay parked (E4.4-c, ADR 5 and ADR 10). Change diff: the raw-byte fingerprint is the default; QUARRY_EDGE__CHANGE_TEXT_FP and QUARRY_EDGE__CHANGE_TEXT_DIFF (GET /v1/change/diff) are built dark. /v1/map reads robots Sitemap lines in both modes; gzip sitemaps only behind QUARRY_EDGE__MAP_GZIP_SITEMAP. Still open: the mobile-only rows and the stealth rows were not re-checked. Register: one update (applied 2026-11-11) row under the Q3-DOC-GAP-c section of docs/STALE_DOC_DELETION_REGISTER.md.
- **Q4-DOC-DRIVER-c**: Q4-DOC-DRIVER-c (doc only, no code, no tests run): DRIVER_MATRIX.md step 4 now reads Browserbase only, with a Verified note under the capabilities matrix and a line under the provider-selection block, both linking the ADR 10 memo. gap-quarry.md QRY-01 (line 161) and QRY-19 (line 179) carry inline correction notes. The stale-doc register has an update row beside the line-222 row and a Q4-DOC-DRIVER-c section with an update row for gap-quarry.md; the line-68 archive row is not executed because the file was not moved. Ledger status: Kernel and Browserless remain unregistered in quarry-edge, and registration stays blocked until the owner decides ADR 10. The memo is Proposed 2026-11-11 and accepts nothing; its section 6 lists the blocked items.
- **Q2-WS15.S3b**: Q2-WS15.S3b built (code only, nothing compiled). GDPR org erasure now hard-deletes schedule_requests rows by their literal org_id. In Postgres this runs inside the PurgeOrg transaction, before the schedules delete, because migration 026 has no FK to schedules. The in-memory store gets a matching memScheduleRequests.purgeByOrg. PurgeResult.ScheduleRequestsDeleted counts the rows, is included in Total(), and appears in the gdpr log line. Orchestrator backfills now use the overlap policy the caller asked for: requests.go reads params.overlap_policy from the claimed row and passes it to Manager.Backfill(ctx, id, start, end, overlap). An empty value keeps ALLOW_ALL; skip, allow and cancel use the create/update overlapPolicy mapping; buffer_one and cancel_other are accepted as aliases. There is no migration and no new switch; the request rows stay behind QUARRY_CONTROL__SCHEDULE_REQUESTS. This closes SCHEDULES.md Known gaps 2 and 13 and the WS15 ownership/ZDR check (2).
- **Q2-WS15.S4c**: WS15.S4c (Q2) built dark in the Quarry orchestrator: QUARRY_SCHEDULE_RUNTIME_SYNC (default off, read once in cmd/orchestrator/main.go into schedules.Config.RuntimeSync). When on, each reconcile pass reads last_run_at and next_run_at from the Temporal schedule list. Every five minutes per desired schedule (only those whose memo org is in the desired set), it describes the schedule and PUTs last_run_at, next_run_at and skipped_overlap_count to control's /v1/internal/schedules/{id}/runtime, signed with the control-internal secret. Each new overlap skip is recorded once as a skipped run (POST /v1/internal/schedules/{id}/runs, run_key sched:<id>:skip:<n>, reason skipped_overlap), at most 10 rows per pass across schedules, for ScheduledJobWF schedules only, because control refuses change_monitor runs (AdmissionKind). The sync carries ids, counts and times only, no run content. Code is in services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/schedules/runtime.go; not compiled or tested in this unit.
- **Q5-S5a**: Q5-S5a (2026-11-11): the Browser tab context menu has a standalone zod action contract in src/shared/actions/browser-tab-menu.ts (browser.tab.select, browser.tab.new, browser.tab.close, browser.tab.open_outside), with no action-registry row. BrowserChrome.tsx builds its items with browserTabMenuItems and parses each chosen action through the schema before the existing handlers run. The dismissal defect is fixed with document-level pointerdown and Escape listeners while the menu is open, plus arrow-key navigation and focus return. The gate is still VITE_BROWSER_TAB_MENU (default off). The menu tests and the flag-on walk are owed separately and were not written or run here.

It reads each connection in the turn's scope through a new IntegrationActionsClient::connection_provider_and_label call (the same GET /api/v1/connections/{id} read already used for provider writes), and only exactly one google or microsoft connection qualifies. The offer then travels on ValidatedBrowserGrant into the PlanConfig. In the loop, gate_fill_otp clears any value, checks the target: it must be on the latest snapshot, in the top frame (Quarry's raw frame id is kept per snapshot), and on a canonical https page origin that is on the plan's domains and matches the root frame's origin. It then asks require_approval with Login and RiskyActionDetail.otp filled. On Granted it acquires a single-use child grant through the new BrowserBroker::acquire_fill_otp_grant: parent = run grant, allowed_actions [fill_otp], one frame id, grant_origin, mail_connection_id, using the person's browser bearer. It then sends FillOtpRef once (driver/429 replay is turned off for this verb). Denied, not offered, non-https, frame mismatch, or a broker refusal fails the step with otp_not_allowed through the failure budget. In tool_bridge, the fill_otp_ref input variant sits beside the other sensitive ones, with require_non_empty and the enum's deny_unknown_fields. The Do-loop browser.act refuses it with ACT_NOT_AVAILABLE, and the legacy MCP browser.act refuses it while the switch is off. The switch EXECUTION_CORE_BROWSER_FILL_OTP (literal `true`, default false) is in the Model Plane compose file and in flag-registry.json, placed in sorted order. | - Deviations: The spec does not mention a proto change, but ExecuteStepRequest had no connector scope, and browser_agent only runs through ExecuteStep. I added `ConnectorScope connector_scope = 15;` to ExecuteStepRequest (additive, documented) and updated the three full model-gateway struct literals with `connector_scope: None`. No caller fills it yet, so fill_otp is never offered until the gateway sends the turn's scope.; The offer is not threaded through execute_step_with_browser_grant / execute_step_inner as new parameters. It rides on ValidatedBrowserGrant (a private field set by `with_fill_otp`, dropped for a ZDR grant), so no runtime_loop signature changed.; fill_otp is offered only to leased runs, never attached runs. An attached run's grant is an attach grant, and its indexed planner menu was left untouched.; Frame origin: Quarry's fill_otp_ref has no frame variant, so the frame origin is the root frame's origin. It must canonicalize to the same https origin as the page; otherwise the step fails otp_not_allowed. allowed_frame_ids carries the target's raw Quarry frame id (one id, or none when Quarry reported none). This matches Quarry's authorizes_sensitive_action rule.; mail_provider_key is the resolved provider (`google` or `microsoft`). The QF-15 doc example said `gmail`. The label is the connection's displayName, cleaned of control characters, capped at 64 chars, and emptied if it contains '@'.; A TimedOut approval aborts the run with ApprovalTimedOut, as every other gate does. A Redirect follows the existing E3.2-c redirect path. Only Denied (or a redirect that is not accepted) maps to otp_not_allowed.; The Do-loop browser.act has no approval continuation, so fill_otp_ref is always refused there with ACT_NOT_AVAILABLE, whatever the switch says. The legacy MCP browser.act refuses it with an input error while the switch is off, and with the switch on forwards it like the other sensitive actions.; BrowserBroker::acquire_fill_otp_grant is a trait method whose default returns Refused, so the existing test fakes needed no change. The loop calls the production broker client directly. |
| Q2-WS10.S4d | plan 3.2 WS10 Slice WS10.S4 detail, agent_routes row ('after receipt, emit StepReceiptRecorded ... EgressDecided ... ArtifactRecorded', control/tab helper, Agen | built | quarry-edge now emits the run-trace events. In step.rs, both `step` and `execute_ws_action` emit `StepReceiptRecorded` once the receipt is appended (on success and on failure). The payload has receipt_id, step, the action's serde tag, outcome, latency_ms, error_code, cost_micro_usd, url_origin (scheme+host only), verified, initiated_by and control_epoch. A step with an observation then emits one `EgressDecided` summary (seq from/to, allowed, blocked) plus at most 20 block events (policy, origin, tab_id). It also emits one `ArtifactRecorded` for each screenshot, visual observation or evidence delta id it has. Every event after the first names the receipt event as its cause (`emit_for_zdr_caused`). In control.rs the new helper `record_timeline_and_trace` writes the timeline row and then emits `BrowserControlChanged` or `BrowserTabChanged`. The control route and the tabs.rs new (including the kept-tab path on a timed-out open), select and close routes use this helper. `AgentStarted` now carries {org_id, execution_tier, driver, profile_scope, control_mode}. An owner's close sends `AgentCompleted` {reason: "closed", steps, duration_ms}. Everything goes through emit_for_zdr, so a ZDR run's events stay live-only, and no payload carries typed text, a full URL or a selector. | - Deviations: The step trace events are emitted even when the receipt plan is Skip (a ZDR run, or a person's step under HUMAN_RECEIPT_MODE=none). The receipt is still built so its facts and id feed the event, and emit_for_zdr keeps a ZDR run live-only. The spec asks for emit_for_zdr so that ZDR runs stay live-only, which only makes sense if those runs emit at all. To support this, execute_ws_action now builds its receipt on every path and appends it only when the plan keeps one.; An EgressDecided summary is emitted only when the observation has at least one egress receipt. A step with no egress emits none rather than a summary of zeros.; bytes and content_type are left out of ArtifactRecorded because the observation does not carry them. Both are optional in the spec.; AgentExpired payloads (sweeper expiry and shutdown drain) are unchanged; the spec only names AgentCompleted. Their verdict reasons (idle, ttl, shutdown) fall back to `expired` in the projection. No BrowserSessionOpened or BrowserSessionClosed emits were added, as the lead note asks.; The delegate step in attach.rs (delegate_step) and the agent tab-open in attach.rs (~line 495) are not wired to the new trace events because the spec's file list leaves attach.rs out. Existing runs never exceed 18 lines, so they are unchanged.; send() now awaits the policy read before startRun. The skip-code entries are in the SPA map and match nothing until control projects the code.; Postgres gap: jobsStore.Create does not insert terminal_reason or finished_at, and writeScheduleSkipped does not run the job projector (markJobTerminalIfNeeded, called only from the events ingest loop in resources.go). A skipped row persists with an empty terminal_reason and no finished_at. For status skipped only, run_view fills finishedAt from created_at, the registration instant, which is the value registerScheduledRun sets in memory.; startedAt is created_at (a run row has no start time of its own).; Run list: the spec gives only the 1..20 clamp, so the default is 10. cursor is not forwarded. The response is a plain array of normalized runs under data, not an {items, nextCursor} page, because the SPA reads only the newest run.; listScheduleRuns takes an optional signal, matching the other client functions in ingestions-client.ts.; The lazy read is a per-row disclosure button (aria-expanded, Show runs / Hide runs) that mounts a child component. A closed row sends no request, and an unmount aborts an in-flight read. The spec named the behaviour but not the control.; No ZDR refusal was added. The read returns only ids, status, a closed reason, and times; the run's params, statistics and origin are dropped by normalization. |

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q2-WS8.J-b**: MP D7: mark only on Completed, loop-driven (no model tool)
- **Q5-S3d**: D-W5-2 = the recommended stance (append the token, never a non-Chromium identity), applied only to the TLS tier and only behind QUARRY_EDGE__UNIFIED_UA
- **QF-12-steer-taken-event**: Count-based event with FIFO marking, instead of plumbing clientMessageId through BFF, model-gateway and execution.proto (the gateway already echoes the id only to the caller)
- **QF-16-otp-exec-propose**: Mail connection = the single google/microsoft mail connection in the turn's connector scope; zero or several means the action is not offered (fail closed)
- **Q2-WS10.S4d**: No QUARRY_EDGE__NATS_AGENT_EVENTS kill switch restoring raw payloads: D2-098 (0704ce6a0) already drops agent-class events from the Ingestion bus unconditionally (never loosen)
- **Q3-E3.5-6c-c**: D-E356-4 one tag per monitor (recommended, as Q3-E3.5-6a)
- **Q4-E4.7-B8d**: Document the agent live surface now that WS8/WS9 shipped; the allowlist reason promised it
- **Q2-WS10.S5b**: Default off (empty URL) per the owner rule that new behaviour starts off; ':-' not ':?' so a stack without the bootstrap secret still starts
- **Q2-WS7.A**: ADR-1 outcome C at the plan's recommendation
- **Q5-S4a**: Clear-data is human-only (not model-visible), so a standalone contract, not a registry row
- **Q2-WS15.S4d**: D17 taken at recommendation: notice principal = schedule creator, content-free, title-only under ZDR (as W2-AP-S4c built)
- **Q5-F1d**: D-W5-4 option (a): org policy key default off, receipt per dismissal, no customise fallback
- **Q2-WS8.I-c**: ADR-3 implicit take-over on a human action (plan Q7)
- **Q4-E4.2-6**: B2(b) recorded: capability-core owns the store
- **Q5-S3c**: D-W5-1 recommended string and domain verevon.io/bot

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q2-WS10.S4e**: Q2-WS10.S4e (quarry-w45): the owner projection's runOrigin is a trace label (user, agent, schedule, webhook or api), never authority. The gateway copies it into session.runOrigin only for those five words, and the SPA reader browserRunOriginFromWire is strict. The seven browser trace events (browser.session.opened and closed, control, tab, receipt, egress, artifact) are listed in the openapi agent events stream and in CONTRACTS.md, and agent-class events are withheld from the Ingestion bus (D2-098). browser.session.opened and browser.session.closed have no emitter in this tree, since the run's open and close ride on agent.started and agent.completed. No UI reads runOrigin yet. The OQ-15 correction to docs/gap-quarry.md:347 is pending with the ledger owner (see deviations).
- **Q2-WS8.J-b**: Q2-WS8.J-b built, code only, not compiled. The Verevon gateway validates tabMarkOnComplete against none/deliverable/handoff and forwards it to Model Gateway as tab_mark_on_complete only on an attached run, under the existing BROWSER_AI_RUN_ATTACH_SESSION switch. With requireAttached and the switch off it refuses 400 browser_tab_mark_requires_attach; otherwise it drops the mark. The SPA sends it only from the controller's startAiRun keepTabOpen choice (false = none, true = deliverable) and never under zero retention. Open item: the visible Keep-open toggle is not built. Dedupe: recipe and tabMarkOnComplete are both optional fields appended after the existing fields of StartAiRunBody, browserRunStartInput and StartBrowserAiRunParams, in that order.
- **Q2-WS9.H-budget**: Q2-WS9.H (D-WS9H-2) built, code only, not compiled or tested. Quarry emits maxSteps on the owner projection for agent-class runs only; the gateway maps session.stepBudget; the SPA has a strict normaliser and a header chip with a warning at two or fewer remaining; all dark behind VITE_CHAT_BROWSER_DOCK (default off). Next for the lead: compile quarry-edge and the gateway, run the projection tests and the Vitest files for browser-session and InteractiveBrowserPane, and decide whether to keep deviation 1.
- **Q5-S3d**: Q5-S3d built (quarry-runtime tls_driver.rs only). Under QUARRY_EDGE__UNIFIED_UA the TLS tier drives Firefox and Safari as Chrome (warn names the profile only), sends the Chrome UA plus the product token when no override is given, and appends the token to a caller override that lacks QuarryBot. Switch off is unchanged. The Chromium tier, quarry-tls and escalation.rs are untouched, and no ZDR path is affected because the change only sets a request header. Inline tests use identity::parse_unified and no env mutation. Not compiled; rustfmt --check clean.
- **QF-12-steer-taken-event**: QF-12 is built and not compiled. A browser run's loop now emits a count-only `BrowserSteerTaken` event (OrchestrationEvent tag 31: run_id, plan_id, uint32 taken) whenever `take_steer_texts` takes queued steers at a step boundary. It never fires for a ZDR run, or while QUARRY_BROWSER_AGENT_STEER is off. session-core broadcasts and replays the event but does not persist it. model-gateway forwards it on /v1/runs/:id/events as SSE `browser_steer_taken` {run_id, plan_id, taken}, not `browser_run.steer_taken`: the taxonomy gate requires the proto field name. The space room projector drops it (policy row plus both copies of the space-room-run-frames contract). The SPA run console parses it and ChatLiveRunPanel calls `steer.markTaken(count)`, which marks the oldest `count` queued steer entries delivered, first in, first out. This closes followups QB02 Q3-E3.3-g3 without plumbing clientMessageId across planes. Still needed: regenerate the Go and Python bindings and sse-event-taxonomy.md.
- **QF-16-otp-exec-propose**: QF-16 (built, dark behind EXECUTION_CORE_BROWSER_FILL_OTP, default false, registered in flag-registry.json). execution-core can now propose fill_otp_ref. The planner sees a `fill_otp` verb only on a leased, non-ZDR browser run whose turn scope holds exactly one google or microsoft connection. The provider is read from integration-corev2 GET /api/v1/connections/{id}; zero, several, or an unreadable connection means the verb is not offered. The proposal raises a Login approval with RiskyActionDetail.otp filled (canonical https top origin and frame origin, provider key, a label that is never an address). On Granted, BrowserBroker gets an AcquireGrant for a single-use child of the run grant: allowed_actions [fill_otp], one frame, grant_origin (tag 24), mail_connection_id (tag 23), under the person's delegated browser bearer. FillOtpRef is sent once with that grant id and never replayed. Denial, non-https, frame mismatch or a broker refusal fails the step otp_not_allowed. Turning it on end to end also needs BROWSER_BROKER_FILL_OTP_ENABLED and QUARRY_EDGE__FILL_OTP. Additive proto change: ExecuteStepRequest.connector_scope = 15 (ConnectorScope). The gateway must still fill it from the turn before the verb can ever be offered, so that wiring is a follow-up. The Do-loop browser.act always refuses fill_otp_ref (ACT_NOT_AVAILABLE).
- **Q2-WS10.S4d**: Q2-WS10.S4d built (quarry-edge agent_routes). step.rs adds StepTraceFacts and emit_step_trace: StepReceiptRecorded after each recorded step receipt, on success and failure, in both the HTTP and WS step paths. A step with an observation then emits an EgressDecided summary plus at most 20 block events, and one ArtifactRecorded per stored screenshot, visual observation or evidence delta. All events go through emit_for_zdr or emit_for_zdr_caused, and every event after the first names the receipt event as its cause. control.rs adds record_timeline_and_trace, which writes the timeline row and then emits BrowserControlChanged or BrowserTabChanged. It is used by the control route and by the tabs.rs new, kept-on-timeout, select and close routes. AgentStarted now carries execution_tier, driver, profile_scope and control_mode, and AgentCompleted carries reason=closed, steps and duration_ms. No BrowserSessionOpened/Closed emits were added (each run emits one open and one close). Not wired: attach.rs delegate_step and the agent tab open. Nothing compiled. Existing tests that count run events or replay-ring seqs after a step or tab call may need new expectations.
- **Q3-E3.5-6c-c**: Q3-E3.5-6c-c (code only, 2026-11-11, branch quarry-w45): Verevon gateway monitor tags. POST /api/v1/monitoring/schedules takes an optional tag, forwarded to the edge only when set. GET /api/v1/monitoring/schedules?tag= filters. PATCH /api/v1/monitoring/schedules/{id} with {tag} sets or clears (""), and refuses a body with no tag. Refusals are 400 validation_error with details {field:'tag', reason}. Every monitor view carries tag as a string or null. The tag grammar is the edge's (leading letter or digit). The SPA gains tag params on monitoring-client.ts createMonitor and listMonitors, plus setMonitorTag and ChangeMonitor.tag. The monitoring.create_monitor registry input gains tag, and the action dispatcher forwards it. Open: the agent-action-admission golden regeneration (VEREVON_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 at the mp-w345 second merge). Nothing was compiled or tested in this unit.
- **Q4-E4.7-B8d**: Q4-E4.7-B8d: docs/openapi.yaml now documents the seven agent browser-session and live-frame routes (GET .../browser-session, POST .../browser-session/control, GET .../browser-session/timeline, GET .../frame, GET .../frames/stream, GET .../frames/ws, GET .../live-view). scripts/openapi-route-allowlist.json no longer lists them, and route_registrations stays 133. Gating is stated per route: none of the seven is switched by QUARRY_EDGE__HUMAN_BROWSE or QUARRY_EDGE__ATTACH, which gate fields only, and the live routes read the scope header only with QUARRY_EDGE__LIVE_VIEW_ONLY. A ZDR timeline read is 409. Nothing was compiled or run. Follow-ups for the lead: add a frame stream and socket section to docs/CONTRACTS.md; correct its camelCase-throughout claim for timeline items; decide whether live_frame_stream's SSE error text should be redacted like the socket's error frame.
- **Q2-WS10.S5b**: Q2-WS10.S5b built: quarry-edge reads `ModelTraceEnv` at boot (trace_config.rs). An empty `QUARRY_EDGE__MODEL_TRACE_NATS_URL` means no publisher (dark). A URL without a password, a queue outside 1..=65536, or a bad subject prefix aborts boot. When enabled, main.rs connects the publish-only `quarry-trace-producer` principal to the Model NATS, chains `EventSink::with_model_trace`, and on shutdown flushes for at most 2s after the usage meter. If the connection fails, a warning is logged (no secrets) and the edge continues without the trace. The Ingestion compose passes the URL (default empty; dev sets nats://model-nats:4222 explicitly), the user (fixed) and the password (`MODEL_QUARRY_TRACE_NATS_PASSWORD`, default empty). Turning it on outside dev waits for the X-4 NATS credential review. Not compiled yet.
- **Q2-WS7.A**: Q2-WS7.A is built as docs only (code only, not compiled, nothing deployed). The Model Plane decision ledger has a new 2026-11-11 entry, 'Org web policy has one issuer: Auth Core signs the claim, org-core stores the policy, consumers only enforce (ADR-1 outcome C, WS7.A)', status decided. It records the claim web_policy, the 345 s staleness bound, the resolved fail-mode open point (the entitlement read fails to floor; the claim keeps 15 minutes of last-good then 503), the built state WS7.H to WS7.K with Q2-WS7.K-a..c, and the switches (off by default). Matrix row C2 now names Q2-WS7.K-a to Q2-WS7.K-c and BROWSER_BROKER_WEB_POLICY_SNAPSHOT.
- **Q4-E4.2-4d-c**: Q4-E4.2-4d-c is partial. The SPA saved-recipe list, Run and mount are built behind VITE_BROWSER_RECIPES (default off). The blocker is that the list GET /api/v1/browser/recipes carries no slot names or kinds, so slot inputs never render and Run is disabled for every recipe. The fix is outside this unit: either list items carry slots as [{name, kind}] (a capability-core join over the latest version, or the gateway reading the version body) or a gateway detail route. - **Q5-S4a**: Q5-S4a built: the SPA clear-browsing-data operation now has a presentation-level zod contract (browser.profile.clear_data) in apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/actions/browser-profile-clear.ts. It is standalone and not a registry row, so clear-data stays human-only and the Model Plane admission golden is unchanged.
