- **Q2-WS15.S4d**: Q2-WS15.S4d built (dark, QUARRY_CONTROL__SCHEDULE_NOTICES default off, also needs NOTIFICATION_CORE_URL and NOTIFICATION_QUARRY_CONTROL_SERVICE_TOKEN). notify.Sink.NotifyScheduleRun sends quarry.schedule_run_failed and quarry.schedule_run_skipped to the schedule's CreatedBy (D17), with the HMAC v2 delegation of NotifyChange. The payload is a fixed title and body plus schedule_id, schedule_kind, a closed reason and run_id; it never carries a URL or target. Under schedule.Zdr it uses retention_mode zdr and sends the title alone. A run_failed or run_timed_out event for a schedule-origin job notifies once per job (key sched-fail:<job_id>); a denied fire notifies after its schedule_skipped append (key sched-skip:<schedule_id>:<run_key>). Both paths are best-effort, 5 s, and never fail the request. Not covered: the run_timed_out that finalizeJob writes directly raises no notice. Not compiled or tested yet.
- **Q3-E3.8.6-sync-c**: Q3-E3.8.6 code built on quarry-w45, uncommitted: the `security-feed-sync` compose service sits behind the default-off `security-feeds` profile and writes ./config/security-feeds/quarry-security.json (plus a .meta.json sidecar) for quarry-edge. The edge loads the file at boot and reloads it only when QUARRY_SECURITY_SNAPSHOT_WATCH_S is positive (default 0, unchanged). Before the watch is enabled anywhere, fix scripts/sync-security-feeds.py: when every feed fetch fails it still writes an empty blocklist, so a URLhaus outage would clear reputation blocking until the next good sync at least 600 s later (the script was out of this unit's file list, so it is unchanged). Also stale or inconsistent, outside this unit's files: Quarry-v2/docs/RUNBOOKS.md (around line 1047) still says nothing runs the sync as a compose service; the README Defaults bullet `QUARRY_SECURITY_SNAPSHOT_PATH=config/security-feeds/...` conflicts with compose forwarding that variable to the edge as an in-container path; .gitignore does not cover quarry-security.meta.json or the .json.tmp file, and the tracked quarry-security.json will show as modified after the first run.
- **Q5-F1d**: Q5-F1d (SPA) built, code only, not compiled. The desktop run reads browser.consent.reject from the org's effective view (browser.get_org_policy through createBrowserPolicyPort) and passes startRun({ consentReject }) to the desktop bridge. An absent key, failed or timed-out (5 s) read, or any value other than 'allowed' is off; the answer is cached per org for 60 s. Each receipt arrives in the adapter's `receipts` field (not consentReceipts) and becomes a planner-history line 'consent banner rejected on <host>'. The planner gets only the latest 19 history lines, because the gateway refuses more than 20. Receipts are not drawn in the assistant panel; the run trace is the planner history, as the spec states. Open for the lead: this gate reads org-core's effective view, not Quarry's resolve(), so an org 'allowed' turns desktop dismissal on even while Quarry's lattice keeps the key disabled (the Q5-F1b closeout). ZDR: a receipt carries only a host, and the planner request already carries the page URL, so no extra refusal was added. No action-contract, golden, SDK or migration change.
- **Q2-WS15.S4e**: WS15.S4e built in the Verevon gateway and SPA, with no switch. GET /api/ingestions/schedules/{id}/runs proxies the S3a edge route and normalizes each run to {id, status, reason, startedAt, finishedAt}; schedule rows carry skippedOverlapCount. Known gap: a run row carries only terminal_reason, and the specific skip code (skipped_zdr and the others) is on the schedule_skipped event, so skipped fires show the generic schedule_skipped label. Control's Postgres jobs insert also drops terminal_reason and finished_at for skipped fires; the gateway fills those two values, and the control fix is a follow-up outside this unit.
- **Q4-062b-spa-c**: Q4-062b-spa-c (SPA research run chip and Stop): built behind VITE_CHAT_RESEARCH_RUNS, default off. The chat stream's research_run frame is dispatched with all five status words, and the controller's evidence handlers, shared by the live send and resume, keep the newest valid run on the assistant turn; an unknown status word keeps the turn's current status. The chip sits beside the answer, outside the printable content, and is hidden in temporary and crawl-plan read-only threads. Stop calls chat.research_run.cancel through executeAction. Open dependency: the gateway's execute_action has no arm for that id yet (Q4-062b-bff-c), so Stop returns 501 until one lands.

- **Q2-WS8.I-c**: Q2-WS8.I-c: BrowserChrome shows a per-tab Take over / Give back chip on agent-opened tabs (from tab.holder; Give back needs a live attach), Levert and Overlevert marks from tab.mark, and a 'Du tar over' notice on the first press or type into an agent-held tab. - **Q5-S1c**: Q5-S1c built: BrowserObservation gains an extra field, `pdf_artifact_id`. It is set only by a `pdf` action, from the id that put_artifact_if_allowed returns, and is None under ZDR/waived or when artifact persistence is absent. The id is also added to proof_bundle.artifact_ids. deny_reads_after_otp drops it from both the observation and the proof bundle, so a withheld OTP page never exposes its PDF. openapi.yaml BrowserObservation lists it as an optional nullable string. It is not on EvidenceDelta, which never carries a PDF. The SPA (Q5-S1d) and gateway replay (Q5-S1b) readers can now get a real value. The SDKs need regenerating.
- **Q2-WS16.S4c**: Q2-WS16.S4c: the Model Plane schema_check.rs port of anyOf, oneOf and const already matches Quarry's S4a json_schema.rs (same codes const_mismatch, any_of_no_match and one_of_mismatch with the match count; zero and many oneOf matches both give one_of_mismatch; branch issues isolated; same check order; properties in serde_json key order, and neither lockfile pulls indexmap in; neither validator sets a depth limit). The only change is the module header, which called these keywords MP-only extensions. | QF-10-mg-browser-page-context | followups.md QB02 Q3-E3.3-d2b (Model Plane acceptance); MP-ASKS W3-E3.3-c and W3-E3.3-d; plan 3.3 E3.3 'attachments classified BrowserScraped' | built | model-gateway now accepts browser tab refs and regions that the BFF has verified. InvokeRequest gets three new fields next to context_refs: browser_tab_refs, browser_regions and browser_context_verified. The new module browser_page_context.rs sits behind MODEL_GATEWAY_BROWSER_PAGE_CONTEXT_ENABLED (only the literal true turns it on; default off). Its resolve() refuses a ZDR or waived turn that carries either field with the ZDR feature browser_page_context, whatever the switch says. It drops both fields unread unless browser_context_verified is true. It refuses Do, deep-research and image-generation turns (code browser_page_context_unsupported) and checks the caps: 8 tab refs, 4 regions, png/jpeg/webp only, at most 2 MiB decoded per image, at most 4000 selection characters. It reads the snake_case shape that the QF-8 BFF writes back. Tab refs and selection text go into one sanitized system block placed just before the turn's user message. The block is labelled untrusted and browser-scraped and says a reference grants no browser action. Region screenshots ride as image parts of the user turn, added through a new ImageParts::push_browser_regions. trust.rs classifies browser_tab_ref and browser_region_attachment as BrowserScraped and has a new test for that. zdr_features.rs gets field rows and a refusal check so both checks give the same answer. The switch is in flag-registry.json and in the model-gateway compose env, default false. Nothing is persisted: the block is only a prompt message and the images live only in request memory. | - Deviations: resolve signature is resolve(enabled, &InvokeRequest, &TurnFacts{zdr, waived, agentic, diverted}) instead of resolve(req, zdr), following context_refs::resolve. The switch is passed in, so tests do not touch the process env. Do, deep-research and image-generation turns are refused typed (browser_page_context_unsupported) instead of silently losing the context, because those paths never read the chat prompt.; As with context_refs, a ZDR or waived turn carrying either field is refused even with the switch off. That keeps zdr_features::refusal (which cannot see the switch) and resolve() in agreement.; The spec named only the files listed. The unit also needed: sse.rs (wiring: resolve after context_refs, region images into image parts, block insert before bind); vision_parts.rs (new ImageParts::push_browser_regions); normalize.rs (the test struct literal gets the new fields); tests/zdr_feature_table.rs (rows for the new fields, so the existing totality test still passes); mp-contracts zdr_refusal.rs (new 'browser_page_context' FEATURES entry, otherwise Refusal::feature_not_supported debug-asserts and the catalogue test fails); and the verevonv3 zdr-refusals.ts feature union and labels (nb/en). I also added a row to docs/ZDR.md.; Region images ride only as image parts. Image parts are gated by the existing CHAT_VISION_AS_PARTS_ENABLED switch and need a picked model that reads images. Without both, a turn with regions is refused (browser_page_context_unsupported) rather than answered without them. Tab refs alone work without vision.; The QF-8 gateway drops region selection text. model-gateway still reads an optional selection_text (alias selectionText), capped at 4000 characters, for a later BFF that signs it. Today that text never arrives.; The context block is inserted as a system message right before the last user message, never inside the standing prompt head (composed_head). I did this instead of adding a new PromptContext slot, so prompt_context.rs and its tier plan stay unchanged. |

Attesting from the existing probe would attest the crawl grants blind. MP-ASKS E3.7.10 asks for the probe_ready pattern with the two new scope sets.; The spec named no capability-core file. availability.go's genericGlobalHealthCapabilityIDs refuses unlisted ids with 403, so the three ids were added there with a comment, following the cap.workspace.read and cap.browser.observe precedent (record as a shared edit). availability_test.go was updated where its exact-set pin would otherwise fail (TestGenericGlobalHealthAllowlistIsExactlyTheExecutionOwnedRuntimes), and its dependency-backed and accepted-and-audited lists were extended to match cap.tool.http.; The crawl rows use reason code quarry_edge_ready_crawl_credentials_probed and their own reason text, not the cap.tool.http code, because their probe proves different grants. Heartbeat, TTL, note_probe_outcome bookkeeping and the POST are the same as for cap.tool.http. Cost classes follow the migration seeds: cap.web.crawl and cap.web.batch variable, cap.web.map bounded.; The crawl leg is gated on the cap.tool.http probe passing, including its search and extract mints, as the spec states. This is conservative. Making the crawl leg independent would need its own quarry /ready check and was not done.; The switch is read in web_tools_ready through crawl_tools_enabled() and folded into DependencyProbes.web_crawl, the same way browser_ready folds its switch into browser. Tests cannot mutate env, so the expected-ids test covers the on and off gate through that bit. |

Values are kept only in component memory, never prefilled, and cleared on a new choice, a session change or an accepted start. | QF-19-skill-restore-url-patterns | followups.md QB06 Q4-E4.3-043c1 ('a restored (undone) skill comes back with url_patterns [] (RESTORE_SKILL_SQL unchanged)') | built | RESTORE_SKILL_SQL in session-core now names url_patterns in the INSERT column list. It selects COALESCE(r.url_patterns, '[]'::jsonb), declares `url_patterns jsonb` in the jsonb_to_record definition (the column is JSONB, per migration 0116), and restores the patterns in the ON CONFLICT arm. The revision snapshot builds its JSON from an explicit key list (agent_skill_revision_snapshot, last replaced in 0110) that never had url_patterns, so fixing the restore SQL alone would still have wiped the patterns. New migration 0117 (with a revert file) replaces the snapshot function with 0110's keys plus 'url_patterns' last, always present. The truncated shape is unchanged, and restore already refuses a truncated snapshot. The existing source assert in restore_keeps_the_kind_of_a_deleted_template now checks the new record definition, the COALESCE(r.url_patterns, ...) select, 0117's snapshot key and the conflict arm. Pattern validation is unchanged: a restore does not validate them again. | - Deviations: The spec assumed only grpc.rs needed changing, or that the snapshot might be to_jsonb of the whole row. It is an explicit key list in a SQL function, so the fix needs a new migration, 0117_skill_revision_url_patterns.sql, plus revert/0117_skill_revision_url_patterns.down.sql. This undoes 0116's choice to leave the snapshot as it was.; The conflict arm does not use a plain `url_patterns = EXCLUDED.url_patterns`. It uses `CASE WHEN $1::jsonb ? 'url_patterns' THEN EXCLUDED.url_patterns ELSE agent_skills.url_patterns END`. Snapshots written before 0117 have no url_patterns key, and undoing to one of them should keep the row's current patterns, not wipe them. Recreating a deleted skill from such a snapshot still gives '[]', because nothing else is available.; One side effect of 0117: an edit that changes only url_patterns now records an 'edited' revision. Before, the two snapshots were equal and nothing was recorded. |

| QF-25-orgcore-keys-boot-wiring | QB12 lead review (Q2-WS17.E2b report): cmd/server never wires the org key service | built | I wired the WS17.E2 org key service into org-core's cmd/server/main.go. It runs only when orgkeys.Enabled(os.Getenv) is true. At startup it calls httpserver.ValidateOrgKeysConfig, builds the service with orgkeys.NewServiceFromEnv(db, os.Getenv) and calls server.SetOrgKeys(svc) after the retention setter. It then starts runOrgKeysAuditOutbox on a 5s ticker with the shutdown context, calling svc.FlushAudit(ctx, 100). If validation or the service build fails, it logs a fixed message that names ORG_CORE_KEYS_ENABLED and exits through log.Fatalf like the other startup refusals. When NATS is available it also sets the service's audit publisher, using the same typed-nil guard as the retention publisher. With the switch off, nothing new is read, built or started. Nothing was compiled. | - Deviations: Added orgKeysService.SetAuditPublisher(natsClient) when NATS is connected (guarded against a typed nil). The spec only covered the case with no publisher. Without this call FlushAudit could never publish, so key audit intents would never leave the outbox.; The audit loop never logs an error string, only fixed text and counts. Repeated failures are rate-limited: one line when flushing starts to fail and one when it recovers. Without that, a missing publisher would write a warning every 5s. The spec allowed one log per tick; this is stricter and less noisy.; The validation runs at the top of main next to the other config validators, before the DB connects. It still runs before the HTTP server starts. |

Compile fix, everything else: Go modules, Python, SQL migrations, compose/JSON/YAML/shell: green. 7 fixes.

Compile fix, Frontend Plane verevonv3 (SPA + apps/gateway BFF): green. 3 fixes.

Owner decisions taken at the recommended option (dark code):

- **Q5-S6b**: D-W5-4 option (a) without the customise fallback; 077 stays parked
- **QF-10-mg-browser-page-context**: Model Plane trusts only BFF-verified fields (browser_context_verified), never a client trustClass, per MP-ASKS W3-E3.3-d
- **QF-18-provider-read-context-cap**: gmail.messages.get joins CONTENT_READS (followup names it the candidate)
- **Q2-WS13-14-compose**: defaults stay observe/lenient/off (X-10 flip is not this unit)

Ledger notes (move into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **Q5-S6b**: 2026-11-11, Q5-S6b (branch quarry-w45, doc only): docs/DECISIONS_W5.md records three Wave 5 decisions. (1) F-t2-077, the user's own Chrome, stays parked (owner ruling 2026-09-28); the isolated-egress gate refuses a user-browser backend, and reopening needs a new owner ruling, the E4.5 multi-backend abstraction (ADR 10) and a redesigned fail-closed gate. (2) F-t2-075 is closed with nothing to delete: deprecation and sunset headers are built in plan D5 WS5.D, there is no signed-image premise, and no usefulness event exists. (3) The ADR-5 amendment per D-W5-4 option (a), without the customise fallback, is amended and built dark behind browser.consent.reject (default disabled): Q5-F1a and Q5-F1b in 3190cbb13, Q5-F1c in 1cf692f59, Q5-F1d in 8184a0eb0. Open: Quarry's resolve() has no unlock path for the key, and the Control Plane catalogue copies lag the Quarry contract until QF-6 (not built).
- **QF-27-spa-schema-error-copy**: QF-27 (copy and comment only, no switch): the SPA's AI-schema refusal copy now matches the QB12 normaliser. $ref (local #/$defs/ and #/definitions/ refs are inlined), anyOf, oneOf and const are accepted; pattern and format are refused. New copy for schema_ref_not_supported and schema_recursive. The json_schema.rs header is corrected. The ScrapeAiFormat text in docs/openapi.yaml already describes this behaviour, so it did not change.
- **QF-6-reputation-catalogue-vendor**: QF-6 (Control Plane): org-core testdata and the auth-core fixture now hold the Quarry web-policy catalogue byte for byte (15 keys, sha256 772f02d7c6306539591e44c61c385feeff555b5ef71b8d273480ba88bfaa8a9e), so browser.reputation.block is a valid org document key with modes allowed and forced (disabled is refused). The org-core key-count assertion moved from 14 to 15. No Go or TS logic, route, migration, proto or secret changed, and the ZDR claim goldens are unchanged because the key has zdr_implies_disabled false.
- **QF-10-mg-browser-page-context**: QF-10 built dark behind MODEL_GATEWAY_BROWSER_PAGE_CONTEXT_ENABLED (default off). model-gateway InvokeRequest now carries browser_tab_refs, browser_regions and browser_context_verified. The new module browser_page_context.rs only reads them when the BFF sets browser_context_verified=true (QF-8 inject_turn_browser_context); client trust classes are never trusted. Tab refs (max 8) and optional selection text become one sanitized system block before the user turn. The block is labelled untrusted and browser-scraped and states that a reference grants no browser action. Region screenshots (max 4, png/jpeg/webp, at most 2 MiB each) ride as image parts of the user turn. That needs CHAT_VISION_AS_PARTS_ENABLED and a vision model, otherwise the turn is refused browser_page_context_unsupported. Do, deep-research and image-generation turns are refused the same way. A ZDR or waived turn carrying either field is refused zdr_feature_not_supported/browser_page_context, whatever the switch says. The new mp-contracts feature key and a zdr_features row keep both checks in agreement. trust.rs classifies browser_tab_ref and browser_region_attachment as BrowserScraped. Nothing is stored. Pending: regenerate the zdr-refusals.json fixtures (Model Plane api/contracts, Go zdrrefusal, verevonv3 contracts plus its sha pin). Nothing was compiled or run.
- **QF-17-cap-web-health-attest**: QF-17 (E3.7.10 execution-core health half): execution-core attests cap.web.crawl, cap.web.map and cap.web.batch in health_attest.rs while EXECUTION_CORE_WEB_CRAWL_TOOLS is on, only after the cap.tool.http probe passes and a new mint probe (web_tools.rs probe_crawl_ready) shows map:read and crawl:write mint. Shared edit: capability-core's generic-health allowlist (availability.go) gained the three ids, with pins in availability_test.go. Known limit: cap.web.crawl and cap.web.batch can attest available while quarry-edge refuses durable jobs under the execution-core ZDR lane (W4 blocker B4), so keep EXECUTION_CORE_WEB_CRAWL_TOOLS off until that decision lands. CAPABILITY_PROMOTION_LEDGER.tsv has no cap.web.* rows; it was not touched.
- **QF-28-gw-action-arms-research-tab**: QF-28 built: the Verevon gateway action surface now dispatches chat.research_run.cancel, browser_tab.control and browser_attach.stop instead of answering 501. chat.research_run.cancel calls the existing POST /api/v1/chat/research-runs/{id}/cancel handler (cancel_research_run, now pub(crate)) behind CHAT_RESEARCH_RUNS, which answers 404 chat_research_runs_unavailable when off. The result is {id, stopRequested} in the action envelope, which fixes the SPA's ResearchRunStatus Stop. browser_tab.control and browser_attach.stop call the Q2-WS8.I-a control_tab and stop_attach handlers, so the BROWSER_AI_RUN_ATTACH_SESSION switch (409 browser_attach_unavailable when off), the owner-projection gate and the ZDR refusal are the same as on the REST routes. No new switch, no SPA change and no golden edits. Nothing has been compiled or tested yet.
- **QF-18-provider-read-context-cap**: QF-18 (code only, not compiled or tested): provider content reads keep their full text in the model's tool context. The cap is READ_RESULT_CHAR_CAP (12,000) plus 512 framing characters for execute_provider_action and read_provider_action content reads, up from 8,000. gmail.messages.get is now a content read (12,000-char cap in the execute render, was 2,000). The Slack message.send summary names body.channel. No new switch: execute_provider_action is in the base offered set (base_tool_defs), so the change is live for connected runs. read_provider_action is not a risky tool, so its content reads over 8,000 chars are handle-parked when EXECUTION_RESULT_HANDLES is on and reach this cap only with handles off. Still out of scope per the spec ("otherwise unchanged"): non-content reads through read_provider_action are cut at 8,000 although render_provider_read allows 12,000. Any other writer adding a ToolStepResult literal in agent.rs must add content_read (28 sites now). Addresses QB08 Q4-E4.3-056a2 and QB07 Q4-E4.3-043d1/056c, pending the lead's compile and tests.
- **QF-29-recipe-list-slots-and-run**: QF-29 built: the verevonv3 BFF has GET /api/v1/browser/recipes/{recipe_id}?version= behind BROWSER_RECIPES. It goes through model-gateway /v1/recipes/{id} and then /v1/recipes/{id}/versions/{v} with the caller's capability token and projects only {recipeId, name, version, slots[{name, kind text/secret}]}, using the closed browser_recipe_* codes. The SPA RecipesPanel (VITE_BROWSER_RECIPES) now loads that detail when a recipe is chosen. It renders text inputs and masked secret inputs, which are never prefilled, stored or logged. Run is pinned to the detail's version, disabled until every slot is filled and hidden under ZDR. Follow-up: the existing createAiRunStartKeyring fingerprint holds the slot values, secrets included, in memory until a start succeeds or fails unambiguously. This is retry memory only, not storage, but it could be replaced with a hash. Nothing has been compiled or tested.
- **QF-19-skill-restore-url-patterns**: QF-19 built. Undoing an edit of a session-core skill no longer drops its url_patterns. Migration 0117 adds 'url_patterns' (always present) to agent_skill_revision_snapshot, which was an explicit key list from 0110 without the column. RESTORE_SKILL_SQL inserts COALESCE(r.url_patterns, '[]'::jsonb) and declares it in the record definition. Its conflict arm writes the snapshot's patterns only when the snapshot has the key, so a snapshot from before 0117 keeps the row's current patterns. Side effect: an edit that changes only url_patterns now records an 'edited' revision. No switch (data-loss fix). The 0117 revert must run before the 0116 revert. Nothing was compiled or run; the extended source assert in restore_keeps_the_kind_of_a_deleted_template pins the new SQL.
- **QF-30-control-skipped-run-row**: QF-30 (2026-11-11): a skipped schedule fire's job row now stores its closed skip code as terminal_reason and the fire time as finished_at. The Postgres jobs insert writes both columns (migration 016 already has them). Closed set in quarry-control schedule_runs.go: skipped_zdr, skipped_posture_unknown, skipped_no_entitlement, skipped_overlap, skipped_disabled, skipped_kind_disabled, preflight_denied; any other reason stores schedule_skipped. The schedule_skipped event payload is unchanged. No switch, no migration, no proto; the in-memory store needed no change.
- **Q2-WS13-14-compose**: Compose passthroughs for the Quarry WS13/WS14 switches are in place (apps/Ingestion Plane/docker-compose.yml and Quarry-v2/deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml). quarry-edge passes QUARRY_EDGE__CRAWL_SCOPE_MODE=observe, CRAWL_REQUEST_VALIDATION=lenient, CRAWL_BATCH_MAX_URLS=500 and SCRAPE_STITCH=off. quarry-control passes AUDIT_CORE_URL and AUDIT_CORE_SERVICE_TOKEN from QUARRY_CONTROL_AUDIT_CORE_URL and QUARRY_CONTROL_AUDIT_CORE_SERVICE_TOKEN, empty by default, so robots-ignore jobs stay refused (503) until both are set. The deploy compose passes the SCRAPE_CAPTURE pair. Defaults equal the code defaults, and the X-10 scope flip is not part of this. Caveat: compose environment values override the same key from env_file, and the Ingestion quarry-edge and quarry-control services read ./Quarry-v2/.env, so an operator must set the QUARRY_EDGE_* or QUARRY_CONTROL_* name in the compose project env. Quarry-v2/docs/RUNBOOKS.md (about line 1825) and Quarry-v2/docs/SCRAPE_CAPTURE.md (line 81) still show the double-underscore names. Follow-up outside this unit: apps/Ingestion Plane/.env.example does not list the new interpolation names.
- **QF-23-quarry-stale-notes**: QF-23 (stale notes) is comment and doc only, code-only, not compiled or run. OTP_HANDOFF section 2 now records the code state at 8184a0eb0 (2026-11-11): B1 to B5 are closed in code (the broker half by Q3-E3.1-b1-c, b2-c and b3-c; the approval event fields by QF-15-otp-approval-event-fields); B6 and B7 stay open; no live run is recorded. QF-16 is in the tree behind EXECUTION_CORE_BROWSER_FILL_OTP (default off). - **Q2-WS7-compose**: Q2-WS7-compose: the ingestion compose now passes 14 of the W2 switches (web policy, edge limits, grant budget claim, human receipt mode, attach idle TTL, and the job plan ceilings) from host variables, each with its code default as fallback. QUARRY_EDGE__WEB_POLICY_FINAL_URL_CHECK stays unset on purpose because its default depends on ENVIRONMENT (on for dev-like names, off otherwise); a single literal would flip one environment. RUNBOOKS.md gained a section with default, effect and rollback for the seven switches that lacked rows. No flag was flipped. Nothing was compiled or run.

The note now names ManifestImportsBearer::for_office_extract and reads pipeline::office_via_imports_mode (QF-3 landed in QB11). - **QF-32-attach-lane-trace-events**: QF-32: the quarry-edge attach lane now emits the run-trace events that S4d (QB13) left out. delegate_step in agent_routes/attach.rs calls step.rs's emit_step_trace (now pub(super)) on its success, failure and attach-released-during-step paths, with the same payload fields and the same emit_for_zdr rule as step and execute_ws_action. The agent's tab-open on attach goes through record_timeline_and_trace, so it emits BrowserTabChanged. No switch, no BrowserSessionOpened/Closed emits, no URLs or text in payloads (url_origin is scheme+host only). Not compiled yet.
- **QF-21-quarry-openapi-contracts**: QF-21 (openapi, code only): docs/openapi.yaml now documents what the edge serves: the job-read wait and resume (E3.7.3, behind QUARRY_EDGE__JOB_CONTROL, default off), the processing_continues 408 with Retry-After 1 (E3.7.11), NormalizedOutput url.fetched_url (QUARRY_RUNTIME__URL_REWRITE, default off), and Source timestamps as RFC 3339 or Unix milliseconds. Still stale outside this file: docs/CONTRACTS.md says the E3.7.3 wait is not built (the table row near line 1490 and the section "The 408 envelope (E3.7.3)" near line 1516) and should change in the same docs pass, as depth row D-E3711-1 asks. Regenerate the SDKs before any client work. Noticed but not in this spec: the openapi NormalizedOutput has no text_fp field, though pipeline.rs emits it (E3.5-4). The E3.7.3 status note records that the gateway does not forward wait or resume (D-E373-1); I did not re-check that.
- **QF-25-orgcore-keys-boot-wiring**: QF-25 built: with ORG_CORE_KEYS_ENABLED=1, org-core cmd/server checks the org key master-key config at startup and refuses to boot on failure (fixed message, no key material or env values). It builds orgkeys.NewServiceFromEnv(db), sets the NATS audit publisher when NATS is available, wires server.SetOrgKeys and runs runOrgKeysAuditOutbox (FlushAudit limit 100, 5s ticker, fixed-text logs, failures logged once until recovery). With the switch off, startup behaviour is unchanged. Not compiled. gofmt -l flags main.go only for an import-order problem that was already there (the browsersites import sorted after metrics), not for any of the new code.
- **QF-4-edge-path-id-hardening**: QF-4 built in the quarry-w45 checkout (not compiled, not run). Schedule pause, unpause, trigger, backfill and delete, the preflight pause_for_posture, and source delete now parse the typed id before building the control path: parse_schedule_id (QB10) and parse_source_id, with check_schedule_id as the single bare-error parse. Malformed ids answer 400 invalid schedule id / invalid source id; valid-id behaviour is unchanged. Still open for the test pass: the spec's inline tests, and an optional openapi.yaml 400 entry for these operations.
